&={}4\iint|\nabla\sqrt n|^2\psi
-\iint n\log n\,\Delta\psi
+\iint_\Gamma n\log n\,\partial_\nu\psi\\
&={}4\iint|\nabla\sqrt n|^2\psi
-\iint n\log n\,\Delta\psi.
\end{align*}
The first boundary integral is zero because \(\partial_\nu n=0\), and the second is zero because \(\partial_\nu\psi=0\). Finally,
\begin{align*}
&\iint\nabla\cdot(a\nabla c)(1+\log n)\psi\\
&={}\iint_\Gamma a\partial_\nu c(1+\log n)\psi
-\iint\frac{a}{n}\nabla n\cdot\nabla c\,\psi
-\iint a(1+\log n)\nabla c\cdot\nabla\psi\\
&={}-\iint\nabla h\cdot\nabla c\,\psi
-\iint a(1+\log n)\nabla c\cdot\nabla\psi,
\end{align*}
because \(\partial_\nu c=0\) on \(\Gamma\).
Here we used \(a=nh_\tau'(n)\), hence \(a\nabla n/n=\nabla h\). Adding the four contributions gives
\begin{align}
&\int_{\mathbb H^3}n\log n\,\psi(t)\,dx+4\iint|\nabla\sqrt n|^2\psi\notag\\
&\quad=\iint n\log n(\partial_s\psi+\Delta\psi)+\iint n\log n\,(\mathcal J_\varepsilon u)\cdot\nabla\psi
+\iint\nabla h\cdot\nabla c\,\psi\notag\\&\quad
+\iint a(1+\log n)\nabla c\cdot\nabla\psi.
\label{eq:approx-cell-local}
\end{align}

\emph{The concentration equation.} Because \(\tilde c=1+c\),
\[
\partial_t\tilde c+u\cdot\nabla\tilde c-\Delta\tilde c=-h\tilde c+h.
\]
Using \(\Delta\tilde c=2|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2+2\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\Delta \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\), we obtain
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:approx-s-equation}
\partial_t\sqrt{\tilde{c}}+u\cdot\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}-(\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2-\Delta \sqrt{\tilde{c}}=-\frac12h\sqrt{\tilde{c}}+\frac12h(\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}.
\end{equation}
Multiply \eqref{eq:approx-s-equation} by \(-\partial_i(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi)\), sum over \(i\), and integrate.

For the time derivative,
\begin{align*}
&-\iint\partial_s\sqrt{\tilde c}\,\nabla\cdot(\psi\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c})\\
&=-\iint_\Gamma\partial_s\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_\nu \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi
+\frac12\int_{\mathbb H^3}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi(t)\,dx
-\frac12\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\partial_s\psi\\
&=\frac12\int_{\mathbb H^3}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi(t)\,dx
-\frac12\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\partial_s\psi,
\end{align*}
because \(\partial_\nu \sqrt{\tilde{c}}=0\). For the transport term, two spatial integrations give
\begin{align*}
-\iint(u\cdot\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c})\nabla\cdot(\psi\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c})
={}&-\iint_\Gamma(u\cdot\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}})\partial_\nu \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi
+\iint\nabla u:(\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\otimes\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}})\psi\\
&-\frac12\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2u\cdot\nabla\psi
+\frac12\iint_\Gamma|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi\,u\cdot\nu\\
={}&\iint\nabla u:(\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\otimes\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}})\psi
-\frac12\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2u\cdot\nabla\psi.
\end{align*}
Both boundary terms vanish because \(u=0\) on \(\Gamma\); the first also contains \(\partial_\nu \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\).

The nonlinear diffusion term is expanded algebraically and creates no boundary integral:
\[
\iint(\sqrt{\tilde c})^{-1}|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}|^2\nabla\cdot(\psi\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c})=\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\Delta \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi
+\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\cdot\nabla\psi.
\]
For the Laplacian term,
\begin{align*}
&\iint\Delta\sqrt{\tilde c}\,\nabla\cdot(\psi\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c})\\
&={}\iint_\Gamma\Delta \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_\nu \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi
-\sum_{i,j}\iint\partial_{ijj}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi\\
&={}\iint_\Gamma\Delta \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_\nu \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi
-\sum_{i,j}\iint_\Gamma\partial_{ij}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\nu_j\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi
+\sum_{i,j}\iint\partial_{ij}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_j(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi)\\
&={}\iint|\nabla^2\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi-\frac12\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\Delta\psi.
\end{align*}
The first boundary term is zero because \(\partial_\nu \sqrt{\tilde{c}}=0\). In the second, \(\nu_j=0\) unless \(j=3\); for \(i=1,2\), \(\partial_{i3}\sqrt{\tilde c}=0\), while for \(i=3\), the factor \(\partial_3\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\) is zero. The final integration of \(\partial_{ij}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_j\psi\) produces the boundary term \(\frac12\iint_\Gamma|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\partial_\nu\psi\), which is zero because \(\partial_\nu\psi=0\).

The two reaction terms satisfy
\begin{align*}
-\frac12\iint h\sqrt{\tilde c}\,\nabla\cdot(\psi\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c})
&=-\frac12\iint_\Gamma h\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_\nu \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi
+\frac12\iint\nabla(h\sqrt{\tilde{c}})\cdot\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi\\
&=\frac12\iint h|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi+\frac14\iint\nabla h\cdot\nabla c\,\psi,
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
\frac12\iint h(\sqrt{\tilde c})^{-1}\nabla\cdot(\psi\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c})
&=\frac12\iint_\Gamma h(\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}\partial_\nu \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi
-\frac12\iint\nabla(h(\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1})\cdot\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi\\
&=-\frac12\iint\nabla(h(\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1})\cdot\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi.
\end{align*}
Both boundary integrals vanish because \(\partial_\nu \sqrt{\tilde{c}}=0\). Adding the six contributions therefore gives
\begin{align}
&\frac12\int|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi(t)+\iint|\nabla^2\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi
\notag\\&=\frac12\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2(\partial_s\psi+\Delta\psi)
+\frac12\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2u\cdot\nabla\psi\notag\\
&-\iint\nabla u:(\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\otimes\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}})\psi
-\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\Delta \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi\notag\\
&-\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\cdot\nabla\psi
-\frac12\iint h|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi\notag\\
&-\frac14\iint\nabla h\cdot\nabla c\,\psi
+\frac12\iint\nabla(h(\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1})\cdot\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi.
\label{eq:approx-c-basic}
\end{align}

We now treat
\[
I:=-\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\Delta \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi
=-\sum_{i,j}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}(\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^2\partial_{ii}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi.
\]
An integration by parts in \(x_i\) yields
\begin{align*}
I={}&-\sum_{i,j}\iint_\Gamma (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}(\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^2\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\nu_i\psi
-\sum_{i,j}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-2}(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^2(\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^2\psi\\
&+\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\cdot\nabla\psi
+2\left(\sum_{i=j}+\sum_{i\neq j}\right)\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}\partial_{ij}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi.
\end{align*}
The displayed boundary term is zero: \(\nu_i=0\) unless \(i=3\), and then it contains \(\partial_3\sqrt{\tilde{c}}=0\).
Using the diagonal part of this identity to eliminate \(\sum_i (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}\partial_{ii}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^2\), we obtain
\begin{align*}
I={}&-\frac13\sum_{i,j}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-2}(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^2(\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^2\psi
+\frac13\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\cdot\nabla\psi\\
&+\frac23\sum_{i\neq j}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}\left(\partial_{ij}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}}-\partial_{ii}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}(\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^2\right)\psi.
\end{align*}
For the first off-diagonal term, Young's inequality gives
\begin{align*}
\frac23\sum_{i\neq j}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}\partial_{ij}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi&
\leq\frac13\sum_{i\neq j}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-2}|\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2|\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi\\&\quad
+\frac13\sum_{i\neq j}\iint|\partial_{ij}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi.
\end{align*}
Integrating the second off-diagonal term by parts once more gives
\begin{align*}
&-\frac23\sum_{i\neq j}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}\partial_{ii}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}(\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^2\psi\\
&=-\frac23\sum_{i\neq j}\iint_\Gamma (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}(\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^2\nu_i\psi-\frac23\sum_{i\neq j}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-2}(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^2(\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^2\psi\\
&\quad+\frac43\sum_{i\neq j}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}\partial_{ij}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi+\frac23\sum_{i\neq j}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_i\psi\\
&\leq\frac23\sum_{i\neq j}\iint|\partial_{ij}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi
+\frac23\sum_{i\neq j}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_i\psi.
\end{align*}

The boundary term in the first equality is zero because \(\nu_i\neq0\) only for \(i=3\), in which case \(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}=\partial_3\sqrt{\tilde{c}}=0\). Combining the last three formulas yields
\begin{align}
I\leq{}&-\frac13\sum_i\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-2}(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^4\psi
+\sum_{i\neq j}\iint|\partial_{ij}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi\notag\\
&+\frac23\sum_{i\neq j}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\partial_j\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_i\psi
+\frac13\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\cdot\nabla\psi.
\label{eq:approx-I-refined}
\end{align}
Substitution into \eqref{eq:approx-c-basic}, together with
\(\frac13|\Delta \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\leq\sum_i|\partial_{ii}\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\), gives
\begin{align}
&\frac12\int|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi(t)
+\frac13\iint|\Delta \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi\notag\\
&\quad+\frac12\iint h|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi
+\frac13\sum_i\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-2}
(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^4\psi\notag\\
&\leq\frac12\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2(\partial_s\psi+\Delta\psi)
+\frac12\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2u\cdot\nabla\psi\notag\\
&\quad-\iint\nabla u:
(\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\otimes\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}})\psi
+\frac12\iint\nabla(h(\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1})
\cdot\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi\notag\\
&\quad-\frac14\iint\nabla h\cdot\nabla c\,\psi
-\frac23\sum_{i=1}^3\iint
(\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2
\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\partial_i\psi.
\label{eq:approx-c-refined}
\end{align}
We also need the rough estimate
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:approx-I-rough}
I\leq\frac32\iint|\Delta \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi
+\frac12\sum_{i=1}^3\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-2}(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^4\psi,
\end{equation}
which follows directly from Young's inequality and
\(|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}|^4\leq3\sum_i(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde c})^4\).
The off-diagonal quartic term in the first Young estimate cancels
the corresponding negative term; hence only the diagonal sum remains
in \eqref{eq:approx-I-refined}--\eqref{eq:approx-c-refined}.
Adding \eqref{eq:approx-c-basic} to six times \eqref{eq:approx-c-refined}
and using \eqref{eq:approx-I-rough} leaves, in addition to the full Hessian,
\[
\frac12\iint|\Delta\sqrt{\tilde c}|^2\psi
+\frac32\sum_i\iint(\sqrt{\tilde c})^{-2}(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde c})^4\psi
\geq\frac12\iint(\sqrt{\tilde c})^{-2}|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}|^4\psi.
\]
Thus the following combined inequality, with its original coefficients,
is still valid:

\begin{align}
&\frac72\int|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi(t)+\iint|\nabla^2\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi+\frac72\iint h|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi
+\frac12\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-2}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^4\psi\notag\\
&\leq\frac72\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2(\partial_s\psi+\Delta\psi)
+\frac72\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2u\cdot\nabla\psi-7\iint\nabla u:(\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\otimes\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}})\psi\notag\\
&\quad-\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\cdot\nabla\psi
-\frac74\iint\nabla h\cdot\nabla c\,\psi+\frac72\iint\nabla(h(\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1})\cdot\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi\notag\\
&\quad-4\sum_{i=1}^3\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^3\partial_i\psi.
\label{eq:approx-c-combined}
\end{align}

The two coupling terms are estimated exactly at this point. Since \((\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^2=1+c\leq M_c\),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:approx-fluid-coupling}
\left|\iint\nabla u:(\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\otimes\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}})\psi\right|
\leq\frac1{112}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-2}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^4\psi
+28M_c\iint|\nabla u|^2\psi.
\end{equation}
Moreover, using \(n(h_\tau'(n))^2\leq h_\tau(n)=h\),
\begin{align}
&\frac72\iint\nabla(h(\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1})\cdot\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi\notag\\
&=-\frac72\iint h(\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-2}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi
+\frac72\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}h_\tau'(n)\nabla n\cdot\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\,\psi\notag\\
&\leq\frac72\iint|\nabla\sqrt n|^2\psi.
\label{eq:approx-reaction-coupling}
\end{align}
Indeed, \(\nabla n=2\sqrt n\nabla\sqrt n\) and \(\sqrt n\,h_\tau'(n)\leq\sqrt h\), so the second integrand is bounded by twice the product of \(\sqrt h\,(\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|\) and \(|\nabla\sqrt n|\). Substituting \eqref{eq:approx-fluid-coupling}--\eqref{eq:approx-reaction-coupling} into \eqref{eq:approx-c-combined} yields
\begin{align}
&\frac72\int|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi(t)+\iint|\nabla^2\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi+\frac72\iint h|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi
+\frac7{16}\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-2}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^4\psi\notag\\
&\leq\frac72\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2(\partial_s\psi+\Delta\psi)
+\frac72\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2u\cdot\nabla\psi-\frac74\iint\nabla h\cdot\nabla c\,\psi\notag\\
&\quad
-\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\cdot\nabla\psi\notag+196M_c\iint|\nabla u|^2\psi
+\frac72\iint|\nabla\sqrt n|^2\psi\notag\\
&\quad-4\sum_{i=1}^3\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^3\partial_i\psi.
\label{eq:approx-c-final}
\end{align}

Multiply \eqref{eq:approx-c-final} by \(4/7\) and add \eqref{eq:approx-cell-local}. The mixed terms \(\iint\nabla h\cdot\nabla c\,\psi\) cancel exactly. Moving the resulting \(2\iint|\nabla\sqrt n|^2\psi\) from the right to the left gives
\begin{align}
&\int n\log n\,\psi(t)+2\int|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi(t)
+2\iint|\nabla\sqrt n|^2\psi\notag\\
&\quad+\frac47\iint|\nabla^2\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi
+2\iint h|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\psi
+\frac14\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-2}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^4\psi\notag\\
&\leq\iint n\log n(\partial_s\psi+\Delta\psi)
+\iint n\log n\,(\mathcal J_\varepsilon u)\cdot\nabla\psi+\iint a(1+\log n)\nabla c\cdot\nabla\psi\notag\\
&\quad+2\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2(\partial_s\psi+\Delta\psi)
+2\iint|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2u\cdot\nabla\psi-\frac47\iint (\sqrt{\tilde c})^{-1}|\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}\cdot\nabla\psi\notag\\
&\quad-\frac{16}{7}\sum_{i=1}^3\iint (\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^{-1}(\partial_i\sqrt{\tilde{c}})^3\partial_i\psi+112M_c\iint|\nabla u|^2\psi.
\label{eq:approx-scalar-local-energy}
\end{align}
This is the regularized analogue of the scalar part of the local energy inequality. The only changes are \((\mathcal J_\varepsilon u)\) in the cell transport, \(a\) in the chemotactic flux, and \(h\) in the concentration reaction; the identity \(a=nh'\) is what preserves the cancellation.

\emph{The momentum equation.} Multiply
\[
\partial_tu+(\mathcal J_\varepsilon u)\cdot\nabla u-\Delta u+\nabla p=-\mathcal K_\varepsilon(n\nabla\phi)
\]
by \(2u\psi\). The convective term satisfies
\[
2\iint((\mathcal J_\varepsilon u)\cdot\nabla u)\cdot u\psi
=\iint_\Gamma |u|^2\psi\,(\mathcal J_\varepsilon u)\cdot\nu
-\iint|u|^2(\mathcal J_\varepsilon u)\cdot\nabla\psi
=-\iint|u|^2(\mathcal J_\varepsilon u)\cdot\nabla\psi,
\]
because \((\mathcal J_\varepsilon u)=0\) on \(\Gamma\). The diffusion term is
\begin{align*}
-2\iint\Delta u\cdot u\psi
={}&2\iint|\nabla u|^2\psi-\iint|u|^2\Delta\psi\\
&-2\iint_\Gamma\partial_\nu u\cdot u\psi
+\iint_\Gamma|u|^2\partial_\nu\psi.
\end{align*}
Both boundary integrals vanish because \(u=0\) on \(\Gamma\). For the pressure, after subtracting an arbitrary spatial constant \(\overline p(s)\),
\begin{align*}
2\iint\nabla(p-\overline p(s))\cdot u\psi
={}&-2\iint(p-\overline p(s))u\cdot\nabla\psi\\
&+2\iint_\Gamma(p-\overline p(s))\psi\,u\cdot\nu
=-2\iint(p-\overline p(s))u\cdot\nabla\psi,
\end{align*}
because \(\nabla\cdot u=0\) and \(u=0\) on \(\Gamma\). The time integration has no initial term because \(\psi(\cdot,0)=0\). Consequently,
\begin{align}
\int|u|^2\psi(t)+2\iint|\nabla u|^2\psi
={}&\iint|u|^2(\partial_s\psi+\Delta\psi)
+\iint|u|^2(\mathcal J_\varepsilon u)\cdot\nabla\psi\notag\\
&+2\iint(p-\overline p(s))u\cdot\nabla\psi
-2\iint \mathcal K_\varepsilon(n\nabla\phi)\cdot u\,\psi.
\label{eq:approx-momentum-local}
\end{align}
Multiply \eqref{eq:approx-momentum-local} by \(112M_c\) and add it to \eqref{eq:approx-scalar-local-energy}. The inequality has $ 112M_c\iint|\nabla u|^2\psi$ on its right, whereas the multiplied momentum identity contributes \(224M_c\iint|\nabla u|^2\psi\) on its left. After moving the former term to the left, the remaining coefficient is \(112M_c\). The resulting inequality is exactly \eqref{eq:approximate-local-energy}.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
\label{rem:approx-lei-test}
Lemma~\ref{lem:approximate-local-energy} is proved for the larger class of
boundary-compatible test functions that merely vanish at the initial
time. In the compactness argument below, however, we only pass to the
limit for the class of test functions appearing in
Definition~\ref{def:suitable-half-space} , whose temporal supports are compactly
contained in $(0,T)$. This restriction is needed only for the pressure
term, since the uniform pressure estimate of
Proposition~\ref{prop:uniform-pressure} is available locally in time away from
the genuine initial time.
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Proposition~\ref{prop:regularized-global} supplies global smooth approximate solutions, while Proposition~\ref{prop:uniform-energy} gives the explicit uniform bound \(C_{\rm in}(1+T)\).
A diagonal compactness argument on time intervals separated from the
genuine initial time yields a global distributional solution satisfying
\eqref{eq:half-space-CNS}, attaining the initial data and the boundary conditions in the usual
weak sense.

The global estimates also pass to this limit. The approximate mass
bounds and maximum principle give
\eqref{eq:limit-global-scalar-bounds} by Fatou's lemma on the common
time slices chosen above, shrinking this full-measure set if necessary
so that $c_k(t,x)\to c(t,x)$ for almost every $x$ as well.
To pass to \eqref{eq:uniform-energy}, first
restrict the nonnegative integrands to $(0,T)\times B_R^+$.
The convergences in \eqref{eq:compactness-common-time-slices}
give the entropy term by Fatou's lemma and the quadratic energy
terms by strong $L^2$ convergence for almost every time.
Weak lower semicontinuity gives the dissipation terms involving
$\nabla\sqrt{1+n}$, $\nabla^2\sqrt{1+c}$, and $\nabla u$.
Moreover, $h_k\to n$, $c_k\to c$, and
$\nabla\sqrt{1+c_k}\to\nabla\sqrt{1+c}$ almost everywhere, so Fatou's
lemma gives the quartic and reaction-weighted terms. Taking the
essential supremum in time and then letting $R\to\infty$ by monotone
convergence proves \eqref{eq:limit-global-energy}, with the same
dependence $C_{\rm in}(1+T)$. Thus the limit satisfies the global
requirements in Definition~\ref{def:suitable-half-space}(i).
The $L^{5/3}$ bound for $n$ follows from
\eqref{eq:uniform-integrability} by Fatou's lemma. Applying the
half-space Gagliardo--Nirenberg inequality to $u$ and
$\nabla\sqrt{1+c}$ gives their $L^{10/3}$ bounds. Finally,
$\nabla c=2\sqrt{1+c}\,\nabla\sqrt{1+c}$ and $1\leq1+c\leq M_c$
give
\[
|\nabla c|^4
\leq16M_c^3\frac{|\nabla\sqrt{1+c}|^4}{1+c},
\]
which proves the asserted global $L^4$ integrability of $\nabla c$.

Moreover, Proposition~\ref{prop:uniform-pressure} implies that, for
every $0<\sigma<T<\infty$ and every bounded spatial domain $D$,
\[
p_k-[p_k]_D(t)
\rightharpoonup
p-[p]_D(t)
\quad\text{weakly in }
L^{5/3}\bigl((\sigma,T)\times D\bigr),
\]
after passing to a diagonal subsequence. Consequently, for every bounded domain
$D\subset\mathbb H^3$ whose closure is compact in
$\overline{\mathbb H^3}$ and every compact interval
$I\Subset(0,\infty)$,
\[
p\in L^{5/3}(I\times D).
\]
Since all distributional test functions and all test functions in the
local energy inequality are supported away from the genuine initial
time, this local pressure compactness is sufficient for the passage to
the limit carried out above. Therefore the limiting quadruple satisfies
all the requirements of Definition \ref{def:suitable-half-space} on
$\mathbb H^3\times(0,\infty)$.

The refinement of this construction used in the eventual regularity
assertion is proved in Lemma~\ref{lem:ev-selection}, with its
additional scalar estimates in Lemma~\ref{lem:ev-scalar-decay}.

This completes the existence argument.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{interpolation}
  	For any $\rho>0$ and any pair of exponents $(q,r)$ satisfying
  	\begin{align*}
  		\frac2q+\frac3r=\frac32,\quad 2\leq q\leq \infty,\quad 2\leq r\leq 6,
  	\end{align*}
  	we have
  	\begin{align*}
  		\|f\|^2_{L_t^qL_x^r(\mathcal{Q})}\leq C\left(\|f\|^2_{L_t^\infty L_x^2(\mathcal{Q})}+\|\nabla f\|^2_{L_t^2 L_x^2(\mathcal{Q})}\right).
  	\end{align*}
  	In particular, the following scale-invariant estimates hold:
  	\begin{align*}
  		&\rho^{-\frac12}\|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^r(\mathcal{Q}_\rho(z_0))}\leq C(A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho))^\frac12,\\
  		&\rho^{-\frac12}\|\sqrt{n}\|_{L_t^qL_x^r(\mathcal{Q}_\rho(z_0))}\leq C(A_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho))^\frac12,\\
	  		&\rho^{-\frac12}\|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}\|_{L_t^qL_x^r(\mathcal{Q}_\rho(z_0))}\leq C\bigl(A_{\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}}(\rho)+E_{\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}}(\rho)\bigr)^\frac12,
  	\end{align*}
  	where $C>0$ is a universal constant.
  	\begin{proof}
  		The results follow from standard interpolation, the Sobolev embedding theorem, and H\"{o}lder's inequality.
  	\end{proof}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
The results follow from standard interpolation, the Sobolev embedding theorem, and H\"{o}lder's inequality.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{AE} 
Let $(n,c,u,p)$ be a suitable weak solution. Write 
\[
A(r):=A_{u,\sqrt n,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}(r),
\qquad
E(r):=E_{u,\sqrt n,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}(r),
\]
and
\[
X(r):=A(r)+E(r),
\qquad
U(r):=A_u(r)+E_u(r).
\]
For $0<\gamma\leq1/2$, every admissible $0<\rho\leq1$, and 
$0<\alpha<1/6$, we have, with $X=X(\rho)$, 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:energy-decay-lemma} 
\begin{aligned} 
X(\gamma\rho)\leq{}&
C_\alpha\gamma^2 
\bigl(X+X^{1+\alpha}+X^{1-\alpha}\bigr)\\ 
&+C_\alpha\gamma^{-2} 
\bigl(X^{3/2}+X^{3/2+\alpha}+X^{3/2-\alpha} 
+U(\rho)^{1/2}D(\rho)\bigr)\\ 
&+C_\alpha\gamma^2
\bigl(X+\gamma^{-4}X^{3/2}\bigr)^{1-\alpha}. 
\end{aligned} 
\end{equation} 
The constant depends on $M_c,M_\phi,\alpha$ and the fixed pressure 
exponents, and is independent of $\rho$ and $\gamma$.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Normalize the cylinder by translation and parabolic scaling, so that 
$\rho=1$ and the terminal point is the origin. The rescaled density is 
\[
n_\rho(y,s)=\rho^2n(x_0+\rho y,t_0+\rho^2s).
\]
The additional term $2\log\rho\,n_\rho$ in $n_\rho\log n_\rho$ is 
accounted for by adding $2\log\rho$ times the weak mass identity to 
the rescaled entropy inequality. Thus the normalized suitable 
inequality has the same form. The rescaled force contains 
$\nabla\phi_\rho=\rho\nabla\phi$, which is bounded by $M_\phi$ for 
$\rho\leq1$.

We give the boundary proof. Set 
\[
\Gamma_\gamma(x,t)
=[4\pi(\gamma^2-t)]^{-3/2}
\exp\bigl(-|x|^2/[4(\gamma^2-t)]\bigr),
\qquad 
\psi=\Gamma_\gamma\varphi, 
\]
where $0\leq\varphi\leq1$ is an even-in-$x_3$ smooth cutoff supported 
in $B_1\times(-1,1)$ and equal to one on $Q_{1/2}$. 
It is chosen to vanish near $t=-1$. Then $\partial_3\psi=0$ on 
the flat boundary, and 
\[
\begin{gathered} 
\psi\geq c\gamma^{-3}\quad\hbox{on }Q_\gamma^+, 
\qquad 
\|\psi(t)\|_{L^1(B^+)}\leq1,\\ 
|\psi|\leq C\gamma^{-3},
\qquad 
|\nabla\psi|\leq C\gamma^{-4},
\qquad 
|\psi_t+\Delta\psi|\leq C
\quad\text{in }(-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3.
\end{gathered} 
\]
The last bound follows because the derivatives of the cutoff are 
supported away from the spatial and temporal pole of the kernel.

Put $X=X(1)$ and $U=U(1)$. For almost every terminal time 
$t\in(-\gamma^2,0)$, all weak identities and inequalities below are 
first integrated on $(-1,t)$. By interpolation, 
\[
\|n\|_{L^{5/3}(Q^+)}\leq CX,
\qquad 
\|u\|_{L^{10/3}(Q^+)}
+\|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}\|_{L^{10/3}(Q^+)}
\leq CX^{1/2}.
\]
Since
\[
|n\log n|
\leq C_\alpha\bigl(n^{1+\alpha}+n^{1-\alpha}\bigr),
\]
H\"older's inequality gives 
\[
\begin{aligned} 
\int_{Q^+}|n\log n|\,dxdt
&\leq C_\alpha
\bigl(X^{1+\alpha}+X^{1-\alpha}\bigr),\\ 
\int_{Q^+}|n\log n|(|u|+|\nabla c|)\,dxdt 
&\leq C_\alpha
\bigl(X^{3/2+\alpha}+X^{3/2-\alpha}\bigr).
\end{aligned} 
\]
Here $\alpha<1/6$ ensures that the H\"older exponents are admissible. 
All other scalar and velocity cubic terms are bounded by $CX^{3/2}$. 
For the pressure term, 
\[
\int |p-[p]_1(t)||u|\,dxdt 
\leq
D(1)
\|u\|_{L_t^{\tilde q'}L_x^{\tilde r'}(Q^+)}
\leq
CD(1)U^{1/2}.
\]
The last embedding follows from the energy interpolation line 
$2/q+3/r=3/2$ and the finite measure of $Q_1^+$. 
Thus the absolute sum of the right-hand terms in the tested local 
energy inequality is at most 
\begin{equation}\label{jbn} 
\begin{aligned} 
\mathcal R_\gamma:={}&
C_\alpha\bigl(X+X^{1+\alpha}+X^{1-\alpha}\bigr)\\ 
&+C_\alpha\gamma^{-4} 
\bigl(
X^{3/2}+X^{3/2+\alpha}+X^{3/2-\alpha}
+U^{1/2}D(1)
\bigr). 
\end{aligned} 
\end{equation} 

The negative part of the entropy must be estimated with the same 
weight. The weak mass identity, with its upper limit still $t$, is 
\[
M_\psi(t)
:=\int_{B^+}n(t)\psi(t)\,dx
=
\int_{-1}^t\int_{B^+}
\bigl[
n(\psi_s+\Delta\psi)
+n(u+\nabla c)\cdot\nabla\psi
\bigr]\,dxds.
\]
Consequently, 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weighted-mass-repair} 
0\leq M_\psi(t)
\leq C\bigl(X+\gamma^{-4}X^{3/2}\bigr). 
\end{equation} 
H\"older's inequality with respect to the measure $\psi(t,x)\,dx$ gives 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weighted-negative-entropy} 
\begin{aligned} 
\int_{B^+\cap\{0<n<1\}}
(-n\log n)\psi\,dx 
&\leq
C_\alpha
\int_{B^+\cap\{0<n<1\}}
n^{1-\alpha}\psi\,dx\\
&\leq
C_\alpha M_\psi(t)^{1-\alpha}
\left(\int_{B^+}\psi\,dx\right)^\alpha\\
&\leq
C_\alpha
\bigl(X+\gamma^{-4}X^{3/2}\bigr)^{1-\alpha}.
\end{aligned} 
\end{equation} 
In particular, the weight cannot be discarded in this step.

Add the bound for the negative entropy to the tested energy inequality, 
and use \eqref{eq:weighted-mass-repair} for the density contribution 
to $A$. First take the essential supremum over $t\in(-\gamma^2,0)$ 
for the terminal energies; then let admissible terminal times increase 
to zero for the nonnegative dissipation integrals. Multiplication 
by $\gamma^2$ gives 
\begin{equation}\label{11} 
\begin{aligned} 
&X(\gamma)
+\gamma^{-1}
\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{-\gamma^2<t<0}
\int_{B_\gamma^+}n|\log n|(t)\,dx\\
&\qquad
+\gamma^{-1}
\iint_{Q_\gamma^+}
\frac{|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}|^4}{\tilde c}\,dxdt\\
&\qquad\leq
C\gamma^2\mathcal R_\gamma
+C_\alpha\gamma^2
\bigl(X+\gamma^{-4}X^{3/2}\bigr)^{1-\alpha}. 
\end{aligned} 
\end{equation} 
Discard the last two nonnegative terms and rescale to obtain 
\eqref{eq:energy-decay-lemma}. The interior argument uses a compactly 
supported cutoff in $Q_1$, with no symmetry restriction, and is identical. 
The cubic terms have critical scaling and acquire no extra factor $\rho$; 
only the gravitational force has the additional factor described above.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{corollary1} 
Assume that $A(\rho)+E(\rho)$ is sufficiently small. Then
\begin{align*} 
A(\gamma\rho)+E(\gamma\rho)
\leq{}&
C_1\gamma^2
\bigl(A(\rho)+E(\rho)\bigr)^{1-\delta}\\
&+C_1\gamma^{-2}
\bigl(A(\rho)+E(\rho)\bigr)^{\frac32-\delta}\\
&+C_1\gamma^{-2}
\bigl(A(\rho)+E(\rho)\bigr)^{1/2}D(\rho),
\end{align*} 
where $C_1=C(M_c,M_\phi,\delta)$,
$0<\delta<\frac14$, and $0<\gamma\leq\frac12$, with the pressure 
exponents fixed.

 
\begin{proof} 
It suffices to assume
\[
A(\rho)+E(\rho)\leq1.
\]
Apply Lemma~\ref{AE} with
\[
\alpha=\frac{2\delta}{3},
\qquad 0<\delta<\frac14.
\]
Since $\alpha<1/6$, this choice is admissible. Writing
\[
X=X(\rho)=A(\rho)+E(\rho),
\]
concavity gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\gamma^2
\bigl(X+\gamma^{-4}X^{3/2}\bigr)^{1-\alpha}
&\leq
\gamma^2X^{1-\alpha}
+\gamma^{-2+4\alpha}
X^{3/2-3\alpha/2}\\
&\leq
\gamma^2X^{1-\delta}
+\gamma^{-2}X^{3/2-\delta}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Indeed, $\alpha=2\delta/3<\delta$, while
\[
\frac32-\frac{3\alpha}{2}
=\frac32-\delta,
\]
and $0<\gamma\leq1/2$ implies
\[
\gamma^{-2+4\alpha}\leq\gamma^{-2}.
\]

Since $X\leq1$ and $\alpha\leq\delta$, we also have
\[
X+X^{1+\alpha}+X^{1-\alpha}
\leq C X^{1-\delta},
\]
and
\[
X^{3/2}+X^{3/2+\alpha}+X^{3/2-\alpha}
\leq C X^{3/2-\delta}.
\]
Finally,
\[
U(\rho)\leq X(\rho)=A(\rho)+E(\rho),
\]
and hence
\[
U(\rho)^{1/2}D(\rho)
\leq
\bigl(A(\rho)+E(\rho)\bigr)^{1/2}D(\rho).
\]
Substituting these estimates into
\eqref{eq:energy-decay-lemma} gives the asserted inequality.
\end{proof}
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
It suffices to assume

\]
Apply Lemma~\ref{AE} with
\[
\alpha=\frac{2\delta}{3},
\qquad 0<\delta<\frac14.
\]
Since $\alpha<1/6$, this choice is admissible. Writing
\[
X=X(\rho)=A(\rho)+E(\rho),
\]
concavity gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\gamma^2
\bigl(X+\gamma^{-4}X^{3/2}\bigr)^{1-\alpha}
&\leq
\gamma^2X^{1-\alpha}
+\gamma^{-2+4\alpha}
X^{3/2-3\alpha/2}\\
&\leq
\gamma^2X^{1-\delta}
+\gamma^{-2}X^{3/2-\delta}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Indeed, $\alpha=2\delta/3<\delta$, while
\[
\frac32-\frac{3\alpha}{2}
=\frac32-\delta,
\]
and $0<\gamma\leq1/2$ implies
\[
\gamma^{-2+4\alpha}\leq\gamma^{-2}.
\]

Since $X\leq1$ and $\alpha\leq\delta$, we also have
\[
X+X^{1+\alpha}+X^{1-\alpha}
\leq C X^{1-\delta},
\]
and
\[
X^{3/2}+X^{3/2+\alpha}+X^{3/2-\alpha}
\leq C X^{3/2-\delta}.
\]

U(\rho)\leq X(\rho)=A(\rho)+E(\rho),

<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{p}
  	Let $(n,c,u,p)$ be a suitable weak solution of \eqref{che}--\eqref{bound}. For any $\gamma\in(0,\frac12]$, $\rho>0$, and $\epsilon_1\in(0,\frac{3}{\tilde{r}}-1)$ is sufficiently small, there exist a constant $C=C(\tilde{q},\tilde{r},\epsilon_1)$ such that
  	\begin{align}
  		D(\gamma\rho)\leq& C\gamma^{1+\epsilon_1}\Big[D_{\tilde{q},1}(\rho)+A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)+\rho\left(A_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)\right)\nonumber\\
  		& \qquad\qquad+\left(A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)\right)^\frac12\Big]\nonumber\\
  		&+C\gamma^{-1}\left[A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)+\rho M_\phi\left(A_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)\right)\right], \label{D_1}
  	\end{align}
  	and
  	\begin{align}
  		D(\gamma\rho)\leq& C\gamma^{1+\epsilon_1}\Big[D(\rho)+A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)+\rho\left(A_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)\right)+\left(A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)\right)^\frac12\Big]\nonumber\\
  		&+C\gamma^{-1}\left[A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)+\rho M_\phi\left(A_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)\right)\right]. \label{D_2}
  	\end{align}
  	\begin{proof}
  		We prove the boundary case first. Without loss of generality, by scaling we assume $\rho=1$. Fix once and for all a smooth domain
$\tilde B$ satisfying $B_{3/4}^+\subset\tilde B\subset B_1^+$,
and set $\tilde Q=(-1,0)\times\tilde B$. This leaves a fixed
geometric gap for the homogeneous Stokes estimate from
$Q_{3/4}^+$ to $Q_{1/2}^+$. Define $r^*$ via $\frac{1}{r^*}=\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}+\frac13<1$. H\"{o}lder's inequality implies
  		\begin{align*}
  			\|u\cdot\nabla u\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(Q^+)}\leq \|\nabla u\|_{L_t^2L_x^2(Q^+)}\|u\|_{L_t^{q_1}L_x^{r_1}(Q^+)},
  		\end{align*}
  		where $\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}=\frac12+\frac{1}{q_1}$ and $\frac{1}{r^*}=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{r_1}$.
  		Under the conditions on $(\tilde{q},\tilde{r})$, we have
  		\begin{align*}
  			&\frac{2}{\tilde{q}}+\frac{3}{r^*}=\frac{3}{\tilde{r}}+1+\frac{2}{\tilde{q}}=4,\\
  			&\frac{2}{\tilde{q}}+\frac{3}{r^*}=\frac{2}{q_1}+1+\frac{3}{r_1}+\frac32=4.
  		\end{align*}
  		Since $q_1\in(2,\infty)$, $r_1\in(2,6)$, and $\frac{2}{q_1}+\frac{3}{r_1}=\frac32$, Lemma \ref{interpolation} yields
  		\begin{align*}
  			\|u\cdot\nabla u\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(Q^+)}\leq C(A_u(1)+E_u(1)).
  		\end{align*}
  		Similarly,
  		\begin{align*}
  			\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(Q^+)}\leq C\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{r}}(Q^+)}= C\|\sqrt{n}\|^2_{L_t^{2\tilde{q}}L_x^{2\tilde{r}}(Q^+)}.
  		\end{align*}
  		Since $2\tilde{q}\in (2,4)$ and $2\tilde{r}\in (3,6)$, the scaling relation $\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}}+\frac{3}{2\tilde{r}}=\frac32$ holds. Thus,
  		\begin{align*}
  			\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(Q^+)}\leq C(A_{\sqrt{n}}(1)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(1)).
  		\end{align*}
  		By Lemma \ref{stokes}, there exists a unique pair
  		\begin{align*}
  			v\in W^{1,2}_{\tilde{q},r^*}(\tilde{Q})\quad \text{and}\quad p_1\in W^{0,1}_{\tilde{q},r^*}(\tilde{Q})
  		\end{align*}
  		solving the initial boundary value problem:
  		\begin{align*}
  			\begin{cases}
  				\partial_tv-\Delta v+\nabla p_1=-u\cdot\nabla u-n\nabla\phi \quad&\text{in}~\tilde{Q},\\
  				\nabla\cdot v=0   &\text{in}~\tilde{Q},\\
  				[p_1]_{\tilde{B}}(t)=0 &\text{for almost everywhere (a.e.)}~t\in[-1,0],\\
  				v=0 &\text{on}~ \partial_p\tilde{Q}.
  			\end{cases}
  		\end{align*}
  		Moreover, the solution satisfies
  		\begin{align}
  			&\||v|+|\nabla v|+|p_1|+|\nabla p_1|\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(\tilde{Q})}\nonumber\\
  			\leq& C\left(\|u\cdot\nabla u\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(Q^+)}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty_{t,x}(Q^+)}\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(Q^+)}\right)\nonumber\\
  			\leq& C(A_u(1)+E_u(1))+C\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty_{t,x}(Q^+)}(A_{\sqrt{n}}(1)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(1)). \label{E1}
  		\end{align}
  		The Sobolev-Poincar\'{e} inequality then yields
  		\begin{align}
  			&\|p_1\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{r}}(\tilde{Q})}\leq C\|\nabla p_1\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(\tilde{Q})}\nonumber\\ 
  			\leq&C(A_u(1)+E_u(1))+C\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty_{t,x}(Q^+)}(A_{\sqrt{n}}(1)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(1)). \label{p_1}
  		\end{align}
  		Setting $w=u-v$ and $p_2=p-p_1$, the system for $(w, p_2)$ is
  		\begin{align*}
  			\begin{cases}
  				\partial_tw-\Delta w+\nabla p_2=0\quad &\text{in}~\tilde{Q},\\
  				\nabla\cdot w=0 &\text{in}~\tilde{Q},\\
  				w=0 &\text{on}~[-1,0)\times(\partial\tilde{B}\cap \{x_3=0\}).
  			\end{cases}
  		\end{align*}
  		Since $\tilde{q}\in (1,2)$, using \eqref{E1}, a simple calculation verifies that 
  		\[
  		w \in W^{0,1}_{\tilde{q}, 1}(\tilde{Q}) \quad \text{and}\; p_2 \in L^{\tilde{q}}_t L^1_x(\tilde{Q}).
  		\]
  		By H\"{o}lder's inequality, Sobolev-Poincar\'{e} inequality, and Lemma \ref{Seregin}, we obtain
  		\begin{align}
  			&\frac{1}{\gamma}\|p_2-[p_2]_\gamma\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{r}}(Q^+(\gamma))}\leq C\|\nabla p_2\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{r}}(Q^+(\gamma))}\nonumber\\
  			\leq & C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\|\nabla p_2\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r'}(Q^+(\gamma))}\leq C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\|\nabla p_2\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r'}(Q^+(\frac12))}\nonumber\\
  			\leq &C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\left(\|p_2-[p_2]_{\tilde{B}}\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^1(\tilde{Q})}+\|w\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^1(\tilde{Q})}\right)\nonumber\\
  			\leq & C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\left(D_{\tilde{q},1}(1)+\|p_1\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^1(\tilde{Q})}+\|v\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^1(\tilde{Q})}+\|u\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^1(Q^+)}\right)\nonumber\\
  			\leq &C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\Big[D_{\tilde{q},1}(1)+A_u(1)+E_u(1)+M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt{n}}(1)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(1)\big)\nonumber\\
  			&\qquad\qquad\quad+ A_u(1)^{(\frac{1}{2\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{6\tilde{q}})}E_u(1)^{\frac{1}{2\tilde{q}}}\Big], \label{p_2}
  		\end{align}
  		where $r'>\tilde{r}$ is arbitrary. Combining (\ref{p_1}) and (\ref{p_2}) yields
  		\begin{align*}
  			D(\gamma)\leq& C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\Big[D_{\tilde{q},1}(1)+A_u(1)+E_u(1)+M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt{n}}(1)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(1)\big)\\
  			& \qquad\qquad\quad+\left(A_u(1)+E_u(1)\right)^\frac12\Big]\\
  			&+C\gamma^{-1}\Big[A_u(1)+E_u(1)+M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt{n}}(1)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(1)\big)\Big].
  		\end{align*}
  		Rescaling this inequality gives
  		\begin{align*}
  			D(\gamma\rho)\leq& C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\Big[D_{\tilde{q},1}(\rho)+A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)+\rho M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)\big)\\
  			&\qquad\qquad\quad+\left(A_u(\rho )+E_u(\rho)\right)^\frac12\Big]\\
  			&+C\gamma^{-1}\left[A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)+\rho M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)\big)\right].
  		\end{align*}
  		Since $\tilde{r}<3$ and $r'$ can be chosen arbitrarily large, $\epsilon_1$ may range in $(0, \frac{3}{\tilde{r}} - 1)$. The force terms in the decaying bracket retain the
factor $\rho M_\phi$. To obtain the stated form of \eqref{D_1},
absorb these terms into the last bracket using
$\gamma^{1+\epsilon_1}\rho M_\phi
\leq\gamma^{-1}\rho M_\phi$; the additional nonnegative density
term in the first bracket may then be kept.
Finally, \eqref{D_2} follows from \eqref{D_1} and H\"older's inequality.

        We now consider the interior case. Fix an interior point
$z_0=(x_0,t_0)$ and an admissible radius $\rho$.
By translation and parabolic rescaling, we may assume that
$z_0=(0,0)$ and $\rho=1$, and retain the same notation for
the rescaled functions. We work on the full cylinder
$Q=(-1,0)\times B$, where $B=B_1$, using the notation
introduced in Section~\ref{subsec:notation-scaling}. In particular, all the
scale-invariant quantities below are evaluated on full
balls and cylinders.

For any spatially integrable function $f$, write
\[
[f]_r(t):=\frac{1}{|B_r|}\int_{B_r}f(x,t)\,dx.
\]
Let $\chi\in C_c^\infty(B)$ satisfy
\[
0\le\chi\le1,\qquad \chi\equiv1\quad\text{in }B_{3/4},
\]
and set $\bar u(t):=[u]_1(t)$ for almost every $t\in(-1,0)$.
Taking the divergence of the momentum equation and using
$\nabla\cdot u=0$, we have in the sense of distributions
\[
    -\Delta p
    =
    \partial_i\partial_j(u_i u_j)
    +
    \nabla\cdot(n\nabla\phi).
\]
Since $\bar u(t)$ is independent of $x$ and $\nabla\cdot u=0$,
\[
    \partial_i\partial_j
    \big((u_i-\bar u_i)(u_j-\bar u_j)\big)
    =
    \partial_i\partial_j(u_i u_j).
\]

For almost every fixed $t$, define on $\mathbb R^3$
\[
\begin{aligned}
p_1
={}&
(-\Delta)^{-1}\partial_i\partial_j
\Big[
\chi
(u_i-\bar u_i)(u_j-\bar u_j)
\Big]+
(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\cdot
\big(\chi n\nabla\phi\big),
\end{aligned}
\]
and put
\[
    p_2=p-p_1.
\]
Since $\chi\equiv1$ in $B_{3/4}$, comparison with the pressure
equation above yields
\[
    \Delta p_2=0
    \qquad\text{in }B_{3/4}
\]
for almost every $t\in(-1,0)$.

We first estimate $p_1$. Write
\[
    p_1=p_1^u+p_1^n,
\]
where $p_1^u$ and $p_1^n$ denote respectively the contributions of the
convective term and the external force. By the Calder\'on--Zygmund singular-integral estimate
(see Stein~\cite{Stein}),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|p_1^u\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}
&\leq
C
\|(u-\bar u)\otimes(u-\bar u)\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q)}
=
C
\|u-\bar u\|_
{L_t^{2\tilde q}L_x^{2\tilde r}(Q)}^2.
\end{aligned}
\]
Since
\[
    \frac{2}{2\tilde q}
    +\frac{3}{2\tilde r}
    =\frac32,
\]
Lemma \ref{interpolation}, together with the Poincar\'e inequality, gives
\[
    \|u-\bar u\|_
    {L_t^{2\tilde q}L_x^{2\tilde r}(Q)}^2
    \leq
    C\big(A_u(1)+E_u(1)\big).
\]
Hence
\[
    \|p_1^u\|_
    {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
    ((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}
    \leq
    C\big(A_u(1)+E_u(1)\big).
\]

For the contribution generated by $n\nabla\phi$, define
$\tilde r_*$ by
\[
    \frac1{\tilde r_*}
    =
    \frac1{\tilde r}+\frac13.
\]
Since $3/2<\tilde r<3$, one has
\[
    1<\tilde r_*<\frac32.
\]
The Hardy--Littlewood--Sobolev inequality
(see~\cite{Stein}) therefore yields
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|p_1^n\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}
&\leq
C
\|\chi n\nabla\phi\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r_*}(Q)}
\leq
CM_\phi
\|n\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r_*}(Q)}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Since $B$ has finite measure and
$\tilde r_*<\tilde r$,
\[
    \|n\|_
    {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r_*}(Q)}
    \leq
    C
    \|n\|_
    {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q)}
    =
    C\|\sqrt n\|_
    {L_t^{2\tilde q}L_x^{2\tilde r}(Q)}^2
    \leq
    C\big(A_{\sqrt n}(1)+E_{\sqrt n}(1)\big).
\]
Consequently,
\[
    \|p_1^n\|_
    {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
    ((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}
    \leq
    CM_\phi
    \big(A_{\sqrt n}(1)+E_{\sqrt n}(1)\big).
\]
Combining the preceding two estimates, we obtain
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|p_1\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}
\leq
C\Big[
A_u(1)+E_u(1)+
M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt n}(1)+E_{\sqrt n}(1)\big)
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]

It remains to estimate the harmonic part $p_2$. Since
$p_2(\cdot,t)$ is harmonic in $B_{3/4}$, the standard interior
estimate for harmonic functions implies
\[
    \|\nabla p_2(\cdot,t)\|_{L^\infty(B_{1/2})}
    \leq
    C
    \|p_2(\cdot,t)-[p_2]_{3/4}(t)\|_
    {L^1(B_{3/4})}.
\]
Hence, for every $\gamma\in(0,1/2]$,
the Poincar\'e inequality gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\|p_2-[p_2]_{\gamma}(t)\|_
{L^{\tilde r}(B_\gamma)}
\leq C\gamma\|\nabla p_2(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\tilde{r}}(B_{\gamma})}\leq C\gamma^{1+\frac3{\tilde r}}\|\nabla p_2(\cdot,t)\|_{L^\infty(B_{1/2})}\\&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\leq
C\gamma^{1+\frac3{\tilde r}}
\|p_2-[p_2]_{3/4}(t)\|_
{L^1(B_{3/4})}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Taking the $L^{\tilde q}$ norm in time, and using
$(-\gamma^2,0)\subset(-1,0)$, we obtain
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|p_2-[p_2]_{\gamma}(t)\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma)}
\leq
C\gamma^{1+\frac3{\tilde r}}
\|p_2-[p_2]_{3/4}(t)\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1
((-1,0)\times B_{3/4})}.
\end{aligned}
\]

Since $p_2=p-p_1$, the elementary estimate
\[
    \|f-[f]_{B_{3/4}}\|_{L^1(B_{3/4})}
    \leq
    2\|f-c(t)\|_{L^1(B_{3/4})}
\]
valid for every function $c(t)$, applied with
$c(t)=[p]_1(t)$, gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|p_2-[p_2]_{3/4}(t)\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1
((-1,0)\times B_{3/4})}
\leq
C D_{\tilde q,1}(1)
+
C
\|p_1\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Therefore,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\frac1\gamma
\|p_2-[p_2]_{\gamma}(t)\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma)}
\\&\leq{}
C\gamma^{\frac3{\tilde r}}
D_{\tilde q,1}(1)
+
C\gamma^{\frac3{\tilde r}}
\Big[
A_u(1)+E_u(1)
+
M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt n}(1)+E_{\sqrt n}(1)\big)
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]

For the localized part $p_1$, we simply use
\[
\begin{aligned}
\frac1\gamma
\|p_1-[p_1]_{\gamma}(t)\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma)}
&\leq
C\gamma^{-1}
\|p_1\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}
\\
&\leq
C\gamma^{-1}
\Big[
A_u(1)+E_u(1)
+
M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt n}(1)+E_{\sqrt n}(1)\big)
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]
Since $\gamma^{3/\tilde r}\leq\gamma^{-1}$ for
$0<\gamma\leq1/2$, combining the estimates for $p_1$ and $p_2$
gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
D(\gamma)
\leq{}
C\gamma^{\frac3{\tilde r}}
D_{\tilde q,1}(1)
+
C\gamma^{-1}
\Big[
A_u(1)+E_u(1)
+
M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt n}(1)+E_{\sqrt n}(1)\big)
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]

A standard parabolic rescaling now yields
\[
\begin{aligned}
D(\gamma\rho)
\leq{}
C\gamma^{\frac3{\tilde r}}
D_{\tilde q,1}(\rho)
+
C\gamma^{-1}
\Big[
A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)
+
\rho M_\phi
\big(A_{\sqrt n}(\rho)+E_{\sqrt n}(\rho)\big)
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]
The factor $\rho$ in front of $\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty}$ follows from
the rescaling
\[
    \phi_\rho(x)=\phi(\rho x),
    \qquad
    \|\nabla\phi_\rho\|_{L^\infty}
    =
    \rho\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty}.
\]

Finally, H\"older's inequality in space and
$ \frac{2}{\tilde q}+\frac{3}{\tilde r}=3$
   give
\[
    D_{\tilde q,1}(\rho)
    \leq
    C D(\rho).
\]
Thus
\[
\begin{aligned}
D(\gamma\rho)
\leq{}
C\gamma^{\frac3{\tilde r}}D(\rho)
+
C\gamma^{-1}
\Big[
A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)
+
\rho M_\phi
\big(A_{\sqrt n}(\rho)+E_{\sqrt n}(\rho)\big)
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]
Since $\tilde r<3$, one has $3/\tilde r>1$, and hence for
every $ 0<\epsilon_1<\frac3{\tilde r}-1$
and every $\gamma\in(0,1/2]$, $\gamma^{3/\tilde r}
    \leq
    \gamma^{1+\epsilon_1}$.
This proves the asserted interior pressure estimate.
  	\end{proof}
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
We prove the boundary case first. Without loss of generality, by scaling we assume $\rho=1$. Fix once and for all a smooth domain
$\tilde B$ satisfying $B_{3/4}^+\subset\tilde B\subset B_1^+$,
and set $\tilde Q=(-1,0)\times\tilde B$. This leaves a fixed
geometric gap for the homogeneous Stokes estimate from
$Q_{3/4}^+$ to $Q_{1/2}^+$. Define $r^*$ via $\frac{1}{r^*}=\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}+\frac13<1$. H\"{o}lder's inequality implies
  		\begin{align*}
  			\|u\cdot\nabla u\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(Q^+)}\leq \|\nabla u\|_{L_t^2L_x^2(Q^+)}\|u\|_{L_t^{q_1}L_x^{r_1}(Q^+)},
  		\end{align*}
  		where $\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}=\frac12+\frac{1}{q_1}$ and $\frac{1}{r^*}=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{r_1}$.
  		Under the conditions on $(\tilde{q},\tilde{r})$, we have
  		\begin{align*}
  			&\frac{2}{\tilde{q}}+\frac{3}{r^*}=\frac{3}{\tilde{r}}+1+\frac{2}{\tilde{q}}=4,\\
  			&\frac{2}{\tilde{q}}+\frac{3}{r^*}=\frac{2}{q_1}+1+\frac{3}{r_1}+\frac32=4.
  		\end{align*}
  		Since $q_1\in(2,\infty)$, $r_1\in(2,6)$, and $\frac{2}{q_1}+\frac{3}{r_1}=\frac32$, Lemma \ref{interpolation} yields
  		\begin{align*}
  			\|u\cdot\nabla u\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(Q^+)}\leq C(A_u(1)+E_u(1)).
  		\end{align*}
  		Similarly,
  		\begin{align*}
  			\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(Q^+)}\leq C\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{r}}(Q^+)}= C\|\sqrt{n}\|^2_{L_t^{2\tilde{q}}L_x^{2\tilde{r}}(Q^+)}.
  		\end{align*}
  		Since $2\tilde{q}\in (2,4)$ and $2\tilde{r}\in (3,6)$, the scaling relation $\frac{2}{2\tilde{q}}+\frac{3}{2\tilde{r}}=\frac32$ holds. Thus,
  		\begin{align*}
  			\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(Q^+)}\leq C(A_{\sqrt{n}}(1)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(1)).
  		\end{align*}
  		By Lemma \ref{stokes}, there exists a unique pair
  		\begin{align*}
  			v\in W^{1,2}_{\tilde{q},r^*}(\tilde{Q})\quad \text{and}\quad p_1\in W^{0,1}_{\tilde{q},r^*}(\tilde{Q})
  		\end{align*}
  		solving the initial boundary value problem:
  		\begin{align*}
  			\begin{cases}
  				\partial_tv-\Delta v+\nabla p_1=-u\cdot\nabla u-n\nabla\phi \quad&\text{in}~\tilde{Q},\\
  				\nabla\cdot v=0   &\text{in}~\tilde{Q},\\
  				[p_1]_{\tilde{B}}(t)=0 &\text{for almost everywhere (a.e.)}~t\in[-1,0],\\
  				v=0 &\text{on}~ \partial_p\tilde{Q}.
  			\end{cases}
  		\end{align*}
  		Moreover, the solution satisfies
  		\begin{align}
  			&\||v|+|\nabla v|+|p_1|+|\nabla p_1|\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(\tilde{Q})}\nonumber\\
  			\leq& C\left(\|u\cdot\nabla u\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(Q^+)}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty_{t,x}(Q^+)}\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(Q^+)}\right)\nonumber\\
  			\leq& C(A_u(1)+E_u(1))+C\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty_{t,x}(Q^+)}(A_{\sqrt{n}}(1)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(1)). \label{E1}
  		\end{align}
  		The Sobolev-Poincar\'{e} inequality then yields
  		\begin{align}
  			&\|p_1\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{r}}(\tilde{Q})}\leq C\|\nabla p_1\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r^*}(\tilde{Q})}\nonumber\\ 
  			\leq&C(A_u(1)+E_u(1))+C\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty_{t,x}(Q^+)}(A_{\sqrt{n}}(1)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(1)). \label{p_1}
  		\end{align}
  		Setting $w=u-v$ and $p_2=p-p_1$, the system for $(w, p_2)$ is
  		\begin{align*}
  			\begin{cases}
  				\partial_tw-\Delta w+\nabla p_2=0\quad &\text{in}~\tilde{Q},\\
  				\nabla\cdot w=0 &\text{in}~\tilde{Q},\\
  				w=0 &\text{on}~[-1,0)\times(\partial\tilde{B}\cap \{x_3=0\}).
  			\end{cases}
  		\end{align*}
  		Since $\tilde{q}\in (1,2)$, using \eqref{E1}, a simple calculation verifies that 
  		\[
  		w \in W^{0,1}_{\tilde{q}, 1}(\tilde{Q}) \quad \text{and}\; p_2 \in L^{\tilde{q}}_t L^1_x(\tilde{Q}).
  		\]
  		By H\"{o}lder's inequality, Sobolev-Poincar\'{e} inequality, and Lemma \ref{Seregin}, we obtain
  		\begin{align}
  			&\frac{1}{\gamma}\|p_2-[p_2]_\gamma\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{r}}(Q^+(\gamma))}\leq C\|\nabla p_2\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{r}}(Q^+(\gamma))}\nonumber\\
  			\leq & C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\|\nabla p_2\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r'}(Q^+(\gamma))}\leq C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\|\nabla p_2\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{r'}(Q^+(\frac12))}\nonumber\\
  			\leq &C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\left(\|p_2-[p_2]_{\tilde{B}}\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^1(\tilde{Q})}+\|w\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^1(\tilde{Q})}\right)\nonumber\\
  			\leq & C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\left(D_{\tilde{q},1}(1)+\|p_1\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^1(\tilde{Q})}+\|v\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^1(\tilde{Q})}+\|u\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^1(Q^+)}\right)\nonumber\\
  			\leq &C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\Big[D_{\tilde{q},1}(1)+A_u(1)+E_u(1)+M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt{n}}(1)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(1)\big)\nonumber\\
  			&\qquad\qquad\quad+ A_u(1)^{(\frac{1}{2\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{6\tilde{q}})}E_u(1)^{\frac{1}{2\tilde{q}}}\Big], \label{p_2}
  		\end{align}
  		where $r'>\tilde{r}$ is arbitrary. Combining (\ref{p_1}) and (\ref{p_2}) yields
  		\begin{align*}
  			D(\gamma)\leq& C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\Big[D_{\tilde{q},1}(1)+A_u(1)+E_u(1)+M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt{n}}(1)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(1)\big)\\
  			& \qquad\qquad\quad+\left(A_u(1)+E_u(1)\right)^\frac12\Big]\\
  			&+C\gamma^{-1}\Big[A_u(1)+E_u(1)+M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt{n}}(1)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(1)\big)\Big].
  		\end{align*}
  		Rescaling this inequality gives
  		\begin{align*}
  			D(\gamma\rho)\leq& C\gamma^{3(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{1}{r'})}\Big[D_{\tilde{q},1}(\rho)+A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)+\rho M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)\big)\\
  			&\qquad\qquad\quad+\left(A_u(\rho )+E_u(\rho)\right)^\frac12\Big]\\
  			&+C\gamma^{-1}\left[A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)+\rho M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+E_{\sqrt{n}}(\rho)\big)\right].
  		\end{align*}
  		Since $\tilde{r}<3$ and $r'$ can be chosen arbitrarily large, $\epsilon_1$ may range in $(0, \frac{3}{\tilde{r}} - 1)$. The force terms in the decaying bracket retain the
factor $\rho M_\phi$. To obtain the stated form of \eqref{D_1},
absorb these terms into the last bracket using
$\gamma^{1+\epsilon_1}\rho M_\phi
\leq\gamma^{-1}\rho M_\phi$; the additional nonnegative density
term in the first bracket may then be kept.
Finally, \eqref{D_2} follows from \eqref{D_1} and H\"older's inequality.

        We now consider the interior case. Fix an interior point
$z_0=(x_0,t_0)$ and an admissible radius $\rho$.
By translation and parabolic rescaling, we may assume that
$z_0=(0,0)$ and $\rho=1$, and retain the same notation for
the rescaled functions. We work on the full cylinder
$Q=(-1,0)\times B$, where $B=B_1$, using the notation
introduced in Section~\ref{subsec:notation-scaling}. In particular, all the
scale-invariant quantities below are evaluated on full
balls and cylinders.

For any spatially integrable function $f$, write
\[
[f]_r(t):=\frac{1}{|B_r|}\int_{B_r}f(x,t)\,dx.
\]
Let $\chi\in C_c^\infty(B)$ satisfy
\[
0\le\chi\le1,\qquad \chi\equiv1\quad\text{in }B_{3/4},
\]
and set $\bar u(t):=[u]_1(t)$ for almost every $t\in(-1,0)$.
Taking the divergence of the momentum equation and using
$\nabla\cdot u=0$, we have in the sense of distributions
\[
    -\Delta p
    =
    \partial_i\partial_j(u_i u_j)
    +
    \nabla\cdot(n\nabla\phi).
\]
Since $\bar u(t)$ is independent of $x$ and $\nabla\cdot u=0$,
\[
    \partial_i\partial_j
    \big((u_i-\bar u_i)(u_j-\bar u_j)\big)
    =
    \partial_i\partial_j(u_i u_j).
\]

For almost every fixed $t$, define on $\mathbb R^3$
\[
\begin{aligned}
p_1
={}&
(-\Delta)^{-1}\partial_i\partial_j
\Big[
\chi
(u_i-\bar u_i)(u_j-\bar u_j)
\Big]+
(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\cdot
\big(\chi n\nabla\phi\big),
\end{aligned}
\]
and put
\[
    p_2=p-p_1.
\]
Since $\chi\equiv1$ in $B_{3/4}$, comparison with the pressure
equation above yields
\[
    \Delta p_2=0
    \qquad\text{in }B_{3/4}
\]
for almost every $t\in(-1,0)$.

We first estimate $p_1$. Write
\[
    p_1=p_1^u+p_1^n,
\]
where $p_1^u$ and $p_1^n$ denote respectively the contributions of the
convective term and the external force. By the Calder\'on--Zygmund singular-integral estimate
(see Stein~\cite{Stein}),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|p_1^u\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}
&\leq
C
\|(u-\bar u)\otimes(u-\bar u)\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q)}
=
C
\|u-\bar u\|_
{L_t^{2\tilde q}L_x^{2\tilde r}(Q)}^2.
\end{aligned}
\]
Since
\[
    \frac{2}{2\tilde q}
    +\frac{3}{2\tilde r}
    =\frac32,
\]
Lemma \ref{interpolation}, together with the Poincar\'e inequality, gives
\[
    \|u-\bar u\|_
    {L_t^{2\tilde q}L_x^{2\tilde r}(Q)}^2
    \leq
    C\big(A_u(1)+E_u(1)\big).
\]
Hence
\[
    \|p_1^u\|_
    {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
    ((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}
    \leq
    C\big(A_u(1)+E_u(1)\big).
\]

For the contribution generated by $n\nabla\phi$, define
$\tilde r_*$ by
\[
    \frac1{\tilde r_*}
    =
    \frac1{\tilde r}+\frac13.
\]
Since $3/2<\tilde r<3$, one has
\[
    1<\tilde r_*<\frac32.
\]
The Hardy--Littlewood--Sobolev inequality
(see~\cite{Stein}) therefore yields
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|p_1^n\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}
&\leq
C
\|\chi n\nabla\phi\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r_*}(Q)}
\leq
CM_\phi
\|n\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r_*}(Q)}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Since $B$ has finite measure and
$\tilde r_*<\tilde r$,
\[
    \|n\|_
    {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r_*}(Q)}
    \leq
    C
    \|n\|_
    {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q)}
    =
    C\|\sqrt n\|_
    {L_t^{2\tilde q}L_x^{2\tilde r}(Q)}^2
    \leq
    C\big(A_{\sqrt n}(1)+E_{\sqrt n}(1)\big).
\]
Consequently,
\[
    \|p_1^n\|_
    {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
    ((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}
    \leq
    CM_\phi
    \big(A_{\sqrt n}(1)+E_{\sqrt n}(1)\big).
\]
Combining the preceding two estimates, we obtain
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|p_1\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}
\leq
C\Big[
A_u(1)+E_u(1)+
M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt n}(1)+E_{\sqrt n}(1)\big)
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]

It remains to estimate the harmonic part $p_2$. Since
$p_2(\cdot,t)$ is harmonic in $B_{3/4}$, the standard interior
estimate for harmonic functions implies
\[
    \|\nabla p_2(\cdot,t)\|_{L^\infty(B_{1/2})}
    \leq
    C
    \|p_2(\cdot,t)-[p_2]_{3/4}(t)\|_
    {L^1(B_{3/4})}.
\]
Hence, for every $\gamma\in(0,1/2]$,
the Poincar\'e inequality gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\|p_2-[p_2]_{\gamma}(t)\|_
{L^{\tilde r}(B_\gamma)}
\leq C\gamma\|\nabla p_2(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\tilde{r}}(B_{\gamma})}\leq C\gamma^{1+\frac3{\tilde r}}\|\nabla p_2(\cdot,t)\|_{L^\infty(B_{1/2})}\\&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\leq
C\gamma^{1+\frac3{\tilde r}}
\|p_2-[p_2]_{3/4}(t)\|_
{L^1(B_{3/4})}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Taking the $L^{\tilde q}$ norm in time, and using
$(-\gamma^2,0)\subset(-1,0)$, we obtain
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|p_2-[p_2]_{\gamma}(t)\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma)}
\leq
C\gamma^{1+\frac3{\tilde r}}
\|p_2-[p_2]_{3/4}(t)\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1
((-1,0)\times B_{3/4})}.
\end{aligned}
\]

Since $p_2=p-p_1$, the elementary estimate
\[
    \|f-[f]_{B_{3/4}}\|_{L^1(B_{3/4})}
    \leq
    2\|f-c(t)\|_{L^1(B_{3/4})}
\]
valid for every function $c(t)$, applied with
$c(t)=[p]_1(t)$, gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|p_2-[p_2]_{3/4}(t)\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1
((-1,0)\times B_{3/4})}
\leq
C D_{\tilde q,1}(1)
+
C
\|p_1\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Therefore,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\frac1\gamma
\|p_2-[p_2]_{\gamma}(t)\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma)}
\\&\leq{}
C\gamma^{\frac3{\tilde r}}
D_{\tilde q,1}(1)
+
C\gamma^{\frac3{\tilde r}}
\Big[
A_u(1)+E_u(1)
+
M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt n}(1)+E_{\sqrt n}(1)\big)
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]

For the localized part $p_1$, we simply use
\[
\begin{aligned}
\frac1\gamma
\|p_1-[p_1]_{\gamma}(t)\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma)}
&\leq
C\gamma^{-1}
\|p_1\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}
((-1,0)\times\mathbb R^3)}
\\
&\leq
C\gamma^{-1}
\Big[
A_u(1)+E_u(1)
+
M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt n}(1)+E_{\sqrt n}(1)\big)
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]
Since $\gamma^{3/\tilde r}\leq\gamma^{-1}$ for
$0<\gamma\leq1/2$, combining the estimates for $p_1$ and $p_2$
gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
D(\gamma)
\leq{}
C\gamma^{\frac3{\tilde r}}
D_{\tilde q,1}(1)
+
C\gamma^{-1}
\Big[
A_u(1)+E_u(1)
+
M_\phi\big(A_{\sqrt n}(1)+E_{\sqrt n}(1)\big)
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]

A standard parabolic rescaling now yields
\[
\begin{aligned}
D(\gamma\rho)
\leq{}
C\gamma^{\frac3{\tilde r}}
D_{\tilde q,1}(\rho)
+
C\gamma^{-1}
\Big[
A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)
+
\rho M_\phi
\big(A_{\sqrt n}(\rho)+E_{\sqrt n}(\rho)\big)
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]
The factor $\rho$ in front of $\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty}$ follows from
the rescaling
\[
    \phi_\rho(x)=\phi(\rho x),
    \qquad
    \|\nabla\phi_\rho\|_{L^\infty}
    =
    \rho\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty}.
\]

Finally, H\"older's inequality in space and
$ \frac{2}{\tilde q}+\frac{3}{\tilde r}=3$
   give
\[
    D_{\tilde q,1}(\rho)
    \leq
    C D(\rho).
\]
Thus
\[
\begin{aligned}
D(\gamma\rho)
\leq{}
C\gamma^{\frac3{\tilde r}}D(\rho)
+
C\gamma^{-1}
\Big[
A_u(\rho)+E_u(\rho)
+
\rho M_\phi
\big(A_{\sqrt n}(\rho)+E_{\sqrt n}(\rho)\big)
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]
Since $\tilde r<3$, one has $3/\tilde r>1$, and hence for
every $ 0<\epsilon_1<\frac3{\tilde r}-1$
and every $\gamma\in(0,1/2]$, $\gamma^{3/\tilde r}
    \leq
    \gamma^{1+\epsilon_1}$.
This proves the asserted interior pressure estimate.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{corollary2}
  	Let $(n,c,u,p)$ be a suitable weak solution of \eqref{che}--\eqref{bound}. For any $\gamma\in(0,\frac12]$, $0<\rho\leq1$, and $\epsilon_1\in(0,\frac{3}{\tilde{r}}-1)$, if
  	\begin{align*}
  		A_{u,\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+E_{u,\sqrt{n}}(\rho)\leq 1,
  	\end{align*}
  	then
  	\begin{align*}
  		&D(\gamma\rho)\leq C_2\Big[\gamma^{1+\epsilon_1}\Big(D_{\tilde{q},1}(\rho)+\big(A_{u,\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+E_{u,\sqrt{n}}(\rho)\big)^\frac12 \Big)\\&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\gamma^{-1}\Big(A_{u,\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+E_{u,\sqrt{n}}(\rho)\Big)\Big],
  	\end{align*}
  	where $C_2=C(\tilde{q},\tilde{r},\epsilon_1,M_\phi)$.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:pressure-53}
Let $(n,c,u,p)$ be a suitable weak solution of
\eqref{che}--\eqref{bound}, Set
\[
\bar P(r)
:=
r^{-5/3}
\int_{\mathcal Q_r}
|p-[p]_r(t)|^{5/3}\,dx\,dt.
\]
Then, for every $0<4r\leq\rho\leq1$,
\begin{align}
\bar P(r)
&\le
C\left(\frac r\rho\right)^{5/3}\bar P(\rho)+
C\left(\frac r\rho\right)^{5/3}
\left[
A_{u,\sqrt n}(\rho)^{2/3}E_{u,\sqrt n}(\rho)
+A_u(\rho)^{1/3}E_u(\rho)^{1/2}
\right]
\nonumber\\
&\quad+
C\left(\frac{\rho}{r}\right)^{5/3}
A_{u,\sqrt n}(\rho)^{2/3}E_{u,\sqrt n}(\rho)+
C\left(\frac{\rho}{r}\right)^{5/3}
\rho ^{5/3}A_{\sqrt n}(\rho)^{5/3}.\label{eq:pressure-53-common}
\end{align}
where $C=C(M_\phi)>0$.
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
By translation and parabolic rescaling, we first assume
$\rho=1$ and write $\gamma=r/\rho\le1/4$.
The local Sobolev inequality and interpolation give
\[
\|n\|_{L^{5/3}(\mathcal{Q})}
=
\|\sqrt n\|_{L^{10/3}(\mathcal{Q})}^2
\le
C\left[
A_{\sqrt n}(1)^{2/5}E_{\sqrt n}(1)^{3/5}
+A_{\sqrt n}(1)
\right].
\]
The last term is necessary because $\sqrt n$ has neither
zero trace nor zero spatial mean.


For a boundary cylinder, use the Stokes decomposition
from Lemma~\ref{p} with
\[
\tilde q=\tilde r=\frac53,
\qquad r_*=\frac{15}{14},
\qquad r'=\frac{15}{4}.
\]
The auxiliary fields $v,p_1$ are defined on $\tilde Q$ from
Lemma~\ref{p}; their global norms below are taken on this
domain. The original fields and forcing terms are estimated
on $\mathcal Q=Q_1^+$, and the smaller cylinders are contained
in $\tilde Q$.
Since $u=0$ on the flat boundary,
Sobolev--Poincar\'e interpolation yields
\[
\|u\cdot\nabla u\|_{L^{5/3}_tL^{15/14}_x(\mathcal{Q})}
\le C A_u(1)^{2/5}E_u(1)^{3/5},
\qquad
\|u\|_{L^{5/3}_tL^1_x(\mathcal{Q})}
\le C A_u(1)^{1/5}E_u(1)^{3/10}.
\]
Moreover,
\[
\|n\nabla\phi\|_{L^{5/3}_tL^{15/14}_x(\mathcal{Q})}
\le CM_\phi
\left[
A_{\sqrt n}(1)^{2/5}E_{\sqrt n}(1)^{3/5}
+A_{\sqrt n}(1)
\right].
\]
Applying the Stokes estimate to the localized problem,
with the preceding bounds for its forcing terms, gives
\begin{align*}
&\|p_1\|_{L^{5/3}(\tilde Q)}
+\|v\|_{L^{5/3}_tL^1_x(\tilde Q)}
\\
&\quad\le
C A_u(1)^{2/5}E_u(1)^{3/5}
+CM_\phi
\left[
A_{\sqrt n}(1)^{2/5}E_{\sqrt n}(1)^{3/5}
+A_{\sqrt n}(1)
\right].
\end{align*}
For the homogeneous remainder $(w,p_2)=(u-v,p-p_1)$,
the boundary Stokes estimate with $r'=15/4$ yields
\begin{align*}
\gamma^{-1}
\|p_2-[p_2]_\gamma\|_{L^{5/3}(\mathcal{Q}_{\gamma})}
\le C\gamma
\left[
\bar P(1)^{3/5}
+\|p_1\|_{L^{5/3}(\tilde Q)}
+\|v\|_{L^{5/3}_tL^1_x(\tilde Q)}
+\|u\|_{L^{5/3}_tL^1_x(\mathcal{Q})}
\right].
\end{align*}
Combining these estimates with
\[
\gamma^{-1}
\|p_1-[p_1]_\gamma\|_{L^{5/3}(\mathcal{Q}_{\gamma})}
\le
C\gamma^{-1}\|p_1\|_{L^{5/3}(\tilde Q)}
\]
and raising to the power $5/3$ proves the asserted
unit-scale estimate. Here the pure density term with
coefficient $\gamma^{5/3}$ is absorbed into its counterpart
with coefficient $\gamma^{-5/3}$.



For an interior cylinder, use the decomposition
$p=p_1^u+p_1^n+p_2$ from Lemma~\ref{p}.
The Calder\'on--Zygmund inequality and interpolation
for $u-[u]_1$ give
\[
\|p_1^u\|_{L^{5/3}(\mathcal{Q})}
\le C A_u(1)^{2/5}E_u(1)^{3/5}.
\]
The Hardy--Littlewood--Sobolev inequality gives
\begin{align*}
\|p_1^n\|_{L^{5/3}(\mathcal{Q})}
\le CM_\phi
\|n\|_{L^{5/3}_tL^{15/14}_x(\mathcal{Q})}
\le CM_\phi
\left[
A_{\sqrt n}(1)^{2/5}E_{\sqrt n}(1)^{3/5}
+A_{\sqrt n}(1)
\right].
\end{align*}
Since $p_2$ is harmonic in $B_{3/4}$, its interior
oscillation estimate implies
\begin{align*}
\bar P(\gamma)
\le C\gamma^3\bar P(1)
+C\gamma^{-5/3}
\Bigl[
A_u(1)^{2/3}E_u(1)
+M_\phi^{5/3}
\bigl(
A_{\sqrt n}(1)^{2/3}E_{\sqrt n}(1)
+A_{\sqrt n}(1)^{5/3}
\bigr)
\Bigr].
\end{align*}
This implies the asserted unit-scale estimate because
$\gamma^3\le\gamma^{5/3}$.

Rescaling back replaces $M_\phi$ by $\rho M_\phi$,
while $A$, $E$, and $\bar P$ remain invariant.
Using $\rho\le1$ and
\[
A_u^{2/3}E_u+A_{\sqrt n}^{2/3}E_{\sqrt n}
\le A_{u,\sqrt n}^{2/3}E_{u,\sqrt n}
\]
at radius $\rho$ completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{key} 
There exists a constant 
$\varepsilon_2=\varepsilon_2(\tilde q,M_c,M_\phi)>0$ such that if 
\begin{equation}\label{4.9} 
A(1)+E(1)+D_{\tilde q,1}(1)\leq\varepsilon_2,
\end{equation} 
then there exist constants $\eta_1,\eta_2>0$ and $\rho_0>0$ such that 
\begin{equation}\label{4.10} 
A(\rho)+E(\rho)\leq\rho^{2-\eta_1},
\qquad 
D(\rho)\leq\rho^{1+\eta_2}
\end{equation} 
for every $\rho\in(0,\rho_0]$.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Choose 
\[
\epsilon_1
=
\min\left\{
\frac1{100},
\frac12\left(\frac3{\tilde r}-1\right)
\right\},
\qquad 
\eta=\epsilon_1^2,
\qquad 
\beta=\frac{\eta}{4},
\qquad 
\delta=\frac{\eta^2}{16},
\]
and set 
\[
l=\left\lceil\frac{12}{\epsilon_1}\right\rceil,
\qquad 
\tilde\beta=(1+\beta)^{l+1}-1.
\]
These parameters depend only on $\tilde q$, since 
\[
\frac2{\tilde q}+\frac3{\tilde r}=3.
\]
Moreover, Bernoulli's inequality and
\[
e^x-1
\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{x^j}{j!}
\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}x^j
=\frac{x}{1-x},
\qquad 0\leq x<1,
\]
give
\[
3\epsilon_1
\leq(l+1)\beta
\leq\tilde\beta
\leq e^{3\epsilon_1+\epsilon_1^2/2}-1
\leq4\epsilon_1.
\]

For an initial radius $\rho_0\in(0,1/2)$ to be chosen below, define
\[
\rho_k=\rho_0^{(1+\beta)^k},
\qquad k\geq0.
\]
We shall prove the stronger discrete estimates
\begin{equation}\label{4.11} 
A(\rho_k)+E(\rho_k)
\leq\frac14\rho_k^{2-\eta},
\qquad 
D(\rho_k)\leq\frac14\rho_k^{1+\eta}.
\end{equation} 
We first derive the inductive estimates, then choose $\rho_0$ and 
initialize the induction.

\par\medskip
\noindent
\textbf{Step 1. Inductive estimates.}\quad
Suppose that \eqref{4.11} holds for all indices
$0\le j\le k$, where $k\ge l$.
We take $\rho_0$ sufficiently small that the smallness
conditions in Corollaries~\ref{corollary1}
and~\ref{corollary2} hold at these scales and
$\rho_0^\beta\le1/2$.
The precise choice will be made in Step~2.

For the energy estimate, take $\gamma=\rho_k^\beta$.
Then $\gamma\rho_k=\rho_{k+1}$, and
Corollary~\ref{corollary1} gives
\begin{align*}
A(\rho_{k+1})+E(\rho_{k+1})
&\le
C_1\rho_k^{2\beta}
[A(\rho_k)+E(\rho_k)]^{1-\delta}
\\
&\quad+
C_1\rho_k^{-2\beta}
[A(\rho_k)+E(\rho_k)]^{3/2-\delta}
\\
&\quad+
C_1\rho_k^{-2\beta}
[A(\rho_k)+E(\rho_k)]^{1/2}D(\rho_k)
\\
&\le
C\left[
\rho_k^{2\beta+(2-\eta)(1-\delta)}
+
\rho_k^{-2\beta+(2-\eta)(3/2-\delta)}
+
\rho_k^{-2\beta+2+\eta/2}
\right].
\end{align*}
Here and below, the constants in the inductive estimates
are independent of $k$, $\rho_0$, and $\varepsilon_2$.
The required strict inequality between exponents is
\begin{equation}\label{4.12}
\begin{split}
\min\Bigl\{
&2\beta+(2-\eta)(1-\delta),\,
-2\beta+(2-\eta)(3/2-\delta),\\
&-2\beta+2+\eta/2
\Bigr\}
>
(2-\eta)(1+\beta).
\end{split}
\end{equation}
Indeed, subtracting the right-hand side from the three
exponents on the left gives, respectively,
\[
\beta\eta-(2-\eta)\delta
=\frac{\eta^2(2+\eta)}{16}>0,
\]
\[
(2-\eta)(1/2-\delta)-(4-\eta)\beta
=
1-\frac32\eta+\frac18\eta^2+\frac1{16}\eta^3>0,
\]
and
\[
\frac32\eta-(4-\eta)\beta
=
\frac12\eta+\frac14\eta^2>0.
\]

For the pressure estimate, we use the delayed scale
$\rho_{k-l}$, as in the iteration scheme of
\cite[Lemma 3.4]{DW2020}.
The definition of $\tilde\beta$ ensures that
\[
\rho_{k-l}^{1+\tilde\beta}=\rho_{k+1}.
\]
Thus we apply Corollary~\ref{corollary2} with
$\rho=\rho_{k-l}$ and
$\gamma=\rho_{k-l}^{\tilde\beta}$.
Since $\tilde\beta>\beta$, this scaling factor is also
at most $1/2$.
Using
$D_{\tilde q,1}(\rho)\le CD(\rho)$ and \eqref{4.11},
we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:D_estimate}
D(\rho_{k+1})
\le
C\left[
\rho_{k-l}^{(1+\epsilon_1)\tilde\beta+1+\eta}
+
\rho_{k-l}^{(1+\epsilon_1)\tilde\beta+1-\eta/2}
+
\rho_{k-l}^{-\tilde\beta+2-\eta}
\right].
\end{equation}
The required pressure exponent condition is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:exponent_condition}
\begin{split}
\min\Bigl\{
&(1+\epsilon_1)\tilde\beta+1+\eta,\,
(1+\epsilon_1)\tilde\beta+1-\eta/2,\\
&-\tilde\beta+2-\eta
\Bigr\}
>
(1+\eta)(1+\tilde\beta).
\end{split}
\end{equation}
The corresponding exponent differences are
\[
(\epsilon_1-\eta)\tilde\beta,
\qquad
(\epsilon_1-\eta)\tilde\beta-\frac32\eta,
\qquad
1-2\eta-(2+\eta)\tilde\beta.
\]
They are all positive. In fact,
\[
(\epsilon_1-\eta)\tilde\beta-\frac32\eta
\ge
\left(\frac32-3\epsilon_1\right)\epsilon_1^2>0,
\]
and
\[
1-2\eta-(2+\eta)\tilde\beta
\ge
1-2\epsilon_1^2
-4\epsilon_1(2+\epsilon_1^2)>0.
\]
Here we used
$3\epsilon_1\le\tilde\beta\le4\epsilon_1$
and $0<\epsilon_1\le1/100$.

The strict exponent inequalities
\eqref{4.12} and \eqref{eq:exponent_condition} therefore
give, for some $\xi>0$ and $C_*>0$ independent of
$k$, $\rho_0$, and $\varepsilon_2$,
\[
A(\rho_{k+1})+E(\rho_{k+1})
\le C_*\rho_{k+1}^{2-\eta+\xi},
\qquad
D(\rho_{k+1})
\le C_*\rho_{k+1}^{1+\eta+\xi}.
\]

\par\medskip
\noindent
\textbf{Step 2. Choice of radius and initialization.}\quad
Fix $\rho_0\in(0,1/2)$ sufficiently small that
\[
\rho_0^\beta\le\frac12,
\qquad
C_*\rho_0^\xi\le\frac14,
\]
and that $\frac14\rho_0^{2-\eta}$ lies below the
smallness thresholds required in
Corollaries~\ref{corollary1} and~\ref{corollary2}.
This fixes all the radii $\rho_k$ before
$\varepsilon_2$ is chosen.

We now initialize \eqref{4.11} for $0\le j\le l$.
By \eqref{4.9}, domain inclusion gives
\[
A(1/2)+E(1/2)\le2\varepsilon_2.
\]
Applying Corollary~\ref{corollary2} at scale one
with $\gamma=1/2$, and taking $\varepsilon_2\le1$,
yields
\[
D(1/2)\le C_0\varepsilon_2^{1/2}.
\]
Consequently, for $0<r\le1/2$, domain inclusion
and comparison of the pressure averages give
\[
A(r)+E(r)\le\frac{\varepsilon_2}{r},
\qquad
D(r)\le\frac{C_0\varepsilon_2^{1/2}}{r},
\]
after enlarging $C_0$ if necessary.

Since $\rho_l\le\rho_j$ for $0\le j\le l$,
it suffices to choose $\varepsilon_2>0$ so small that
\[
\varepsilon_2\le\frac14\rho_l^{3-\eta},
\qquad
C_0\varepsilon_2^{1/2}
\le\frac14\rho_l^{2+\eta}.
\]
These inequalities imply \eqref{4.11} for
$j=0,\ldots,l$.

The estimates in Step~1 and
$C_*\rho_0^\xi\le1/4$ then show that, whenever
\eqref{4.11} holds up to an index $k\ge l$,
\[
A(\rho_{k+1})+E(\rho_{k+1})
\le\frac14\rho_{k+1}^{2-\eta},
\qquad
D(\rho_{k+1})
\le\frac14\rho_{k+1}^{1+\eta}.
\]
Thus \eqref{4.11} holds for every $k\ge0$.
All parameter choices depend only on
$\tilde q$, $M_c$, and $M_\phi$, so the same is true
of the resulting threshold $\varepsilon_2$.

\par\medskip
\noindent
\textbf{Step 3. Passage to arbitrary radii.}\quad
Let $0<r\le\rho_0$, and choose $k\ge0$ such that
\[
\rho_{k+1}<r\le\rho_k.
\]
By the definitions and domain inclusion,
\[
A(r)+E(r)
\le
\frac{\rho_k}{r}
\bigl(A(\rho_k)+E(\rho_k)\bigr)
\le
\frac{\rho_k^{3-\eta}}{4r}.
\]
For the pressure, comparison of spatial averages gives,
for almost every time,
\[
\|p-[p]_r\|_{L^{\tilde r}(B_r)}
\le
2\|p-[p]_{\rho_k}\|_{L^{\tilde r}(B_r)}
\le
2\|p-[p]_{\rho_k}\|_{L^{\tilde r}(B_{\rho_k})}.
\]
Restricting the time interval accordingly, we obtain
\[
D(r)
\le
2\frac{\rho_k}{r}D(\rho_k)
\le
\frac{\rho_k^{2+\eta}}{2r}.
\]
Since
$r>\rho_{k+1}=\rho_k^{1+\beta}$, it follows that
\[
A(r)+E(r)
\le
\frac14 r^{(3-\eta)/(1+\beta)-1}
\le r^{2-\eta_1},
\]
and
\[
D(r)
\le
\frac12 r^{(2+\eta)/(1+\beta)-1}
\le r^{1+\eta_2},
\]
where
\[
\eta_1=\frac{\eta+3\beta}{1+\beta}>0,
\qquad
\eta_2=\frac{\eta-2\beta}{1+\beta}>0.
\]
The positivity of $\eta_2$ follows from
$\beta=\eta/4$.
This proves \eqref{4.10}.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
We use the normalized reference cylinders
$\mathcal Q_r=(-r^2,0)\times\mathcal B_r$
introduced in Section~\ref{subsec:notation-scaling}, and write $\mathcal Q=\mathcal Q_1$.
After a spatial translation and a time shift, we take the center
to be $(0,0)$; in the boundary case, the spatial translation
is tangential to $\Gamma$.

We first give the preliminary estimates common to both cases.
We then establish the local boundary estimates, treat the
interior case, and finally complete the boundary case
by a covering argument. For any integrable scalar- or vector-valued function $f$, set
\[
[f]_\rho:=\fint_{\mathcal Q_\rho}f(x,t)\,dx\,dt,
\]
where vector-valued averages are taken componentwise.
For the pressure, we retain the spatial-average notation
\[
[p]_\rho(t):=\fint_{\mathcal B_\rho}p(x,t)\,dx.
\]
Whenever the argument $t$ is omitted, $[p]_\rho$ still denotes
this time-dependent spatial average.
	   		 If the exponent in the statement satisfies $\tilde q\geq2$, set $q_*=3/2$.  Since the time interval has length one,
	   		 \[
	   		 \|p-[p]_1(t)\|_{L_t^{q_*}L_x^1(\mathcal{Q})}\leq
	   		 \|p-[p]_1(t)\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1(\mathcal{Q})}.
	   		 \]
	   		 Thus the result for $q_*$ implies the stated result after decreasing the threshold.  It is therefore enough to prove the proposition for $1<\tilde q<2$, which is precisely the range used in the preceding pressure lemmas; $\tilde r\in(3/2,3)$ is then determined by $2/\tilde q+3/\tilde r=3$.
	   		 First, we observe that the smallness condition \eqref{14} in Proposition \ref{main1} implies the initial condition \eqref{4.9} of Lemma \ref{key}. Indeed, by the definitions of the scale-invariant quantities, the total energy at the unit scale satisfies
   		 \begin{align*}
   		 A_{u,\sqrt{n},\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}}(1) + E_{u,\sqrt{n},\nabla \sqrt{\tilde{c}}}(1) + D_{\tilde{q},1}(1) \leq C \left( \varepsilon_0 + \varepsilon_0^{1/\tilde{q}} \right)
   		 \end{align*}
	   		 where $C=C(\tilde q,M_c,M_\phi)>1$. Therefore, by choosing $\varepsilon_0 = \varepsilon_0(\tilde{q},M_c,M_\phi) > 0$ sufficiently small such that $C ( \varepsilon_0 + \varepsilon_0^{1/\tilde{q}} ) \leq \varepsilon_2$, the condition \eqref{4.9} holds.

             We now consider the boundary case, so that
$\mathcal Q_r=Q_r^+$ and $\mathcal B_r=B_r^+$.
   		 Consequently, applying Lemma \ref{key}, we have the following estimates for $\rho \in (0, \rho_0]$:
   		\begin{align}\label{rho-estimate}
   			&\|p-[p]_{\rho}\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{r}}(Q^+_\rho)} \leq \rho^{2+\eta_2}, \quad \||u|^2\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{r}}(Q^+_\rho)} \leq C\rho^{3-\eta_1}, \nonumber\\
&\||\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{r}}(Q^+_\rho)} \leq C\rho^{3-\eta_1}, \quad \|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{r}}(Q^+_\rho)} \leq C\rho^{3-\eta_1}.
   		\end{align}
The last three bounds also use Lemma~\ref{interpolation};
their constant $C$ depends only on the fixed parameters and is
independent of $\rho$.
    
    For $\rho\in(0,\rho_0]$ and each $i\in\{1,2,3\}$, put
$f^i=-n\partial_i\phi$ on $Q_\rho^+$.
Throughout the following construction, a superscript $*$,
placed after the component index, denotes the corresponding
function on the full ball or cylinder.

For almost every $t\in(-\rho^2,0)$, let $f^{i,*}$ be the odd
extension of $f^i$ across $\{x_3=0\}$, and let
$h^i\in W^{2,\tilde r}(B_\rho)\cap W_0^{1,\tilde r}(B_\rho)$
solve
\begin{equation}\label{eq:poisson}
    \Delta h^i=f^{i,*}\quad\text{in }B_\rho,
    \qquad h^i=0\quad\text{on }\partial B_\rho.
\end{equation}
Uniqueness and symmetry imply that $h^i$ is odd in $x_3$.
Set
\[
G^{i,*}:=\nabla h^i,
\qquad
G^i:=G^{i,*}|_{Q_\rho^+},
\qquad
G:=(G_j^i)_{i,j=1}^3.
\]
Then $\operatorname{div}G^i=f^i$ in $Q_\rho^+$.
Moreover, $G_1^{i,*}$ and $G_2^{i,*}$ are odd in $x_3$,
whereas $G_3^{i,*}$ is even.
The elliptic estimate, followed by integration in time, gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:force-divergence-bound}
    \|G\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
    \leq C\rho\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty(Q_\rho^+)}
    \|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
    \leq C\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}.
\end{equation}

For each $i\in\{1,2,3\}$, define the vector field
$\mathcal F^i=(\mathcal F_j^i)_{j=1}^3$ on $Q_\rho^+$ by
\[
\mathcal F_j^i
=-u^iu^j-\delta_{ij}(p-[p]_\rho)+G_j^i.
\]
Let $\mathcal F^{i,*}$ be its extension to $Q_\rho$ obtained
by extending $\mathcal F_1^i,\mathcal F_2^i$ oddly and
$\mathcal F_3^i$ evenly across $\{x_3=0\}$.
Then $\operatorname{div}\mathcal F^{i,*}$ is odd in $x_3$
in the sense of distributions, and
\[
\|\mathcal F^{i,*}\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho)}
\leq C
\|\mathcal F^i\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}.
\]

Extend $\mathcal F^{i,*}$ by zero outside $B_\rho$ and define
\[
\omega^{i,*}:=\mathcal T_\rho\mathcal F^{i,*},
\qquad \omega^i:=\omega^{i,*}|_{Q_\rho^+},
\qquad \omega=(\omega^1,\omega^2,\omega^3),
\]
using Lemma~\ref{lem:heat-potentials}.
Then $\omega^{i,*}$ is odd and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:omega}
\partial_t\omega^i-\Delta\omega^i
=-\partial_j(u^iu^j)-n\partial_i\phi-\partial_i(p-[p]_\rho)
\quad\hbox{in }Q_\rho^+.
\end{equation}
In particular, \eqref{eq:heat-potential-div} gives directly
\begin{align}\label{omega}
\|\omega\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
&\leq C\rho\left(
 \||u|^2\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
 +\|p-[p]_\rho\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
 +\|G\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}\right)\notag\\
&\leq C\rho(\rho^{3-\eta_1}+\rho^{2+\eta_2})
\leq C\rho^{3+\eta_2},
\end{align}
where $\eta_1+\eta_2<1$ and $\rho\leq1$.

Let $u^{i,*}$ be the odd extension of $u^i$, component by
component; no solenoidal property of this extension is required.
Its scalar momentum equation has right-hand side
$\operatorname{div}\mathcal F^{i,*}$ in $Q_\rho$.
Indeed, test the half-cylinder equation with the odd part of a
full-cylinder test function. That part vanishes on $\Gamma$;
the zero trace of $u^i$ removes the diffusion boundary term, and
the prescribed parities give exactly the reflected divergence.
The identity follows for the present weak solutions by approximation
of such zero-trace tests and the local integrability of
$\nabla u,u\otimes u,p,n$.
Thus $v^{i,*}=u^{i,*}-\omega^{i,*}$ is odd and caloric in $Q_\rho$.
In particular, its smooth interior representative vanishes on
the flat boundary. Writing $v=u-\omega$ and using
Lemma~\ref{lem:caloric-parity-decay}(i) with
$2/\tilde q+3/\tilde r=3$, we have
\begin{equation}\label{v}
\|v\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma^+)}
\leq C(\gamma/\rho)^4
\|v\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)},
\qquad 0<\gamma<\rho.
\end{equation}
This estimate uses the zero boundary value itself, so no subtraction
of an incompatible constant at $\Gamma$ is involved.

Combining \eqref{omega} and \eqref{v} gives
\[
\|u\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma^+)}
\leq C(\gamma/\rho)^4
\|u\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
+C\rho^{3+\eta_2}.
\]
The norm $\|u\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_{\rho_0}^+)}$
is bounded by \eqref{rho-estimate} and H\"older's inequality.
Lemma~\ref{it1}, since $3+\eta_2<4$, yields
\[
\|u\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma^+)}
\leq C\gamma^{3+\eta_2}.
\]


The mixed-norm bound for the averaging operator then implies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:u-campanato-decay}
\|u-[u]_\gamma\|_{L^1(Q_\gamma^+)}
\leq C\gamma^2\|u-[u]_\gamma\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma^+)}
\leq C\gamma^{5+\eta_2}.
\end{equation}



We next estimate $\nabla\tilde c=\nabla c$.
On $Q_\rho^+$ set
\[
g=-n\tilde c+n-u\cdot\nabla\tilde c.
\]
Extend $g$ evenly across $\Gamma$ and by zero outside $B_\rho$;
denote the resulting function by $g^*$. Define
\[
c_1^*=\mathcal S_\rho g^*,\qquad
c_1=c_1^*|_{Q_\rho^+},\qquad c_2=\tilde c-c_1.
\]
These definitions specify the auxiliary functions uniquely, without
requiring zero data on the artificial sphere. The even extension
$\tilde c^*$ satisfies
$(\partial_t-\Delta)\tilde c^*=g^*$ inside $Q_\rho$.
This follows by testing the weak scalar equation with the even
part of a full-cylinder test function. The weak Neumann condition
and $u=0$ on $\Gamma$ remove the boundary flux.
Consequently $c_2^*:=\tilde c^*-c_1^*$ is even and caloric,
and hence $\partial_3c_2^*=0$ on the flat boundary.

By \eqref{rho-estimate}, $1\leq\tilde c\leq M_c$, and
$\nabla\tilde c=2\sqrt{\tilde c}\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}$,
\[
\begin{split}
\|g\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
\leq C(M_c)\left(
\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
 +\||u|^2\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}^{1/2}
  \||\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}|^2\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}^{1/2}
\right)\leq C\rho^{3-\eta_1}.
\end{split}
\]
The explicit gradient estimate \eqref{eq:heat-potential-scalar}
therefore gives
\[
\|\nabla c_1\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
\leq C\rho\|g\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
\leq C\rho^{4-\eta_1}.
\]

To preserve the boundary symmetry when subtracting constants, set
\[
\Phi_c(r)=\inf_{a\in\mathbb R^3,\ a_3=0}
 \|\nabla\tilde c-a\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_r^+)},
\qquad
\Phi_{c_2}(r)=\inf_{a\in\mathbb R^3,\ a_3=0}
 \|\nabla c_2-a\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_r^+)}.
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:caloric-parity-decay}(ii) gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c2-gradient-decay}
\Phi_{c_2}(\gamma)
\leq C(\gamma/\rho)^4\Phi_{c_2}(\rho),
\qquad 0<\gamma<\rho.
\end{equation}
The triangle inequality, taking the infimum over $a_3=0$,
implies
\[
\Phi_c(\gamma)\leq\Phi_{c_2}(\gamma)+C\rho^{4-\eta_1},
\qquad
\Phi_{c_2}(\rho)\leq\Phi_c(\rho)+C\rho^{4-\eta_1}.
\]
Hence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:grad-c-campanato-iteration}
\Phi_c(\gamma)
\leq C(\gamma/\rho)^4\Phi_c(\rho)+C\rho^{4-\eta_1}.
\end{equation}
The initial value $\Phi_c(\rho_0)$ is bounded by taking $a=0$.
Since $4-\eta_1<4$, Lemma~\ref{it1} yields
$\Phi_c(\gamma)\leq C\gamma^{4-\eta_1}$.
For every admissible constant $a$ the ordinary averaging estimate gives
$\|\nabla\tilde c-[\nabla\tilde c]_\gamma\|
\leq2\|\nabla\tilde c-a\|$ in the same mixed norm.
Thus
\begin{equation}\label{eq:grad-c-mixed-campanato}
\|\nabla\tilde c-[\nabla\tilde c]_\gamma\|_
 {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma^+)}
\leq2\Phi_c(\gamma)\leq C\gamma^{4-\eta_1},
