\qquad 0<\gamma\leq\rho_0.
\end{equation}


Since
\[
	\frac{2}{\tilde q}+\frac{3}{\tilde r}=3,
\]
H\"older's inequality yields
\begin{align}
	\|\nabla\tilde c-[\nabla\tilde c]_{\gamma}\|_
	{L^1(Q_\gamma^+)}
	&\leq
	C\gamma^2
	\|\nabla\tilde c-[\nabla\tilde c]_{\gamma}\|_
	{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma^+)}
	\nonumber\\
	&\leq C\gamma^{6-\eta_1}.
	\label{ccc}
\end{align}

We justify the uniformity in the center before applying
Campanato's characterization.
Recall that the reference cylinder in the present
boundary case is $Q_1^+(0,0)$.
For every boundary center
\[
z_b=((x_1,x_2,0),t),
\qquad |(x_1,x_2)|<1/8,\quad -1/64<t<0,
\]
we have
\[
Q_{1/2}^+(z_b)\subset Q_1^+(0,0).
\]
Write
\[
X(z,r):=
A_{u,\sqrt n,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}(z,r)
+
E_{u,\sqrt n,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}(z,r).
\]
By restriction to $Q_{1/2}^+(z_b)$ and comparison
of the spatial pressure averages, hypothesis \eqref{14} gives
\[
X(z_b,1/2)+D_{\tilde q,1}(z_b,1/2)
\leq C\bigl(\varepsilon_0+\varepsilon_0^{1/\tilde q}\bigr),
\]
with $C$ independent of $z_b$.
Choosing $\varepsilon_0$ sufficiently small, we may
therefore rescale each $Q_{1/2}^+(z_b)$ to a unit
half-cylinder and apply Lemma~\ref{key}, with the same
parameters and the same choice of $\rho_0$ for all
these centers.

Returning to the present coordinates, we obtain
\[
X(z_b,r)\leq Cr^{2-\eta_1},
\qquad
D(z_b,r)\leq Cr^{1+\eta_2},
\qquad 0<r\leq \rho_0/2,
\]
uniformly in $z_b$.

Repeating the preceding arguments gives, uniformly for the same
boundary centers and radii,
\[
\|u\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_r^+(z_b))}
\leq Cr^{3+\eta_2},\qquad
\inf_{a_3=0}\|\nabla\tilde c-a\|_
 {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_r^+(z_b))}
\leq Cr^{4-\eta_1}.
\]
In particular, \eqref{eq:u-campanato-decay} and \eqref{ccc}
hold on these translated half-cylinders with common constants.


Fix
\[
0<\rho\leq \min\{1/8,\rho_0/6\},
\]
to be decreased further below if necessary.
For an interior center $z=(x,t)\in Q_\rho^+(0,0)$,
put
\[
d=x_3,\qquad s=d/2,\qquad
z_b=((x_1,x_2,0),t).
\]
Here $d$ is the distance to the flat boundary,
so $Q_s(z)$ is a full cylinder contained in the
half-space. Moreover,
\[
Q_s(z)\subset Q_{3s}^+(z_b),
\qquad
3s<3\rho/2\leq \rho_0/4.
\]
Thus the uniform boundary estimates apply at
the center $z_b$ and radius $3s$.
Comparison of the energy quantities and the
spatial pressure averages gives
\[
X(z,s)\leq Ks^{2-\eta_1},
\qquad
D(z,s)\leq Ks^{1+\eta_2},
\]
where $K$ is independent of the choice of
$z\in Q_\rho^+(0,0)$.

For $0<r\leq s$, all cylinders $Q_r(z)$ are full
interior cylinders.
Corollary~\ref{corollary1} applies whenever its
smallness condition holds, while the interior
pressure estimate in Lemma~\ref{p} gives
\[
D(z,\theta r)
\leq C\theta^{3/\tilde r}D(z,r)
+C\theta^{-1}X(z,r).
\]
The boundary comparison provides decay only at the
starting radius $s$.
To propagate this decay to $r<s$ with constants
independent of $s=d/2$, we repeat the induction
argument of Lemma~\ref{key}, retaining the above
powers of $s$ in the initial bounds.
Choose $\sigma>0$ sufficiently small that
\[
2\sigma<\eta_2,\qquad
\sigma(2+\eta_2)<1-\eta_1-\eta_2,
\]
and
\[
\sigma(4-\eta_1)
<
\min\left\{
\frac{2-\eta_1}{4},
\frac{\eta_1}{2}+\eta_2
\right\}.
\]
Then choose
\[
0<\delta<
\min\left\{
\frac14,
\frac{\sigma\eta_1}{2(2-\eta_1)}
\right\}.
\]
These choices are independent of the center $z$.
Since
\[
\eta_2<3/\tilde r-1,
\qquad \eta_1+\eta_2<1,
\]
the scale induction used in Lemma~\ref{key}, now with
$s_k=s^{(1+\sigma)^k}$ and the above interior pressure
estimate, yields
\[
X(z,s_k)\leq Ks_k^{2-\eta_1},
\qquad D(z,s_k)\leq Ks_k^{1+\eta_2}.
\]
Decrease $\rho$ so that the initial energy bounds
lie below the threshold in Corollary~\ref{corollary1},
$s^\sigma\leq 1/2$, and the positive powers of $s_k$
absorb the fixed constants in the induction.
This choice is independent of the center $z$.

Comparison of consecutive scales then gives, for $r\leq s$,
\[
X(z,r)\leq Cr^{2-\widehat\eta_1},
\qquad D(z,r)\leq Cr^{1+\widehat\eta_2},
\]
where
\[
\widehat\eta_1=\frac{\eta_1+3\sigma}{1+\sigma},
\qquad
\widehat\eta_2=\frac{\eta_2-2\sigma}{1+\sigma}.
\]
These exponents satisfy
$0<\widehat\eta_2<\eta_2$,
$\eta_1<\widehat\eta_1<1$, and
$\widehat\eta_1+\widehat\eta_2<1$.


For the interior cylinders $Q_r(z)$, $0<r\leq s$, use the same
heat potentials, with the data extended by zero outside the spatial
ball and without any reflection. For the force, solve the full-ball
Poisson problem in \eqref{eq:poisson} without taking an odd extension.
The remainders $v$ and $c_2$ are caloric in the full cylinder.
Define
\[
\Psi_f(z,r)=\left\|f-\fint_{Q_r(z)}f\right\|_
 {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_r(z))}.
\]
The just-proved energy and pressure decay, Lemma~\ref{lem:heat-potentials},
and Lemma~\ref{lem:caloric-parity-decay}(iii), applied also to
the caloric vector $\nabla c_2$, imply
\[
\Psi_f(z,\gamma)
\leq C(\gamma/r)^4\Psi_f(z,r)+Cr^{3+\alpha},
\qquad 0<\gamma<r\leq s,
\]
where $(f,\alpha)=(u,\widehat\eta_2)$ or
$(\nabla\tilde c,1-\widehat\eta_1)$.
The initial bound at the moving radius $s=d/2$ is uniform:
restriction from $Q_{3s}^+(z_b)$ and the boundary mixed-norm estimates
give
\[
\Psi_f(z,s)\leq Cs^{3+\alpha_b},\qquad
\alpha_b=\eta_2\ \hbox{for }f=u,
\quad \alpha_b=1-\eta_1\ \hbox{for }f=\nabla\tilde c.
\]
In both cases $\alpha_b>\alpha$. Applying Lemma~\ref{it1} with
starting radius $s$ therefore yields
\[
\begin{split}
\Psi_f(z,r)
&\leq C(r/s)^{3+\alpha}\Psi_f(z,s)+Cr^{3+\alpha}\\
&\leq C(1+s^{\alpha_b-\alpha})r^{3+\alpha}
\leq Cr^{3+\alpha},\qquad 0<r\leq s<1.
\end{split}
\]
This explicitly proves that the oscillation constants do not
deteriorate as $d\downarrow0$.


For $r\geq d/2$, the same bound on
\[
\widehat Q_r(z)
:=(B_r(x)\cap\mathbb H^3)\times(t-r^2,t)
\]
follows from
$\widehat Q_r(z)\subset Q_{3r}^+(z_b)$
and the boundary oscillation estimates.
Fix $0<\rho_1<\rho/8$. On $Q_{\rho_1}^+$,
H\"older's inequality gives
\[
\int_{\widehat Q_r(z)}
\left|f-\fint_{\widehat Q_r(z)}f\right|\,dx\,dt
\leq Cr^{5+\alpha},
\]
uniformly in the center and in sufficiently small radii.
The parabolic Campanato characterization implies
\[
u\in C^{\widehat\eta_2,\widehat\eta_2/2}
(\overline{Q_{\rho_1}^+}),
\qquad
\nabla c=\nabla\tilde c
\in C^{1-\widehat\eta_1,(1-\widehat\eta_1)/2}
(\overline{Q_{\rho_1}^+}).
\]
The chosen $\rho_1$ depends only on the fixed parameters,
and the preceding estimates hold uniformly for
centers in $Q_{\rho_1}^+$ and all sufficiently
small radii.
For the remainder of the proof, we retain the notation
$\eta_1,\eta_2$ for the weaker exponents
$\widehat\eta_1,\widehat\eta_2$. Thus we have 
   		 \begin{align}\label{yj}
   		 \|u\|_{L^\infty_{t,x}(Q^+_{\rho_1})} + \|\nabla\tilde{c}\|_{L^\infty_{t,x}(Q^+_{\rho_1})} \leq C.
   		 \end{align}
   		We next estimate $n$.
Recall that \eqref{yj} gives uniform bounds for
$u$ and $\nabla\tilde c$ on $Q_{\rho_1}^+$.
Here and below, $\eta_1$ denotes the exponent retained
after the preceding interior estimates.

For $0<\rho\leq\rho_1/2$, put
$F=-n(u+\nabla\tilde c)$ on $Q_\rho^+$.
Extend $F_1,F_2$ evenly and $F_3$ oddly across $\Gamma$, then
extend the resulting field $F^*$ by zero outside $B_\rho$.
Set
\[
n_1^*=\mathcal T_\rho F^*,\qquad
n_1=n_1^*|_{Q_\rho^+},\qquad n_2=n-n_1.
\]
The even extension $n^*$ satisfies
$(\partial_t-\Delta)n^*=\operatorname{div}F^*$ in $Q_\rho$:
this is exactly the weak no-flux identity tested with even functions.
One may move the derivative of $n$ onto the test function because
its even part has zero normal derivative on $\Gamma$.
Thus $n_2^*:=n^*-n_1^*$ is even and caloric. This construction
uses no classical normal trace of $n$ or $F$ and imposes no
artificial spherical boundary data.
By \eqref{yj}, the density bound following from the energy decay,
and \eqref{eq:heat-potential-div},
\[
\begin{split}
\|n_1\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
\leq C\rho\|n(u+\nabla\tilde c)\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
\leq C\rho\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
\leq C\rho^{4-\eta_1}.
\end{split}
\]


The even extension of $n_2$ solves the homogeneous
heat equation on the full cylinder $Q_\rho$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:caloric-parity-decay}(iii) therefore implies, for
$0<\gamma\leq\rho/2$,
\[
\|n_2-[n_2]_\gamma\|_
 {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma^+)}
\leq
C\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right)^4
\|n_2-[n]_\rho\|_
 {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}.
\]
Using $n_2=n-n_1$ and the estimate for $n_1$, we obtain
\begin{align*}
\|n-[n]_\gamma\|_
 {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma^+)}
&\leq
\|n_2-[n_2]_\gamma\|_
 {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma^+)}
+2\|n_1\|_
 {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma^+)}\\
&\leq
C\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right)^4
\|n-[n]_\rho\|_
 {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\rho^+)}
+C\rho^{4-\eta_1}.
\end{align*}
Applying Lemma~\ref{it1} with the fixed starting radius
$\rho_1/2$, and noting that $4-\eta_1<4$, yields
\[
\|n-[n]_\gamma\|_
 {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma^+)}
\leq C\gamma^{4-\eta_1},
\qquad 0<\gamma\leq\rho_1/2.
\]
The constant may depend on the fixed radius $\rho_1$,
but is independent of $\gamma$.

Finally, since $2/\tilde q+3/\tilde r=3$,
H\"older's inequality gives
\begin{align}
\|n-[n]_\gamma\|_{L^1(Q_\gamma^+)}
&\leq
C\gamma^2
\|n-[n]_\gamma\|_
 {L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_\gamma^+)}
\leq C\gamma^{6-\eta_1}.
\label{nnn}
\end{align}

We spell out the uniformity for $n$ as well. For boundary centers
with $|x'|<\rho_1/8$ and $-\rho_1^2/64<t<0$, fix
$0<R_n\leq\rho_1/16$ small enough that the preceding uniform
energy bounds apply at all radii up to $R_n$. Their larger
cylinders lie in $Q_{\rho_1}^+$, where \eqref{yj} holds.
Repeating the preceding heat-potential argument gives the boundary
mixed-norm oscillation bound $Cr^{4-\eta_1}$ for $r\leq R_n$
with a common constant.
For an interior center $z$ in a sufficiently small fixed
half-cylinder, put $s=d/2$ and $a=\min\{s,R_n/3\}$.
If $s\leq R_n/3$, comparison with the boundary cylinder of radius
$3s\leq R_n$ gives $\Psi_n(z,a)\leq Ca^{4-\eta_1}$.
If $s>R_n/3$, the starting radius $a=R_n/3$ is fixed;
the uniform density norm then gives the same bound after changing
the constant, which may depend on $R_n$ but not on $z$.
On scales $r\leq a$, the full-cylinder heat-potential construction
and Lemma~\ref{lem:caloric-parity-decay}(iii) give
\[
\Psi_n(z,\gamma)
\leq C(\gamma/r)^4\Psi_n(z,r)+Cr^{4-\eta_1}.
\]
Here $\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde r}(Q_r(z))}
\leq Cr^{3-\eta_1}$ follows from the uniform energy decay above.
Lemma~\ref{it1} thus gives $\Psi_n(z,r)\leq Cr^{4-\eta_1}$
with a constant independent of $s$. For $s\leq r\leq R_n/3$,
restriction from the boundary cylinder of radius $3r$ gives the
same bound on the truncated cylinder. These two cases cover every
$0<r\leq R_n/3$. H\"older's inequality and Campanato's characterization
therefore give H\"older continuity of $n$ up to the boundary
on a fixed smaller half-cylinder.




 For an interior reference cylinder $Q_1$, we have
$Q_{1/4}(z)\subset Q_1$ for every $z\in Q_{1/2}$.
After decreasing $\varepsilon_0$ by a fixed factor,
Lemma~\ref{key} and the preceding full-cylinder
estimates give uniform oscillation decay at these
centers. Campanato's characterization therefore yields
H\"older continuity of $(n,\nabla c,u)$ in $Q_{1/2}$.

In the boundary case, cover $\overline{Q_{1/2}^+}$
by the closures of finitely many smaller boundary
half-cylinders and interior full cylinders, with
their larger cylinders contained in $Q_1^+$.
Fix this covering and decrease $\varepsilon_0$
so that, after rescaling, Lemma~\ref{key} applies
on every larger cylinder.
The local boundary and interior results proved above
then yield H\"older continuity of $(n,\nabla c,u)$
on $\overline{Q_{1/2}^+}$.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{main3}
	For any triple of exponents $(q,r,\tilde{q})$ satisfying
	\begin{equation*}
		\frac2q+\frac3r<2,\quad 2<q<\infty,\quad \frac32<r<\infty,
	\end{equation*}
		and
		\begin{equation}\label{qq}
			\frac{1}{\tilde q}<F(q,r):=
\begin{cases}
1-\dfrac{1}{q}\dfrac{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{6}},
& 1\le r<2,\\[1.2ex]
1,
& r\ge 2.
\end{cases},
\end{equation}
		 Then
			there exists
			\[
			\varepsilon_1=\varepsilon_1(q,r,\tilde{q},M_c,M_\phi)>0
			\]
			such that if $(n,c,u,p)$ is a suitable weak solution to \eqref{che}--\eqref{bound}
in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:suitable-half-space} satisfying
	\begin{equation}
			C^{q,r}_{u,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}}(1)+\|n\|_{L^1_{t,x}(\mathcal{Q})}+D_{\tilde{q},1}(1)<\varepsilon_1,
	\end{equation}
		then $(n,\nabla c,u)$ is regular in $\overline{\mathcal Q_{1/2}}$.
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
We first prove the boundary case, Define
				\[
				L(q,r):=\max\left\{\frac78,1-\frac1q,\frac12+\frac1q\right\}.
				\]
				Under the hypotheses $2/q+3/r<2$ and $r>3/2$, one checks that
				$L(q,r)<F(q,r)$.  If the pressure exponent in the statement does not
				already satisfy $L(q,r)<1/\tilde q$, choose
				$\tilde q_0\in(1,\tilde q)$ such that
				\[
				L(q,r)<\frac1{\tilde q_0}<F(q,r).
				\]
				Because the time interval has finite length,
				$L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1\hookrightarrow L_t^{\tilde q_0}L_x^1$ and the
				corresponding pressure smallness is preserved up to a fixed constant.
				We relabel $\tilde q_0$ as $\tilde q$ below.  This justifies all later
				uses of $L(q,r)<1/\tilde q<F(q,r)$ without strengthening the theorem's
				hypothesis.
				Let $\rho_k=1-2^{-k}$, $B_k^+=B^+(\rho_k)$, and $Q_k^+=Q^+(\rho_k)$ for any integer $k\geq 1$. 

                For each $k\geq0$, choose a smooth domain
$\tilde B_k\subset\mathbb H^3$ such that, for some universal
$\vartheta\in(0,1/8)$,
\[
B^+_{\rho_{k+1}+\vartheta 2^{-k}}
\subset
\tilde B_k
\subset
B^+_{\rho_{k+2}},
\]
and such that the flat part of $\partial\tilde B_k$ coincides
with a portion of $\Gamma$.  We set
\[
\tilde Q_k
:=
(-\rho_{k+2}^2,0)\times\tilde B_k.
\]
For each $k\ge1$, choose
\[
\psi_k\in C_c^\infty
\bigl(B_{\rho_{k+1}}\times(-\rho_{k+1}^2,1)\bigr),
\qquad 0\le\psi_k\le1,
\]
such that $\psi_k$ is even in $x_3$ and
\[
\psi_k\equiv1
\quad\text{on }
\overline{B_{\rho_k}}\times[-\rho_k^2,0].
\]
We use its restriction to the closed half-space, still denoted
by $\psi_k$. It satisfies
\[
|\partial_t\psi_k|\le C2^{2k},
\qquad
|\nabla\psi_k|\le C2^k,
\qquad
|\nabla^2\psi_k|\le C2^{2k},
\qquad
\partial_3\psi_k=0\quad\text{on }\Gamma.
\]
Thus $\psi_k$ vanishes near the initial time
$t=-\rho_{k+1}^2$ and near the spherical lateral boundary,
but may be nonzero on the flat boundary and at $t=0$.

Set
\[
J_k:=
\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}
n\bigl(1+|u|+|\nabla c|\bigr)\,dx\,ds.
\]
For almost every $t\in(-\rho_k^2,0)$, testing the cell
equation with $\psi_k$ up to time $t$ gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\int_{B_k^+}n(x,t)\,dx
&\leq \int_{B^+}n(x,t)\psi_k(x,t)\,dx\\
&=
\int_{-\rho_{k+1}^2}^{t}\int_{B^+}
\left[
n(\partial_s\psi_k+\Delta\psi_k)
+n(u+\nabla c)\cdot\nabla\psi_k
\right]\,dx\,ds\leq C2^{2k}J_k.
\end{aligned}
\]

For $0<\delta<1$, weighted H\"older's inequality yields
\[
\begin{aligned}
\int_{B_k^+}|n\log n|(x,t)\,dx
&\leq
\int_{B^+}n\log n\,\psi_k(x,t)\,dx\\
&\quad+
C_\delta
\left(\int_{B^+}n\psi_k(x,t)\,dx\right)^{1-\delta}
\left(\int_{B^+}\psi_k(x,t)\,dx\right)^\delta\\
&\leq
\int_{B^+}n\log n\,\psi_k(x,t)\,dx
+C_\delta 2^{2k(1-\delta)}J_k^{1-\delta}.
\end{aligned}
\]

We combine these estimates with the local energy inequality,
estimating each term on its right-hand side in absolute value.
We then take essential suprema for the terminal energy terms
and let admissible terminal times increase to zero for the
nonnegative dissipation terms. Using the bounds for $\psi_k$
and discarding the remaining nonnegative terms gives

			\begin{align*}
				&\operatorname{ess\,sup}_{-\rho_k^2\leq t\leq 0}\int_{B_k^+}\left(n+|u|^2+|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\right)(\cdot,t)\,dx\\&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\int_{Q_k^+}\left(|\nabla\sqrt{n}|^2+|\nabla^2\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2+|\nabla u|^2\right)dxdt\\
				&\leq C2^{2k}\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}\left(n^{1+\delta}+n^{1-\delta}+|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2+n+|u|^2\right)dxdt\\
				&\quad +C\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty_{t,x}(Q_{k+1}^+)}\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|n||u|\,dxdt +C2^k\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}\left(n^{1+\delta}+n^{1-\delta}\right)\left(|u|+|\nabla c|\right)\,dxdt\\
				&\quad +C2^k\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}\left(|n||\nabla c|+|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2|u|+|u|^3+|p||u|+|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^3\right)dxdt\\
				&\quad +C2^{2k}\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}(|n|+|n||u|+|n||\nabla c|)\,dxdt\\
&\quad +C2^{2k}\left(\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}(|n|+|n||u|+|n||\nabla c|)\,dxdt\right)^{1-\delta}\\
				&:=\text{RHS}.
			\end{align*}
			Since $\rho_k\in[\frac{1}{2},1]$, we define $A_k=A_{u,\sqrt{n},\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}}(\rho_k)$ and $E_k=E_{u,\sqrt{n},\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}}(\rho_k)$. Thus, the above inequality can be rewritten as
			\begin{equation}\label{eq:section5-rhs}
				A_k+E_k\leq \text{RHS}.
			\end{equation}
			Without loss of generality, we replace $p-[p]_1(t)$ with $p$. By hypothesis, there exists $\tau^*>0$ such that $\frac{2}{q}+\frac{3}{r}=2-\tau^*$. 

        Consequently, we choose $\hat r\in(3/2,r)$ and $\tau_1>0$ sufficiently small so that $\frac2q+\frac3{\hat r}=2-\tau_1$, and
\[
0<\tau_1<
\min\left\{
\frac13,\,
\frac1{\hat r},\,
\frac1{\hat r'}-\frac13
\right\}.
\]
Then
\[
\frac1q+\frac2{\hat r}>1,
\qquad
\frac1q+\frac1{\hat r}>\frac23,
\]
and the interpolation exponents introduced below lie in the required
ranges.
			
			We first estimate the integral term involving $|u|^3$:
			\begin{align}
				\int_{Q^+_{k+1}}|u|^3\,dxdt&\leq \left(\|u\|_{L_t^{q_u}L_x^{r_u}(Q^+_{k+1})}^{\theta_u}\|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^{\hat{r}}(Q^+_{k+1})}^{1-\theta_u}\right)^3\nonumber\\
				&\leq C(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^\frac{3\theta_u}{2}\|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^{\hat{r}}(Q^+_{k+1})}^{3(1-\theta_u)}\nonumber\\&\leq C(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^\frac{3\theta_u}{2}\varepsilon_1^{3(1-\theta_u)},\label{eq:u-L3-estimate}
			\end{align}
			where the parameters satisfy
			\begin{equation*}
				\frac{\theta_u}{q_u}+\frac{1-\theta_u}{q}=\frac{\theta_u}{r_u}+\frac{1-\theta_u}{\hat{r}}=\frac13,\quad \theta_u\in[0,1].
			\end{equation*}
			To ensure the existence of exponents $q_u\in[2,\infty]$ and $r_u\in[2,6]$ satisfying $\frac{2}{q_u}+\frac{3}{r_u}=\frac{3}{2}$ and $\frac{3\theta_u}{2}<1$, we verify this via the relation
			\begin{equation*}
				(1-\theta_u)\left(\frac2q+\frac{3}{\hat{r}}\right)+\theta_u\left(\frac{2}{q_u}+\frac{3}{r_u}\right)=(2-\tau_1)(1-\theta_u)+\frac{3}{2}\theta_u=\frac{5}{3} \implies \theta_u=\frac{\frac13-\tau_1}{\frac12-\tau_1}<\frac23,
			\end{equation*}
			which yields
			\begin{equation*}
				q_u=\frac{\theta_u}{\frac13-\frac{1-\theta_u}{q}};\quad r_u=\frac{\theta_u}{\frac13-\frac{1-\theta_u}{\hat{r}}}.
			\end{equation*}
			Choosing $\hat{r}\in(\frac{3}{2},r)$ such that $\frac1q+\frac{2}{\hat{r}}>1$ and $\frac1q+\frac{1}{\hat{r}}>\frac23$ guarantees that $q_u\in[2,\infty]$ and $r_u\in[2,6]$.
			
			Similarly, the integral term involving $|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^3$ can be bounded as
			\begin{align}\label{eq:grad-c-L3-estimate}
				\int_{Q^+_{k+1}}|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^3\,dxdt\leq C(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^\frac{3\theta_u}{2}\varepsilon_1^{3(1-\theta_u)}.
			\end{align}
			Applying H\"{o}lder's inequality, we also have
			\begin{align*}
				\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|u|^2\,dxdt&\leq C\left(\int_{Q^+_{k+1}}|u|^3\,dxdt\right)^\frac23\leq C\left((A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^\frac{3\theta_u}{2}\varepsilon_1^{3(1-\theta_u)}\right)^\frac23,\\
				\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2\,dxdt&\leq C\left(\int_{Q^+_{k+1}}|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^3\,dxdt\right)^\frac23\leq C\left((A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^\frac{3\theta_u}{2}\varepsilon_1^{3(1-\theta_u)}\right)^\frac23,\\
				\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^2|u|\,dxdt&\leq \left(\int_{Q^+_{k+1}}|u|^3\,dxdt\right)^\frac13\left(\int_{Q^+_{k+1}}|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^3\,dxdt\right)^\frac23\\
				&\leq C(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^\frac{3\theta_u}{2}\varepsilon_1^{3(1-\theta_u)}.\stepcounter{equation}\tag{\arabic{section}.\arabic{equation}}\label{cu23}
			\end{align*}     
			For the terms involving $n$, we obtain
			\begin{align*}
				&\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|n|\,dxdt\leq \|n\|_{L^1_{t,x}(Q^+)}\leq \varepsilon_1,\\
				&\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|n|^{1+\delta}\,dxdt\leq C\left(\|n\|_{L^1_{t,x}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{\frac{2-3\delta}{2+2\delta}}\|n\|_{L_{t,x}^{5/3}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{\frac{5\delta}{2+2\delta}}\right)^{1+\delta}\leq C(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^\frac{5\delta}{2}\varepsilon_1^{1-\frac{3\delta}{2}},\\
				&\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|n|^{1-\delta}\,dxdt\leq C\left(\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|n|\,dxdt\right)^{1-\delta}\leq C\varepsilon_1^{1-\delta}. \stepcounter{equation}\tag{\arabic{section}.\arabic{equation}}\label{n1+-}
			\end{align*}
		
For the coupling term $|n||u|$, we first apply H\"{o}lder's inequality followed by the interpolation inequality on $Q_{k+1}^+$ to obtain:
\begin{align}
	\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|n||u|\,dxdt &\leq \|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^{\hat{r}}(Q_{k+1}^+)}\|n\|_{L_t^{q'}L_x^{\hat{r}'}(Q_{k+1}^+)} \nonumber \\
	&\leq \|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^{\hat{r}}(Q_{k+1}^+)}\|n\|_{L_t^{1}L_x^{1}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{\theta_n}\|n\|_{L_t^{q_n}L_x^{r_n}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{1-\theta_n}, \label{nu}
\end{align}
where the conjugate exponents and the interpolation parameters satisfy:
\begin{align*}
	\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{q'}=1,\quad \frac{1}{\hat{r}}+\frac{1}{\hat{r}'}=1,\quad \frac{2}{q_n}+\frac{3}{r_n}=3,
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
	\frac{1-\theta_n}{q_n}+\theta_n=\frac{1}{q'},\quad \frac{1-\theta_n}{r_n}+\theta_n=\frac{1}{\hat{r}'}.
\end{align*}
Recall from the definition of $\hat{r}$ that $\frac{2}{q} + \frac{3}{\hat{r}} = 2 - \tau_1$. By using the relations of conjugate exponents, this is equivalent to $\frac{2}{q'} + \frac{3}{\hat{r}'} = 3 + \tau_1$. On the other hand, taking a linear combination of the interpolation relations yields:
\begin{align*}
	\frac{2}{q'}+\frac{3}{\hat{r}'} = 2\left(\frac{1-\theta_n}{q_n}+\theta_n\right) + 3\left(\frac{1-\theta_n}{r_n}+\theta_n\right) = 3(1-\theta_n)+5\theta_n.
\end{align*}
Comparing these two expressions leads directly to:
\begin{align*}
	\theta_n=\frac{\tau_1}{2},\quad q_n=\frac{1-\theta_n}{\frac{1}{q'}-\theta_n},\quad \text{and}\quad r_n=\frac{1-\theta_n}{\frac{1}{\hat{r}'}-\theta_n}.
\end{align*}
Since $q\in(2,\infty)$ and $r\in(\frac{3}{2},\infty)$, we have $q'\in(1,2)$ and $\hat{r}'\in(r',3)\subset(1,3)$. To ensure $q_n\in(1,\infty)$ and $r_n\in(1,3)$, we restrict the positive parameter $\tau_1$ to the range:
\begin{equation*}
	0 < \tau_1 < \min\left\{ \frac{2}{q'}, \, \frac{2}{\hat{r}'}, \, \frac{3}{\hat{r}'} - 1 \right\}.
\end{equation*}
Consequently, utilizing the bounds $\|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^{\hat{r}}} \leq C\varepsilon_1$, $\|n\|_{L_t^1L_x^1} \leq \varepsilon_1$, and applying Lemma \ref{interpolation} to bound the $L_t^{q_n}L_x^{r_n}$ norm of $n$ by $A_{k+1}+E_{k+1}$, we arrive at the desired estimate:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:interpolation-n} 
	\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|n||u|\,dxdt \leq C\varepsilon_1^{1+\frac{\tau_1}{2}}(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^{1-\frac{\tau_1}{2}}.
\end{equation}
					
					By a similar argument, we have
					\begin{equation*}
						\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|n||\nabla c|\,dxdt\leq CM_c\varepsilon_1^{1+\frac{\tau_1}{2}}(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^{1-\frac{\tau_1}{2}}.
					\end{equation*}
					
                    For the term involving $n^{1+\delta}$, introduce new exponents
\[
\theta_{n,+}:=\frac{\tau_1-3\delta}{2(1+\delta)},
\]
\[
\frac1{q_{n,+}}
=\frac{\frac1{q'(1+\delta)}-\theta_{n,+}}
{1-\theta_{n,+}},\qquad
\frac1{r_{n,+}}
=\frac{\frac1{\hat r'(1+\delta)}-\theta_{n,+}}
{1-\theta_{n,+}}.
\]
Then $2/q_{n,+}+3/r_{n,+}=3$.
For sufficiently small $\delta>0$, these exponents remain in the
admissible range, and interpolation holds separately in time and space:

\begin{align*}
\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|n|^{1+\delta}|u|\,dxdt&\leq \|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^{\hat{r}}(Q_{k+1}^+)}\|n\|_{L_t^{q'(1+\delta)}L_x^{\hat{r}'(1+\delta)}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{1+\delta}\\
&\leq \|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^{\hat{r}}(Q_{k+1}^+)}\left(\|n\|_{L_t^1L_x^1(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{\theta_{n,+}}
\|n\|_{L_t^{q_{n,+}}L_x^{r_{n,+}}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{1-\theta_{n,+}}\right)^{1+\delta}\\
&=\|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^{\hat{r}}(Q_{k+1}^+)}\|n\|_{L_t^{1}L_x^{1}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{\frac{\tau_1-3\delta}{2}}\|n\|_{L_t^{q_{n,+}}L_x^{r_{n,+}}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{1+\frac{5\delta}{2}-\frac{\tau_1}{2}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_1^{1+\frac{\tau_1-3\delta}{2}}(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^{1+\frac{5\delta}{2}-\frac{\tau_1}{2}},\stepcounter{equation}\tag{\arabic{section}.\arabic{equation}}\label{nu1+}
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}	
\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|n|^{1-\delta}|u|\,dxdt&\leq \|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^{\hat{r}}(Q_{k+1}^+)}\|n\|_{L_t^{q'(1-\delta)}L_x^{\hat{r}'(1-\delta)}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{1-\delta}\\
&\leq C\|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^{\hat{r}}(Q_{k+1}^+)}\|n\|_{L_t^{q'}L_x^{\hat{r}'}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{1-\delta}\\
&\leq C\|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^{\hat{r}}(Q_{k+1}^+)}\left(\|n\|_{L_t^{1}L_x^{1}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{\theta_n}\|n\|_{L_t^{q_n}L_x^{r_n}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{1-\theta_n}\right)^{1-\delta}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_1^{1+\frac{\tau_1}{2}(1-\delta)}(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^{(1-\frac{\tau_1}{2})(1-\delta)}.\stepcounter{equation}\tag{\arabic{section}.\arabic{equation}}\label{nu1-}
\end{align*}
Analogously, we obtain
\begin{align*}
&\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|n|^{1+\delta}|\nabla c|\,dxdt\leq CM_c^{\frac12}\varepsilon_1^{1+\frac{\tau_1-3\delta}{2}}(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^{1+\frac{5\delta}{2}-\frac{\tau_1}{2}},\\
&\int_{Q_{k+1}^+}|n|^{1-\delta}|\nabla c|\,dxdt\leq CM_c^{\frac12}\varepsilon_1^{1+\frac{\tau_1}{2}(1-\delta)}(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^{(1-\frac{\tau_1}{2})(1-\delta)}. \stepcounter{equation}\tag{\arabic{section}.\arabic{equation}}\label{nc1+-}
\end{align*}
Finally, we address the pressure term $|p||u|$. For a suitable pair of exponents $(q', d)$ where $d$ reflects the spatial regularity of the Stokes flow, there exists a unique solution pair $(v_k, p_k) \in W^{1,2}_{q', d}(\tilde{Q}_k) \times W^{0,1}_{q', d}(\tilde{Q}_k)$ to the following initial-boundary value problem:
\begin{equation}\label{p_k}
\begin{cases}	
\partial_t v_k - \Delta v_k + \nabla p_k = -u \cdot \nabla u - n \nabla \phi \quad & \text{in } \tilde{Q}_k, \\
									\nabla \cdot v_k = 0 & \text{in } \tilde{Q}_k, \\
									[p_k]_{\tilde{B}_k}(t) = 0 & \text{for a.e. } t \in [-\rho_{k+2}^2, 0], \\
									v_k = 0 & \text{on } \partial_p \tilde{Q}_k,
								\end{cases}
							\end{equation}
							where $\tilde{B}_k \in \mathbb{R}^3$ is a fixed domain with smooth boundary satisfying $B_{k+1}^{+} \subset \tilde{B}_k \subset B_{k+2}^+$, the $C^2$ norm of $\partial\tilde{B}_k$ is bounded by $C2^k$, and $\tilde{Q}_{k} = (-\rho_{k+2}^2, 0) \times \tilde{B}_k$. 
							
							Setting $w_k = u - v_k$ and $h_k = p - p_k$, we observe that $w_k$ and $h_k$ satisfy:
							\begin{equation}
								\begin{cases}
									\partial_t w_k - \Delta w_k + \nabla h_k = 0 \quad & \text{in } \tilde{Q}_k, \\
									\nabla \cdot w_k = 0 & \text{in } \tilde{Q}_k, \\
									w_k = 0 & \text{on } [-\rho_{k+2}^2, 0) \times (\partial\tilde{B}_k \cap \{x_3 = 0\}).
								\end{cases}
							\end{equation}
							
							Before estimating the pressure term $\int_{Q_{k+1}^+} |u||p_k| \, dxdt$, we first establish the local integrability of the non-linear convective term $u \cdot \nabla u$. By H\"{o}lder's inequality, the $L_t^{q'} L_x^d$-norm of $u \cdot \nabla u$ on the larger domain $Q_{k+2}^+$ is bounded by:
							\begin{equation}
								\|u \cdot \nabla u\|_{L_t^{q'} L_x^d(Q_{k+2}^+)} \le \|\nabla u\|_{L_{t,x}^2(Q_{k+2}^+)} \|u\|_{L_t^{a_1} L_x^{b_1}(Q_{k+2}^+)},
								\label{eq:nonlinear-bound}
							\end{equation}
							where $d$ is chosen such that $\hat{r}' = \frac{3d}{3-d}$. Since $\hat r\in(3/2,\min\{r,3\})$, we have $\hat r'\in(\max\{r',3/2\},3)$. Hence $d=3\hat r'/(3+\hat r')\in(1,3/2)$ and $d<\hat r'$. In addition, the exponents satisfy:
							\begin{equation}\label{frac1}
								\frac{1}{q'} = \frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{a_1}, \quad \frac{1}{d} = \frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{b_1}.
							\end{equation}
							
							Direct calculation yields:
							\begin{equation*}
								\frac{3}{\hat{r}'} = \frac{3-d}{d} = \frac{3}{d} - 1 \implies \frac{2}{q'} + \frac{3}{d} = \frac{2}{q'} + \frac{3}{\hat{r}'} + 1 = 4 + \tau_1.
							\end{equation*}
							From \eqref{frac1}, since $1 < q' < 2$ and $1 < d < \frac{3}{2}$, we have:
							\begin{equation*}
								\frac{2}{q'} + \frac{3}{d} = \frac{2}{a_1} + \frac{3}{b_1} + \frac{5}{2} = 4 + \tau_1 \implies \frac{2}{a_1} + \frac{3}{b_1} = \frac{3}{2} + \tau_1,
							\end{equation*}
							with $a_1 \in (2, \infty)$ and $b_1 \in (2, 6)$.  The interpolation must hold separately in time and space.  Choose
							\begin{equation}\label{eq:theta-p-corrected}
								\theta_p=\frac{\tau_1}{\frac12-\tau_1},\qquad 0<\tau_1<\frac14,
							\end{equation}
							and define $\hat a_1,\hat b_1$ by
							\begin{equation}\label{eq:perturbation}
								\frac1{\hat a_1}=
								\frac{\frac1{a_1}-\frac{\theta_p}{q}}{1-\theta_p},
								\qquad
								\frac1{\hat b_1}=
								\frac{\frac1{b_1}-\frac{\theta_p}{\hat r}}{1-\theta_p}.
							\end{equation}
							Then
							\[
							\frac1{a_1}=\frac{1-\theta_p}{\hat a_1}+\frac{\theta_p}{q},
							\qquad
							\frac1{b_1}=\frac{1-\theta_p}{\hat b_1}+\frac{\theta_p}{\hat r},
							\]
							and, using $2/q+3/\hat r=2-\tau_1$,
							\[
							\frac2{\hat a_1}+\frac3{\hat b_1}
							=\frac{\frac32+\tau_1-\theta_p(2-\tau_1)}{1-\theta_p}
							=\frac32.
							\]
							After decreasing $\tau_1$ if necessary, $\hat a_1\in(2,\infty)$ and $\hat b_1\in(2,6)$.  Hence the mixed-norm interpolation and Lemma~\ref{interpolation} give
							\begin{equation}\label{eq:u-corrected-interpolation}
								\|u\|_{L_t^{a_1}L_x^{b_1}(Q_{k+2}^+)}
								\leq C\|u\|_{L_t^{\hat a_1}L_x^{\hat b_1}}^{1-\theta_p}
								\|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^{\hat r}}^{\theta_p}
								\leq C\varepsilon_1^{\theta_p}
								(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\frac{1-\theta_p}{2}}.
							\end{equation}
							Therefore,
							\begin{equation}\label{pk1}
								\|u \cdot \nabla u\|_{L_t^{q'} L_x^{d}(Q_{k+2}^+)} \le C\varepsilon_1^{\theta_p} (A_{k+2} + E_{k+2})^{1 - \frac{\theta_p}{2}}.
							\end{equation}
							
							Similarly to \eqref{eq:interpolation-n} and applying H\"{o}lder's inequality, we obtain:
							\begin{equation}\label{nk2}
								\|n\|_{L_t^{q'} L_x^{d}(Q_{k+2}^+)} \le C \|n\|_{L_t^{q'} L_x^{\hat{r}'}(Q_{k+2}^+)} \le C \varepsilon_1^{\frac{\tau_1}{2}} (A_{k+2} + E_{k+2})^{1 - \frac{\tau_1}{2}}.
							\end{equation}
							
							Next, we consider the linear Stokes system \eqref{p_k}. In view of the maximal regularity estimate in Lemma \ref{stokes}, and noting the domain inclusion $Q_{k+1}^+ \subset \tilde{Q}_k \subset Q_{k+2}^+$, the pressure term $p_k$ satisfies:
							\begin{align*}
								\|p_k\|_{L_t^{q'} W_{d,x}^{0,1}(Q_{k+1}^+)} &\le \|p_k\|_{W_{q', d}^{0,1}(\tilde{Q}_k)} \\
								&\le C 2^{bk} \left( \|u \cdot \nabla u\|_{L_t^{q'} L_x^d(\tilde{Q}_k)} + \|n \nabla \phi\|_{L_t^{q'} L_x^d(\tilde{Q}_k)} \right).\stepcounter{equation}\tag{\arabic{section}.\arabic{equation}}\label{L3.1}
							\end{align*}
							Since $\tilde{Q}_k \subset Q_{k+2}^+$ and $\nabla \phi \in L^\infty_{t,x}(Q^+)$, the above inequality can be further estimated as:
							\begin{equation}
								\|p_k\|_{L_t^{q'} W_{d,x}^{0,1}(Q_{k+1}^+)} \le C 2^{bk} \left( \|u \cdot \nabla u\|_{L_t^{q'} L_x^d(Q_{k+2}^+)} + \|\nabla \phi\|_{L_{t,x}^\infty(Q^+)} \|n\|_{L_t^{q'} L_x^d(Q_{k+2}^+)} \right).
								\label{eq:pressure-estimate-intermediate}
							\end{equation}
							
							With these preparations, by applying H\"{o}lder's inequality and the Sobolev embedding theorem (where the spatial exponents satisfy $\hat{r}' = \frac{3d}{3-d}$ with $1 < d < \frac{3}{2}$), we obtain the estimate for the target pressure product term on $Q_{k+1}^+$:
							\begin{align*}
								\int_{Q^+_{k+1}} |u||p_k| \, \mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t &\le \|u\|_{L_t^q L_x^{\hat{r}}(Q_{k+1}^+)} \|p_k\|_{L_t^{q'} L_x^{\hat{r}'}(Q_{k+1}^+)} \\
								&\le \|u\|_{L_t^q L_x^{\hat{r}}(Q_{k+1}^+)} \|p_k\|_{ W_{q',d}^{0,1}(Q_{k+1}^+)} \\
								&\le C \|u\|_{L_t^q L_x^{\hat{r}}(Q_{k+1}^+)} 2^{bk} \left[ \|u \cdot \nabla u\|_{L_t^{q'} L_x^d(Q_{k+2}^+)} +  \|n\|_{L_t^{q'} L_x^d(Q_{k+2}^+)} \right].
							\end{align*}
							Thus, combining \eqref{pk1}--\eqref{eq:pressure-estimate-intermediate}, we conclude that:
							\begin{align}\label{pkk}
								\int_{Q^+_{k+1}} |u||p_k| \, \mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t
								&\le C2^{bk}\left[\varepsilon_1^{1+\theta_p}(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{1-\frac{\theta_p}{2}}+\varepsilon_1^{1+\frac{\tau_1}{2}}(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{1-\frac{\tau_1}{2}}\right].
						\end{align}        
We now estimate the convective coupling term $\int_{Q^+_{k+1}}|u||h_k|\,dxdt$. Applying H\"{o}lder's inequality, we obtain
\begin{align}\label{eq:u-hk-holder}
	\int_{Q^+_{k+1}}|u||h_k|\,dxdt \leq \|u\|_{L_t^{q_2}L_x^{r_2}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{1-\theta_1}\|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^r(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{\theta_1}\|h_k\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{s}}(Q_{k+1}^+)},
\end{align}
where the exponents satisfy the scaling and interpolation relations
\begin{align}\label{eq:exponent-relations}
	\frac{2}{q_2}+\frac{3}{r_2}=\frac{3}{2},\quad \frac{1}{\tilde{q}}+\frac{1-\theta_1}{q_2}+\frac{\theta_1}{q}=1,\quad \frac{1}{\tilde{s}}+\frac{1-\theta_1}{r_2}+\frac{\theta_1}{r}=1.
\end{align}
Since the parameters $\tilde{q}$, $q$, and $r$ are given, selecting $q_2$ uniquely determines the exponents $r_2$ and $\theta_1$, which subsequently fixes the value of $\tilde{s}$. To apply the interpolation inequalities from Lemma \ref{interpolation}, we require $2\leq q_2\leq \infty$, which is equivalent to
\begin{align*}
	\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}+\frac{\theta_1}{q}\leq 1 \leq \frac{1}{\tilde{q}}+\frac{\theta_1}{q}+\frac{1-\theta_1}{2}.
\end{align*}
	By the preliminary reduction of the pressure exponent, $F(q,r)>\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}>1-\frac{1}{q}$. We choose $\theta_1=q(1-\frac{1}{\tilde{q}})$, so $\theta_1\in (0,1)$.

To apply    Lemma \ref{Seregin} to the pair $(w_k, h_k)$ on the domain $\tilde{Q}_k$, we must verify the regularities $w_k \in W^{0,1}_{\tilde{q}, 1}(\tilde{Q}_k)$ and $h_k \in L_t^{\tilde{q}} L_x^1(\tilde{Q}_k)$:
\begin{enumerate}
	\item \textbf{Energy bounds of $u$:}
	The velocity field satisfies the standard energy bounds:
	\[
	u \in L^\infty_t L^2_x(Q^+) \quad \text{and}\; \nabla u \in L^2_{t,x}(Q^+).
	\]
	According to \eqref{qq}, we may assume $\tilde{q} \in (1, 2)$.  H\"older's inequality on the bounded cylinder implies that $u \in W^{0,1}_{\tilde{q}, 1}(\tilde{Q}_k)$.
	
	\item \textbf{Regularity of the auxiliary velocity $v_k$:}
	The auxiliary velocity field $v_k$ is obtained from Lemma \ref{stokes} with $v_k \in W^{1,2}_{q', d}(\tilde{Q}_k)$. Utilizing the assumption $\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}>1-\frac{1}{q}$ combined with H\"older's inequality yields $v_k \in W^{0,1}_{\tilde{q}, 1}(\tilde{Q}_k)$. Combining these estimates, we obtain $w_k = u - v_k \in W^{0,1}_{\tilde{q}, 1}(\tilde{Q}_k)$.
	
	\item \textbf{Pressure bounds of $p$:}
By the pressure smallness assumption, after the normalization
$p\mapsto p-[p]_1(t)$ made above, we have
\[
\|p\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1(Q^+)}\le\varepsilon_1.
\]
Since $\tilde Q_k\subset Q^+$, it follows that
\[
\|p\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1(\tilde Q_k)}
\le
\|p\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1(Q^+)}
\le\varepsilon_1.
\]
In particular, $p\in L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1(\tilde Q_k)$.
	
	\item \textbf{Regularity of the auxiliary pressure $p_k$:}
	From \eqref{L3.1}, we have $p_k \in W^{0,1}_{q', d}(\tilde{Q}_k)$, which guarantees that $\nabla p_k \in L^t_{q'} L^x_{d  }(\tilde{Q}_k)$. Since $p_k$ satisfies the zero-mean condition $[p_k]_{\tilde{B}_k}(t) = 0$, the Sobolev--Poincar\'e inequality implies $p_k \in L^t_{q'} L^x_{d}(\tilde{Q}_k)$. Consequently, we have $h_k = p - p_k \in L^t_{\tilde{q}} L^x_1(\tilde{Q}_k)$.
\end{enumerate}
Since $(w_k,h_k)$ solves the homogeneous Stokes system in
$\tilde Q_k$, it also solves it in the standard half-cylinder
\[
Q^+\bigl(\rho_{k+1}+\vartheta2^{-k}\bigr)
\Subset \tilde Q_k.
\]
Applying the rescaled version of Lemma~\ref{Seregin} from
$Q^+(\rho_{k+1}+\vartheta2^{-k})$ to $Q^+_{\rho_{k+1}}$ yields
\begin{align*}
\|h_k\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde s}(Q_{k+1}^+)}
&\leq
C2^{bk}
\left(
\|w_k\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1(\tilde Q_k)}
+
\|h_k\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1(\tilde Q_k)}
\right)\\
&\leq C2^{bk}\left[\||u|+|p|\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{1}(\tilde{Q}_k)}+\||v_k|+|p_k|\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{1}(\tilde{Q}_k)}\right].
\end{align*}
Applying H\"{o}lder's inequality, we obtain
\begin{align*}
	\||u|+|p|\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{1}(\tilde{Q}_k)}\leq \|u\|_{L_t^{q}L_x^{r}(\tilde{Q}_k)}+\|p\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{1}(\tilde{Q}_k)}\leq \varepsilon_1.
\end{align*}
For any $\hat{s}>1$, the maximal regularity of the Stokes system (Lemma \ref{stokes}) yields
\begin{align*}
	\||v_k|+|p_k|\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{1}(\tilde{Q}_k)}&\leq C\||v_k|+|p_k|\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\hat{s}}({\tilde{Q}}_{k})}\\
	&\leq C2^{bk}\left[\|u\cdot\nabla u\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\hat{s}}({\tilde{Q}}_{k})}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty_{t,x}(Q^+)}\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\hat{s}}({\tilde{Q}}_{k})}\right].
\end{align*}

We next verify that the external force and convective terms satisfy the necessary integrability conditions. For any $1<\hat{s}<\tilde{q}<\frac53$, H\"{o}lder's inequality gives
\begin{align*}
	\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\hat{s}}({\tilde{Q}}_{k})}\leq \|n\|_{L_{t,x}^{\tilde{q}}({\tilde{Q}}_{k})}\leq  \|n\|_{L^\frac53_{t,x}({\tilde{Q}}_{k})}^{\theta_2}\|n\|_{L^1_{t,x}({\tilde{Q}}_{k})}^{1-\theta_2},
\end{align*}
where $\theta_2 \in (0,1)$ is determined by the relation
\begin{align*}
	1-\theta_2+\frac{3}{5}\theta_2=\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}\implies \theta_2=\frac{5}{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}\right).
\end{align*}
This yields the bound
\begin{align*}
	\|n\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\hat{s}}({\tilde{Q}}_{k})}\leq C(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\theta_2}\varepsilon_1^{1-\theta_2}.
\end{align*}
We choose $\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}>\frac45$ to guarantee that $\frac12-\frac{\theta_1}{2}+\theta_2<1$.

For the convective term $u\cdot\nabla u$, H\"{o}lder's inequality yields
\begin{align*}
	\|u\cdot\nabla u\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\hat{s}}({\tilde{Q}}_{k})}\leq \|\nabla u\|_{L^2_{t,x}(\tilde{Q}_{k})}\|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^r(\tilde{Q}_{k})}^{1-\theta_3}\|u\|_{L_t^{q_3}L_x^{r_3}(\tilde{Q}_{k})}^{\theta_3},
\end{align*}
where the exponents satisfy $2\leq q_3\leq \infty$, $\frac{2}{q_3}+\frac{3}{r_3}=\frac{3}{2}$, and
\begin{align*}
	\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1-\theta_3}{q}+\frac{\theta_3}{q_3}\leq \frac{1}{\tilde{q}},\quad \frac{1}{2}+\frac{1-\theta_3}{r}+\frac{\theta_3}{r_3}\leq \frac{1}{\hat{s}}. 
\end{align*}
To ensure $\hat{s}>1$, we choose a suitable exponent $q_3$ and its associated $r_3$ such that
\begin{align}\label{theta3}
	\frac{1-\theta_3}{r}+\frac{\theta_3}{r_3}< \frac12.
\end{align}
To optimize the admissible range of $\tilde{q}$, we minimize the combination $1+\theta_3-\theta_1$ by choosing the smallest possible $\theta_3$ and the largest possible $\theta_1$, namely, $\theta_1=q(1-\frac{1}{\tilde{q}})$. We analyze the choice of $\theta_3$ by considering two cases:

\textit{Case i):} If $r>2$, we select $\theta_3=0$, which implies $\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}>\frac12+\frac1q$. Under this condition, we obtain $\hat{s}>1$ and
\begin{align*}
	\|u\cdot\nabla u\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\hat{s}}({\tilde{Q}}_{k})}\leq \|\nabla u\|_{L^2_{t,x}(\tilde{Q}_{k})}\varepsilon_1\leq C(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^\frac12\varepsilon_1.
\end{align*}

\textit{Case ii):} If $r\in(\frac32,2]$, we have $q>4$ since $\frac2q+\frac3r<2$. Assuming $1<\hat{s}\leq \min\{\frac{6}{5},\tilde{q}\}$, we have $(\frac{1}{\hat{s}}-\frac12)^{-1}\in (2,3)$. Choosing $q_3 = 2$ and $r_3 = 6$ to minimize $\theta_3$, we obtain from \eqref{theta3}:
\begin{align*}
	\theta_3>\frac{\frac1r-\frac12}{\frac1r-\frac16}.
\end{align*} 
We set $\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}>\frac78$ to satisfy the inequality above since
\begin{align*}
	\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1-\theta_3}{q}+\frac{\theta_3}{q_3}\leq \frac{1}{2}+\frac{1-\theta_3}{4}+\frac{\theta_3}{2}<\frac78,\quad \text{when}\quad q>4, \,\theta_3<\frac12,
\end{align*} 
and 
\begin{align*}
	0\leq\frac{\frac1r-\frac12}{\frac1r-\frac16}<\frac13, \quad\text{when}\quad r\in\left(\frac32,2\right].
\end{align*} 

Since $1/\tilde q<F(q,r)$, the lower bound above is also
strictly less than $\theta_1=q(1-1/\tilde q)$. We therefore choose
\[
\frac{\frac1r-\frac12}{\frac1r-\frac16}
<\theta_3<\min\left\{\frac12,\theta_1\right\}.
\]
This simultaneous choice ensures both the required mixed-norm
estimate and $1+\theta_3/2-\theta_1/2<1$, as needed for Young's
inequality in the energy iteration.


Under these conditions, the estimate for $h_k$ becomes
\begin{align*}
	\|h_k\|_{L_t^{\tilde{q}}L_x^{\tilde{s}}(Q_{k+1}^+)}\leq C2^{2bk}\Big(\varepsilon_1+&\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty_{t,x}(Q^+)}(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\theta_2}\varepsilon_1^{1-\theta_2}\\
	&+(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\frac12+\frac12\theta_3}{\varepsilon_1}^{1-\theta_3}\Big).
\end{align*}
 Combining the exponents yields
\begin{align}\label{hkk}
	\int_{Q^+_{k+1}}|u||h_k|\,dxdt &\leq C2^{2bk}\Big(\varepsilon_1+(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\theta_2}\varepsilon_1^{1-\theta_2}\nonumber\\
	&\quad +(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\frac12+\frac12\theta_3}{\varepsilon_1}^{1-\theta_3}\Big)\|u\|_{L_t^{q_2}L_x^{r_2}(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{1-\theta_1}\|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^r(Q_{k+1}^+)}^{\theta_1}\nonumber\\
	&\leq C2^{2bk}\Big(\varepsilon_1^{1+\theta_1}(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\frac{1-\theta_1}{2}}+(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\frac12-\frac12\theta_1+\theta_2}\varepsilon_1^{1+\theta_1-\theta_2}\nonumber\\
	&\quad +(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{1+\frac12\theta_3-\frac12\theta_1}{\varepsilon_1}^{1-\theta_3+\theta_1}\Big).
\end{align}
Finally, substituting \eqref{eq:u-L3-estimate}--\eqref{nc1+-}, \eqref{pkk} and \eqref{hkk} back into \eqref{eq:section5-rhs}, and assuming $\max\left\{\frac78, 1-\frac{1}{q}, \frac12+\frac1q\right\} < \frac{1}{\tilde{q}} < F(q,r)$, $\min\{\frac{2}{q'}, \frac{1}{\hat{r}'},\frac{1}{\hat{r}'} -\frac13 \}>\tau_1>5\delta$, we obtain
\begin{align*}
	A_k + E_k &\le \Big\{C(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^\frac{3\theta_u}{2}\varepsilon_1^{3(1-\theta_u)}+C(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^{\theta_u}\varepsilon_1^{2(1-\theta_u)}\\&\quad+\varepsilon_1+C(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^\frac{5\delta}{2}\varepsilon_1^{1-\frac{3\delta}{2}}+C\varepsilon_1^{1-\delta}\\&\quad+C\varepsilon_1^{1+\frac{\tau_1}{2}}(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^{1-\frac{\tau_1}{2}}+C\varepsilon_1^{1+\frac{\tau_1-3\delta}{2}}(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^{1+\frac{5\delta}{2}-\frac{\tau_1}{2}}\\&\quad+C\varepsilon_1^{(1+\frac{\tau_1}{2})(1-\delta)}(A_{k+1}+E_{k+1})^{(1-\frac{\tau_1}{2})(1-\delta)}\\&\quad+C2^{bk}\left[\varepsilon_1^{1+\theta_p}(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{1-\frac{\theta_p}{2}}+\varepsilon_1^{1+\frac{\tau_1}{2}}(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{1-\frac{\tau_1}{2}}\right]\\&\quad+C2^{2bk}\Big[\varepsilon_1^{1+\theta_1}(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\frac{1-\theta_1}{2}}+(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\frac12-\frac12\theta_1+\theta_2}\varepsilon_1^{1+\theta_1-\theta_2}\\
	&\qquad +(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{1+\frac12\theta_3-\frac12\theta_1}{\varepsilon_1}^{1-\theta_3+\theta_1}\Big]\Big\}\times 2^{2k}.
\end{align*}
By Young's inequality, there exist
$B=B(q,r,\tilde q)>0$ and
$\beta=\beta(q,r,\tilde q)\in(0,1)$ such that,
for every $\delta_1>0$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:section5-recursion}
A_k+E_k\leq\delta_1^2(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})+\tilde C\,2^{Bk}\varepsilon_1^\beta.
\end{equation}
where
$\tilde C=
\tilde C(\delta_1,q,r,\tilde q,M_c,M_\phi)>0$
is independent of $k$ and $\varepsilon_1$.


Choosing $\delta_1 = 3^{-B}$ ensures that the second term on the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:section5-recursion} is summable. Multiplying both sides of \eqref{eq:section5-recursion} by $\delta_1^k$ and summing over $k$ from $1$ to infinity, we obtain
\begin{align*}
	\sum_{k=1}^\infty \delta_1^k(A_k + E_k) &\le \sum_{k=1}^\infty \delta_1^{k+2}(A_{k+2} + E_{k+2}) + \tilde{C} \varepsilon_1^\beta \sum_{k=1}^\infty \left(\frac23\right)^{Bk} \\
	&\le \sum_{k=3}^\infty \delta_1^k(A_k + E_k) + \tilde{C} \varepsilon_1^\beta.
\end{align*}

Since $\rho_k\geq1/2$, domain inclusion gives
\[
\sup_{k\geq1}(A_k+E_k)
\leq
2\bigl(A_{u,\sqrt n,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}(1)
      +E_{u,\sqrt n,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}(1)\bigr)
<\infty.
\]
Thus all weighted series below converge, and cancellation
of their common tails is legitimate.
Since all terms are non-negative, this implies
\[
A_1 + E_1 \le \tilde{C} \varepsilon_1^\beta.
\]

Together with $D_{\tilde q,1}(1)<\varepsilon_1$ and the fixed-ratio
pressure bound $D_{\tilde q,1}(1/2)\leq C D_{\tilde q,1}(1)$,
this permits application of Proposition~\ref{main1} at radius $1/2$.
For sufficiently small $\varepsilon_1$, it first gives regularity in
$\overline{Q_{1/4}^+}$. After proving the analogous interior assertion
below, we extend this conclusion to $\overline{Q_{1/2}^+}$ by a
uniform localization argument.


We record the corresponding estimate of the pressure term when the
center is an interior point. After translation and parabolic rescaling,
we may work in a full cylinder $Q_1$.
Let
\[
B_k=B_{\rho_k},\qquad
Q_k=(-\rho_k^2,0)\times B_k,
\qquad
\rho_k=1-2^{-k}.
\]
Choose a smooth domain $\tilde B_k$ such that
\[
B_{k+1}\Subset\tilde B_k\Subset B_{k+2},
\qquad
\operatorname{dist}
(B_{k+1},\partial\tilde B_k)
\geq c2^{-k},
\]
and set
\[
\tilde Q_k
=
(-\rho_{k+2}^2,0)\times\tilde B_k.
\]

As in the boundary argument, we may replace $p$ by
$p-[p]_{B_1}(t)$ and still denote it by $p$. Indeed, since
$\nabla\cdot u=0$ and the cutoff functions are compactly supported
in the interior,
\[
\int c(t)u\cdot\nabla\psi_k\,dx=0
\]
for every function $c=c(t)$. Hence
\[
\|p\|_{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1(Q_1)}
\leq\varepsilon_1.
\]

Consider the auxiliary Stokes problem
\[
\left\{
\begin{aligned}
\partial_t v_k-\Delta v_k+\nabla p_k
&=-u\cdot\nabla u-n\nabla\phi
&&\text{in }\tilde Q_k,\\
\nabla\cdot v_k&=0
&&\text{in }\tilde Q_k,\\
[p_k]_{\tilde B_k}(t)&=0
&&\text{for a.e. }t,\\
v_k&=0
&&\text{on }\partial_p\tilde Q_k.
\end{aligned}
\right.
\]
The zero boundary condition above is imposed only on the auxiliary
Stokes problem and does not impose any additional boundary condition
on the original velocity $u$.

Set
\[
w_k=u-v_k,\qquad h_k=p-p_k.
\]
Then
\[
\partial_t w_k-\Delta w_k+\nabla h_k=0,
\qquad
\nabla\cdot w_k=0
\qquad\text{in }\tilde Q_k.
\]
Taking the divergence of this equation gives
\[
\Delta h_k(\cdot,t)=0
\qquad\text{in }\tilde B_k
\]
for almost every $t\in(-\rho_{k+2}^2,0)$.

We first estimate the contribution of $p_k$. The estimates
\eqref{eq:nonlinear-bound}--\eqref{eq:pressure-estimate-intermediate} are unchanged in the full-cylinder setting. In
particular, with $d$ chosen so that
\[
\hat r'=\frac{3d}{3-d},
\]
the maximal regularity of the Stokes system and the Sobolev embedding
$W^{1,d}\hookrightarrow L^{\hat r'}$ give
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|p_k\|_
{L_t^{q'}L_x^{\hat r'}(Q_{k+1})}
\leq C2^{bk}\Big[
\varepsilon_1^{\theta_p}
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{1-\frac{\theta_p}{2}}
+
\varepsilon_1^{\frac{\tau_1}{2}}
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{1-\frac{\tau_1}{2}}
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]
Consequently,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\int_{Q_{k+1}}|u||p_k|\,dxdt
&\leq
\|u\|_{L_t^qL_x^{\hat r}(Q_{k+1})}
\|p_k\|_{L_t^{q'}L_x^{\hat r'}(Q_{k+1})}
\\
&\leq
C2^{bk}\Big[
\varepsilon_1^{1+\theta_p}
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{1-\frac{\theta_p}{2}}
+
\varepsilon_1^{1+\frac{\tau_1}{2}}
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{1-\frac{\tau_1}{2}}
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]
Thus the estimate corresponding to~\eqref{pkk} is the same as in the
boundary case.

The estimate of $h_k$ is simpler. Since $h_k(\cdot,t)$ is harmonic
in $\tilde B_k$ and
$B_{k+1}\Subset\tilde B_k$, the standard interior estimate for
harmonic functions yields, for every fixed
$1<\tilde s<\infty$,
\[
\|h_k(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\tilde s}(B_{k+1})}
\leq
C2^{bk}
\|h_k(\cdot,t)\|_{L^1(\tilde B_k)}.
\]
Taking the $L^{\tilde q}$ norm in time gives
\[
\|h_k\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde s}(Q_{k+1})}
\leq
C2^{bk}
\|h_k\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1(\tilde Q_k)}.
\]
Since $h_k=p-p_k$,
\[
\|h_k\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1(\tilde Q_k)}
\leq
\varepsilon_1
+
\|p_k\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1(\tilde Q_k)}.
\]

To estimate the last term, we use the same lower-integrability
Stokes estimate as in the boundary proof. For a suitable
$\hat s>1$,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|p_k\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^1(\tilde Q_k)}
\leq
C2^{bk}\Big(
\|u\cdot\nabla u\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\hat s}(\tilde Q_k)}+
\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^\infty(Q_1)}
\|n\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\hat s}(\tilde Q_k)}
\Big).
\end{aligned}
\]
The interpolation estimates used above give
\[
\|n\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\hat s}(\tilde Q_k)}
\leq
C
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\theta_2}
\varepsilon_1^{1-\theta_2},
\]
and
\[
\|u\cdot\nabla u\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\hat s}(\tilde Q_k)}
\leq
C
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\frac{1+\theta_3}{2}}
\varepsilon_1^{1-\theta_3}.
\]
Therefore, after enlarging $b$ if necessary,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|h_k\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde s}(Q_{k+1})}
\leq
C2^{2bk}\Big[
\varepsilon_1
+
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\theta_2}
\varepsilon_1^{1-\theta_2}
+
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\frac{1+\theta_3}{2}}
\varepsilon_1^{1-\theta_3}
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]

We now use the same exponents
$q_2,r_2,\theta_1,\tilde s$ as in~\eqref{eq:u-hk-holder}--\eqref{eq:exponent-relations}. Thus
\[
\frac2{q_2}+\frac3{r_2}=\frac32,
\]
and
\[
\frac1{\tilde q}
+\frac{1-\theta_1}{q_2}
+\frac{\theta_1}{q}
=1,
\qquad
\frac1{\tilde s}
+\frac{1-\theta_1}{r_2}
+\frac{\theta_1}{r}
=1.
\]
H\"older's inequality, Lemma~\ref{interpolation}, and the smallness assumption give
\[
\begin{aligned}
\int_{Q_{k+1}}|u||h_k|\,dxdt
\leq{}&
\|u\|_
{L_t^{q_2}L_x^{r_2}(Q_{k+1})}^{1-\theta_1}
\|u\|_
{L_t^qL_x^r(Q_{k+1})}^{\theta_1}
\|h_k\|_
{L_t^{\tilde q}L_x^{\tilde s}(Q_{k+1})}
\\
\leq{}&
C2^{2bk}\Big[
\varepsilon_1^{1+\theta_1}
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\frac{1-\theta_1}{2}}
\\
&+
\varepsilon_1^{1+\theta_1-\theta_2}
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{
\frac12-\frac{\theta_1}{2}+\theta_2}
\\
&+
\varepsilon_1^{1-\theta_3+\theta_1}
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{
1+\frac{\theta_3}{2}-\frac{\theta_1}{2}}
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]
This is precisely the full-cylinder counterpart of~\eqref{hkk}.

Combining the estimates for $p_k$ and $h_k$, we conclude that
\[
\begin{aligned}
\int_{Q_{k+1}}|u||p|\,dxdt
\leq
C2^{2bk}\Big[
&
\varepsilon_1^{1+\theta_p}
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{1-\frac{\theta_p}{2}}
\\
&+
\varepsilon_1^{1+\frac{\tau_1}{2}}
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{1-\frac{\tau_1}{2}}
\\
&+
\varepsilon_1^{1+\theta_1}
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{\frac{1-\theta_1}{2}}
\\
&+
\varepsilon_1^{1+\theta_1-\theta_2}
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{
\frac12-\frac{\theta_1}{2}+\theta_2}
\\
&+
\varepsilon_1^{1-\theta_3+\theta_1}
(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})^{
1+\frac{\theta_3}{2}-\frac{\theta_1}{2}}
\Big].
\end{aligned}
\]
Under the same restrictions on
$\theta_p,\theta_1,\theta_2,\theta_3$ as in the boundary argument,
all powers of $A_{k+2}+E_{k+2}$ on the right-hand side are strictly
less than one. Hence Young's inequality gives, for every
$\delta_1>0$,
\[
2^k\int_{Q_{k+1}}|u||p|\,dxdt
\leq
\delta_1^2(A_{k+2}+E_{k+2})
+
C_{\delta_1}2^{Bk}\varepsilon_1^\beta,
\]
for some $B>0$ and $\beta=\beta(q,r,\tilde{q})\in(0,1)$.

Thus the pressure contribution in the interior satisfies exactly the
same type of estimate needed to derive~\eqref{eq:section5-recursion}. The only essential
difference from the boundary argument is that the estimate of $h_k$
uses the interior harmonic regularity of
$\Delta h_k=0$, rather than the boundary Stokes estimate of
Lemma~\ref{Seregin}; in particular, no separate regularity estimate for $w_k$
is required.

All the remaining terms in the localized energy inequality are
estimated exactly as in the boundary case, with $Q_k^+$ and $B_k^+$
replaced by $Q_k$ and $B_k$, respectively. In fact, the interior
cutoff functions are compactly supported in the full cylinder, so
no flat-boundary terms occur. Combining these estimates with the
interior pressure estimate above yields the same recursion
\eqref{eq:section5-recursion}. The same summation argument therefore
gives
\[A_1+E_1\leq C\varepsilon_1^\beta.\]

Choosing $\varepsilon_1$ sufficiently small and applying the interior
counterpart of Proposition~\ref{main1} at radius $1/2$ gives regularity in
$\overline{Q_{1/4}}$.

We now recover the claimed region in both cases. Denote the small
threshold for these quarter-cylinder conclusions by $\eta$. If a
boundary or interior cylinder of radius $R$ is contained in the
reference cylinder, restriction, change of pressure mean, and rescaling
bound its normalized lower-order hypothesis by
\[
C\left(R^{1-2/q-3/r}+R^{-3}+R^{-1-2/\tilde q}\right)\varepsilon_1.
\]
For the pressure, use
$\int_{\mathcal B_R}|p-[p]_{\mathcal B_R}|dx
\leq2\int_{\mathcal B_R}|p-[p]_{\mathcal B_1}|dx$.
For a boundary reference cylinder, take $R_b=1/8$. At each
$z=(x,t)\in\overline{Q_{1/2}^+}$ with $x_3\leq1/64$, the cylinder
$Q_{R_b}^+(x',0,t)$ lies in $Q_1^+$ and its quarter-cylinder contains
$z$. If $x_3>1/64$, the full cylinder $Q_{R_i}(z)$ with $R_i=1/128$
is contained in $Q_1^+$. For an interior reference cylinder, the same
fixed radius $R_i$ works for every $z\in\overline{Q_{1/2}}$.
Choose $\varepsilon_1$ smaller than $\eta$ divided by the maximum
of the two fixed rescaling constants. The quarter-cylinder result
then applies at every center just described. Translating the terminal
times slightly forward when $t<0$, and using the terminal closure
when $t=0$, gives neighborhoods of every point in the claimed
closed half-cylinder. A finite subcover yields regularity throughout
$\overline{\mathcal Q_{1/2}}$.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Translate the normalized center to the origin. All constants
below may depend on $q,\tilde q,M_c,M_\phi$.
Write $\varepsilon$ for the left-hand side of
\eqref{eq:main2-smallness} and initially assume
$\varepsilon\leq1$. We use $C_M$ for constants that also depend
on the prescribed bound $M$, but not on the solution's entropy
or energy norms. They may be chosen nondecreasing in $M$ by
using $1+M$ in their upper bounds.

\medskip\noindent
\textit{Step 1: a mass-dependent local energy bound.}
Set
\[
\rho_k=\frac78-2^{-k-3},\qquad
X_k=A_{u,\sqrt n,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}(\rho_k)
    +E_{u,\sqrt n,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}(\rho_k),
\qquad k\geq0.
\]
In particular, $\rho_0=3/4$ and $\rho_k\uparrow7/8$.
Repeat the cutoff and pressure estimates in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{main3} on these nested cylinders, with its spatial
exponent equal to $q$. The pressure-exponent reduction made
there remains valid, since $F(q,q)=1$.
In those estimates, retain the bound $M$ for each density
$L^1$ factor and the bound $\varepsilon$ for the velocity,
signal-gradient, and pressure factors.

Here is the resulting control of the terms for which the
density smallness was previously used. With the interpolation
parameter $\tau_1$ of that proof and sufficiently small
$0<\alpha<\min\{2/5,\tau_1/5\}$, the estimates on each
$\mathcal Q_{\rho_{k+1}}$ give
\begin{align*}
\int n&\leq M,\qquad
\int n^{1-\alpha}\leq C M^{1-\alpha},\\
\int n^{1+\alpha}
&\leq C M^{1-3\alpha/2}X_{k+1}^{5\alpha/2},\\
\int n|u|
&\leq C\varepsilon M^{\tau_1/2}
X_{k+1}^{1-\tau_1/2},\\
\int n^{1+\alpha}|u|
&\leq C\varepsilon M^{(\tau_1-3\alpha)/2}
X_{k+1}^{1+5\alpha/2-\tau_1/2},\\
\int n^{1-\alpha}|u|
&\leq C\varepsilon M^{\tau_1(1-\alpha)/2}
X_{k+1}^{(1-\tau_1/2)(1-\alpha)}.
\end{align*}
All integrals in this display are space--time integrals.
The same estimates hold with $|u|$ replaced by $|\nabla c|$,
because
$|\nabla c|\leq2\sqrt{M_c}|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}|$.
The fractional power of the mass-flux integral in
\eqref{eq:section5-rhs} is controlled by the same bounds and
$(a+b)^{1-\alpha}\leq a^{1-\alpha}+b^{1-\alpha}$.

For completeness, the density contributions to the two pressure
products in~\eqref{pkk} and~\eqref{hkk} are now bounded by
\[
C2^{bk}\varepsilon M^{\tau_1/2}
X_{k+2}^{1-\tau_1/2},
\qquad
C2^{2bk}\varepsilon^{\theta_1}M^{1-\theta_2}
X_{k+2}^{(1-\theta_1)/2+\theta_2},
\]
respectively. The energy exponents remain strictly less than
one, by the choices made in that proof. All the remaining
terms are independent of density mass smallness and have the
same sublinear energy exponents as before. Since the radii
stay in $[3/4,7/8]$, inclusion of cylinders bounds $X_{k+1}$
by a fixed multiple of $X_{k+2}$. Consequently, for some
$\theta\in(0,1)$ and $b>0$,
\[
X_k\leq C_M2^{bk}(1+X_{k+2}^{\theta}).
\]
Young's inequality therefore gives, for every $a>0$,
\begin{equation}
X_k\leq aX_{k+2}+C_{M,a}2^{Bk}
\label{eq:bounded-mass-energy-recursion}
\end{equation}
with $B>0$. Choose $a2^{2B}<1$. Suitability and cylinder
inclusion imply $\sup_kX_k<\infty$; this finiteness is used
only to discard the tail in the iteration
\[
X_0\leq a^jX_{2j}
+C_{M,a}\sum_{\ell=0}^{j-1}(a2^{2B})^\ell.
\]
Letting $j\to\infty$ and using the energy interpolation
inequality yields
\begin{equation}
A_{u,\sqrt n,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}(3/4)
+E_{u,\sqrt n,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}(3/4)
+\|n\|_{L^{5/3}(\mathcal Q_{3/4})}
\leq C_M.
\label{eq:bounded-mass-local-energy}
\end{equation}
This is a boundedness estimate, not a small-energy estimate.

\medskip\noindent
\textit{Step 2: a caloric decomposition of the density.}
The density equation is
\[
\partial_t n-\Delta n=-\nabla\cdot F,
\qquad F=n(u+\nabla c).
\]
Since $q'=q/(q-1)<5/3$, H\"older's inequality and
\eqref{eq:bounded-mass-local-energy} give
\begin{equation}
\|F\|_{L^1(\mathcal Q_{3/4})}
\leq\|n\|_{L^{q'}(\mathcal Q_{3/4})}
\|u+\nabla c\|_{L^q(\mathcal Q_{3/4})}
\leq C_M\varepsilon.
\label{eq:bounded-mass-small-flux}
\end{equation}
In the boundary case, extend $n$ evenly across $x_3=0$,
extend $F_1,F_2$ evenly and $F_3$ oddly, and denote the
extensions by $N,F^{\mathrm e}$ on $Q_{3/4}$.
The weak Neumann identity gives
\[
\partial_tN-\Delta N=-\nabla\cdot F^{\mathrm e}
\quad\text{in }\mathcal D'(Q_{3/4}).
\]
Indeed, the sum of a test function and its reflection has
zero normal derivative on the plane and is an admissible
test in that identity. Thus no classical normal trace is
being assumed. In the interior case put $N=n$ and
$F^{\mathrm e}=F$.

Let $a_0=-9/16$, and extend $F^{\mathrm e}$ by zero from
$Q_{3/4}$ to $\mathbb R^3\times(a_0,0)$. Denote the result
by $\widehat F$. With the heat kernel
\[
G(x,t)=(4\pi t)^{-3/2}\exp(-|x|^2/(4t)),\qquad t>0,
\]
define
\[
w(x,t)=-\int_{a_0}^t\int_{\mathbb R^3}
\nabla_xG(x-y,t-s)\cdot\widehat F(y,s)\,dy\,ds.
\]
Then $(\partial_t-\Delta)w=-\nabla\cdot\widehat F$ in
distributions. Since
$\|\nabla G(\cdot,t)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}\leq Ct^{-1/2}$,
Tonelli's theorem gives
\begin{align}
\int_{a_0}^0\|w(\cdot,t)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}\,dt
&\leq C\int_{a_0}^0\int_{a_0}^t
(t-s)^{-1/2}\|\widehat F(\cdot,s)\|_{L^1}\,ds\,dt
\nonumber\\
&\leq C\|\widehat F\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3\times(a_0,0))}
\leq C_M\varepsilon.
\label{eq:bounded-mass-heat-potential}
\end{align}
The function $v=N-w$ is caloric on $Q_{3/4}$. The local
$L^1$-to-$L^\infty$ estimate for caloric functions therefore
implies
\begin{equation}
\|v\|_{L^\infty(Q_{5/8})}
\leq C\|v\|_{L^1(Q_{3/4})}
\leq C M+C_M\varepsilon.
\label{eq:bounded-mass-caloric-part}
\end{equation}
Only the flux has been extended by zero at the spherical
boundary; no artificial boundary condition is imposed on $n$.

\medskip\noindent
\textit{Step 3: small rescaled density mass.}
For every full or boundary half-cylinder
$\mathcal Q_\rho(z)\subset\mathcal Q_{5/8}$, with
$0<\rho\leq1$, the preceding decomposition and $n\geq0$
give
\begin{equation}
\rho^{-3}\int_{\mathcal Q_\rho(z)}n\,dx\,dt
\leq C M\rho^2+C_M\varepsilon\rho^{-3}.
\label{eq:bounded-mass-density-decay}
\end{equation}
Here the $C_M\varepsilon\rho^2$ contribution from
\eqref{eq:bounded-mass-caloric-part} has been absorbed into
the last term. This estimate is uniform in the center.

Let $\eta>0$ be the threshold in Theorem~\ref{main3} for
the equal exponents $(q,q)$ and pressure exponent $\tilde q$.
It is admissible because $5/q<2$ and $F(q,q)=1$.
For scales at most one we use the original upper bound
$M_\phi$ for the rescaled potential gradient.
Choose $0<r\leq1/64$, depending only on $M$ and the fixed
parameters, so that
\[
C M r^2\leq\eta/6.
\]
The constants can be enlarged independently of $r$ so that,
at either radius $\rho=r$ or $\rho=r/8$, the error term in
\eqref{eq:bounded-mass-density-decay} is bounded by
$C_M\varepsilon r^{-3}$.
For the same cylinders, restriction, rescaling, and change of
pressure mean give
\begin{align*}
C^{q,q}_{u,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}(\rho;z)
&\leq C r^{1-5/q}\varepsilon,\\
D_{\tilde q,1}(\rho;z)
&\leq C r^{-1-2/\tilde q}\varepsilon.
\end{align*}
In the second inequality we used, for almost every time,
\[
\int_{\mathcal B_\rho(z)}|p-[p]_{\mathcal B_\rho(z)}|\,dx
\leq2\int_{\mathcal B_\rho(z)}|p-[p]_1|\,dx.
\]
After fixing $r$, choose
\begin{equation}
0<\varepsilon_1\leq
\min\left\{1,\frac{\eta r^3}{6C_M},
\frac{\eta r^{5/q-1}}{3C},
\frac{\eta r^{1+2/\tilde q}}{3C}\right\}.
\label{eq:bounded-mass-threshold-choice}
\end{equation}
For $\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_1$, every such cylinder of
either selected radius satisfies
\[
C^{q,q}_{u,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}(\rho;z)
+\rho^{-3}\int_{\mathcal Q_\rho(z)}n\,dx\,dt
+D_{\tilde q,1}(\rho;z)\leq\eta.
\]
Thus Theorem~\ref{main3} applies after rescaling.
In particular, it is the small density mass on this smaller
cylinder, rather than the original bound $M$, that enters
the small-energy iteration leading to Proposition~\ref{main1}.

\medskip\noindent
\textit{Step 4: coverage of the asserted region.}
In the boundary case, take any
$z=(x,t)\in\overline{Q_{1/2}^+}$. If $x_3\leq r/4$,
use the boundary cylinder $Q_r^+(x',0,t)$; its half-cylinder
contains $z$ in its spatial interior relative to the closed
half-space. If $x_3>r/4$, use the full cylinder $Q_{r/8}(z)$,
which is contained in the half-space. These cylinders lie
in $Q_{5/8}^+$. In the interior reference case, use
$Q_{r/8}(z)\subset Q_{5/8}$ for every
$z\in\overline{Q_{1/2}}$.
The preceding estimates are uniform for all these centers.
As in the proof of Theorem~\ref{main3}, move terminal times
slightly forward when $t<0$ and use the terminal closure at
$t=0$. The resulting neighborhoods cover the asserted closed
half-cylinder or full cylinder, and a finite subcover gives
the claimed regularity.
Finally, choose the intermediate radius nonincreasing in $M$
and replace $r^{5/q-1}$ in~\eqref{eq:bounded-mass-threshold-choice}
by $r^{\max\{5/q-1,0\}}$ if necessary. Together with the
nondecreasing choice of $C_M$, this makes $\varepsilon_1$
nonincreasing in $M$.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- corollary-4: body_md -->
\label{6.1}
Let $(n,c,u,p)$ be a normalized local restriction of a suitable weak
solution in the sense described above. There exists
\[
\varepsilon_4=\varepsilon_4(M_c,M_\phi)>0
\]
such that, if
\begin{align}\label{coro2}
\|u\|_{L_{t,x}^{10/3}(\mathcal Q)}
+\|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}\|_{L_{t,x}^{10/3}(\mathcal Q)}
+\|n\|_{L_{t,x}^{5/3}(\mathcal Q)}
+\|p-[p]_1(t)\|_{L_t^{5/3}L_x^1(\mathcal Q)}
\leq\varepsilon_4,
\end{align}
then $(n,\nabla c,u)$ is regular in
$\overline{\mathcal Q_{1/2}}$.
<!-- end corollary-4 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Apply Theorem~\ref{main3} with $q=r=10/3$ and $\tilde q=5/3$.
On the unit cylinder,
$\|n\|_{L^1}\leq |\mathcal Q|^{2/5}\|n\|_{L^{5/3}}$.
Choosing $\varepsilon_4$ sufficiently small in terms of the
threshold in that theorem proves the assertion. Its only
nonuniversal dependencies are $M_c$ and $M_\phi$.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- corollary-5: body_md -->
\label{coro6.2}
	Let $\rho_1 \in (0, 1)$. There exists a constant $\varepsilon_5 = \varepsilon_5(\varepsilon_4) > 0$ determined by the threshold $\varepsilon_4$ in Corollary \ref{6.1} such that if
	\begin{align}\label{6.2}
		C^{\frac{10}{3}, \frac{10}{3}}_{\sqrt{n}, u, \nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}}(\rho_1) + P(\rho_1) \leq \varepsilon_5,
	\end{align}
	then the scaled solution $(n_{\rho_1}, \nabla c_{\rho_1}, u_{\rho_1})$ is regular in $\overline{\mathcal Q_{1/2}}$.
<!-- end corollary-5 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
By scale invariance,
	\begin{align*}
		\|u_{\rho_1}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}_{t,x}(\mathcal{Q})}
		&=C^{\frac{10}{3},\frac{10}{3}}_u(\rho_1),\\
		\|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c_{\rho_1}}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}_{t,x}(\mathcal{Q})}
		&=C^{\frac{10}{3},\frac{10}{3}}_{\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}(\rho_1),\\
		\|n_{\rho_1}\|_{L^{\frac53}_{t,x}(\mathcal{Q})}
		&=\left(C^{\frac{10}{3},\frac{10}{3}}_{\sqrt n}(\rho_1)\right)^2.
	\end{align*}
	Moreover, H\"older's inequality in space gives
	$D_{\frac53,1}(\rho_1)\leq C P(\rho_1)$.  Therefore the left-hand side of \eqref{coro2} for the scaled solution is bounded by $C(\varepsilon_5+\varepsilon_5^2)$.  Choosing
	\[
	\varepsilon_5\leq\min\left\{1,\frac{\varepsilon_4}{3C}\right\}
	\]
	allows us to apply Corollary~\ref{6.1}, proving the claim.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
Fix
\[
z_0=(x_0,t_0)
\in
\overline{\mathbb H^3}\times(0,\infty).
\]
Choose $r_0>0$ sufficiently small so that
\[
4r_0^2<t_0.
\]
If $x_0\in\mathbb H^3$, we additionally require
\[
2r_0<\operatorname{dist}(x_0,\Gamma).
\]
Hence the cylinder of scale $2r_0$ is either a boundary half-cylinder
or an interior full cylinder.

After translating $z_0$ to the origin and parabolically rescaling by
$r_0$, the reference unit cylinder is $Q_1^+$ in the boundary case
and $Q_1$ in the interior case. In either case we denote it by
$\mathcal Q$ and keep the same notation for the rescaled solution.

We first initialize the stronger pressure quantity in \eqref{6.2}.
Since
\[
p\in L^{5/3}(\mathcal{Q}),
\]
Jensen's inequality for the
spatial average and the triangle inequality give
\begin{align}\label{eq:pressure-gain-initialization}
P(\rho)
&=\rho^{-1}
\|p-[p]_\rho(t)\|_{L^{5/3}(\mathcal{Q}_\rho)}
\leq 2\rho^{-1}\|p\|_{L^{5/3}(\mathcal{Q}_\rho)}
<\infty
\end{align}
for every fixed admissible radius $0<\rho<1$. Therefore the pressure iteration below is well defined at every fixed admissible starting scale ($0<\rho<1$).
In particular, no additional pressure integrability assumption is required.
\par\textbf{Estimate of $C^{\frac{10}{3},\frac{10}{3}}_{\sqrt{n},u,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}}(r)$:}
For any $0 < 4r < \rho<\rho_0 < 1$, we begin by decomposing the velocity term as follows:
\begin{align*}
	\left(C^{\frac{10}{3},\frac{10}{3}}_{u}(r)\right)^\frac{10}{3}=r^{-\frac{5}{3}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_r}|u|^\frac{10}{3}dxdt\leq C r^{-\frac{5}{3}}\left(\int_{\mathcal{Q}_r}|u-u_\rho|^\frac{10}{3}dxdt + \int_{\mathcal{Q}_r}|u_\rho|^\frac{10}{3}dxdt\right).
\end{align*}
For almost every $t$, denote the spatial mean by
\[
u_\rho(t)
:=
\fint_{\mathcal B_\rho}u(x,t)\,dx.
\] we obtain:
\begin{align*}
	C r^{-\frac{5}{3}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_r}|u_\rho|^\frac{10}{3}dxdt &\leq Cr^\frac{4}{3}\int_{-\rho^2}^{0}\rho^{-10}\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}_\rho}|u|dx\right)^\frac{10}{3}dt\\
	&\leq Cr^\frac{4}{3}\rho^{-3}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_\rho}|u|^\frac{10}{3}dxdt = C\left(\frac{r}{\rho}\right)^\frac{4}{3}\left(C^{\frac{10}{3},\frac{10}{3}}_{u}(\rho)\right)^\frac{10}{3}.
\end{align*}
Applying the Sobolev embedding theorem along with H\"{o}lder's inequality in space yields:
\begin{align*}
	\|u-u_\rho\|_{L^\frac{10}{3}(\mathcal{B}_\rho)}\leq \|u-u_\rho\|^\frac{2}{5}_{L^2(\mathcal{B}_\rho)}\|u-u_\rho\|^\frac{3}{5}_{L^6(\mathcal{B}_\rho)}\leq C\|u\|^\frac{2}{5}_{L^2(\mathcal{B}_\rho)}\|\nabla u\|^\frac{3}{5}_{L^2(\mathcal{B}_\rho)}.
\end{align*}
Consequently, we have:
\begin{align*}
	Cr^{-\frac{5}{3}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_r}|u-u_\rho|^\frac{10}{3}dxdt\leq Cr^{-\frac{5}{3}}\int_{-\rho^2}^{0}\|u\|^\frac{4}{3}_{L^2(\mathcal{B}_\rho)}\|\nabla u\|^2_{L^2(\mathcal{B}_\rho)}dt\leq C\left(\frac{\rho}{r}\right)^\frac{5}{3}A_u^\frac{2}{3}(\rho)E_u(\rho).
\end{align*}
Since the estimates for $C^{\frac{10}{3},\frac{10}{3}}_{\sqrt{n}}(r)$ and $C^{\frac{10}{3},\frac{10}{3}}_{\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}}(r)$ can be handled in an analogous manner, their details are omitted for brevity. Finally, we define
\begin{align*}
	\mathscr{C}(\rho):=\rho^{-\frac{5}{3}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_\rho}\left(|u|^\frac{10}{3}+|\sqrt{n}|^\frac{10}{3}+|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}|^\frac{10}{3}\right)dxdt,
\end{align*}
which leads to the following bound:
\begin{align}\label{C}
	\mathscr{C}(r)\leq C\left(\frac{r}{\rho}\right)^\frac{4}{3}\mathscr{C}(\rho)+C\left(\frac{\rho}{r}\right)^\frac{5}{3}A_{\sqrt{n},u,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}}^\frac{2}{3}(\rho)E_{\sqrt{n},u,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde{c}}}(\rho).
\end{align}

\par\textbf{Estimate of $\bar P(r)$:}
By Corollary~\ref{cor:pressure-53}, we have:
\begin{align}\label{D}
	\bar P(r)\leq& C\left(\frac{r}{\rho}\right)^\frac{5}{3}\bar P(\rho)+C\left(\frac{r}{\rho}\right)^\frac{5}{3}\left(A_{u,\sqrt{n}}^\frac{2}{3}(\rho)E_{u,\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+A_u(\rho)^{\frac13}E_u(\rho)^{\frac12}\right)\nonumber\\
	&+C\left(\frac{\rho}{r}\right)^\frac{5}{3}A_{u,\sqrt{n}}^\frac{2}{3}(\rho)E_{u,\sqrt{n}}(\rho)+C\left(\frac{\rho}{r}\right)^\frac{5}{3}\rho^{\frac53} A_{\sqrt{n}}^\frac{5}{3}(\rho).
\end{align}


\par\textbf{Estimate of $A(r)+E(r)$:}

Use the abbreviations $A=A_{u,\sqrt n,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}$ and
$E=E_{u,\sqrt n,\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}}$. For $0<r<1/2$, normalize
the cylinder of radius $2r$ and use the preceding heat-kernel proof
with $\gamma=1/2$ and, for example, $\alpha=1/15$.

The normalized norms obey
\[
(2r)^{-1}\|n\|_{L^{5/3}(\mathcal Q_{2r})}
\leq C\mathscr C(2r)^{3/5}
\]
and
\[
(2r)^{-1/2}\left(\|u\|_{L^{10/3}(\mathcal Q_{2r})}
+\|\nabla\sqrt{\tilde c}\|_{L^{10/3}(\mathcal Q_{2r})}\right)
\leq C\mathscr C(2r)^{3/10}.
\]
The weak mass identity bounds $M_\psi$ by
$C\bigl(\mathscr C(2r)^{3/5}+\mathscr C(2r)^{9/10}\bigr)$.
Estimate the pressure contribution directly by
$C\mathscr C(2r)^{3/10}\bar P(2r)^{3/5}$,
using $u\in L^{5/2}$ on the unit cylinder.
Including \eqref{eq:weighted-negative-entropy} therefore gives
\begin{equation}\label{aerr}
\begin{aligned}
A(r)+E(r)\leq C_\alpha\bigl[&\mathscr C(2r)^{3/5}
+\mathscr C(2r)^{3(1+\alpha)/5}
+\mathscr C(2r)^{3(1-\alpha)/5}\\
&+\mathscr C(2r)^{9/10}
+\mathscr C(2r)^{9/10+3\alpha/5}
+\mathscr C(2r)^{9/10-3\alpha/5}\\
&+\mathscr C(2r)^{3/10}\bar P(2r)^{3/5}
+\bigl(\mathscr C(2r)^{3/5}+\mathscr C(2r)^{9/10}\bigr)^{1-\alpha}\bigr]\\
\leq{}&C\bigl(\mathscr C(2r)+\bar P(2r)+1\bigr).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
All pure powers of $\mathscr C(2r)$ displayed here are less than one;
the mixed pressure term is bounded by
$C\bigl(\mathscr C(2r)+\bar P(2r)+1\bigr)$ by Young's inequality.
Thus $C=C(M_c,M_\phi)$ after fixing $\alpha$.




\par\textbf{Iteration argument:} 
We define the combined quantity:
\begin{align}
	G(r):=\bar P(r)+\mathscr{C}(r).
\end{align}

Combining \eqref{C} and \eqref{D} at radius $\rho/2$ is valid for
$0<8r\leq\rho$. Put $r=\theta\rho$, $0<\theta<1/8$.
Since $G(\rho/2)\leq CG(\rho)$, \eqref{aerr} and
\[
A^{2/3}E\leq(A+E)E^{2/3},\qquad
A_u^{1/3}E_u^{1/2}\leq(A+1)E^{1/2}
\]
give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:corrected-G-iteration}
\begin{aligned}
G(\theta\rho)\leq{}&C\left[\theta^{4/3}
+\theta^{-5/3}E(\rho/2)^{2/3}
+\theta^{5/3}E(\rho/2)^{1/2}\right]G(\rho)\\
&+C\left[\theta^{-5/3}E(\rho/2)^{2/3}
+\theta^{5/3}E(\rho/2)^{1/2}
+\theta^{-5/3}\bigl(\rho A_{\sqrt n}(\rho/2)\bigr)^{5/3}\right].
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Here $E$ is the full dissipation, including both scalar terms, at every
occurrence.

Choose $\theta_0\in(0,1/8)$ with $C\theta_0^{4/3}\leq1/8$.
Choose $0<\varepsilon_6\leq1$ sufficiently small that
$C(\varepsilon_6^{3/10}+\varepsilon_6^{3/5})<\varepsilon_5$,
where $\varepsilon_5$ is the threshold in Corollary~\ref{coro6.2}.
Now choose $\varepsilon_3>0$ such that
\[
C\theta_0^{-5/3}(2\varepsilon_3)^{2/3}\leq\varepsilon_6/16,
\qquad
C\theta_0^{5/3}(2\varepsilon_3)^{1/2}\leq\varepsilon_6/16.
\]
The limsup assumption in Theorem~\ref{main4} implies that, for some
$\rho_2>0$,
\[
E(\rho/2)\leq2\varepsilon_3\qquad(0<\rho\leq\rho_2).
\]
The slack factor $2$ makes this a uniform statement at all subsequent
scales, not merely at one selected radius.

The local entropy bound also implies $rA_{\sqrt n}(r)\to0$ as
$r\downarrow0$. Indeed, for a fixed bounded domain $D$ containing
the balls and any $K>1$,
\[
rA_{\sqrt n}(r)
\leq K|\mathcal B_r|
+\frac1{\log K}\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{-1<s<0}
\int_D n(x,s)\log^+n(x,s)\,dx.
\]
The supremum is finite by Definition~\ref{def:suitable-half-space}(iii).
Let $r\downarrow0$ first and then $K\to\infty$. Shrinking $\rho_2$
therefore ensures
\[
C\theta_0^{-5/3}\bigl(\rho A_{\sqrt n}(\rho/2)\bigr)^{5/3}
\leq\varepsilon_6/8\qquad(0<\rho\leq\rho_2).
\]
Equation~\eqref{eq:corrected-G-iteration} now yields, at every such scale,
\[
G(\theta_0\rho)\leq\tfrac12G(\rho)+\tfrac14\varepsilon_6,
\qquad
G(\theta_0^k\rho_2)\leq2^{-k}G(\rho_2)+\tfrac12\varepsilon_6.
\]
The initial value $G(\rho_2)$ is finite. For sufficiently large $k$,
$G(\theta_0^k\rho_2)\leq3\varepsilon_6/4$, which implies
\eqref{6.2}. Corollary~\ref{coro6.2} proves regularity at the center.
The regularity threshold $\varepsilon_3$ depends only on
$M_c$, $M_\phi$, and fixed geometric constants.
The starting radius $\rho_2$ and the number of iterations
needed to reach the regularity threshold may depend on
the solution and on the point under consideration.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
Recall the dissipation density $e$ and the parabolic metric $d_P$
introduced in Section~\ref{subsec:main-regularity-results}. We denote the corresponding
parabolic balls by
\[
P_r(z):=B_r(x)\times(t-r^2,t+r^2),
\qquad z=(x,t).
\]

For each integer $m\ge2$, define
\[
K_m
:=
\left\{
(x,t)\in
\overline{\mathbb H^3}\times(0,\infty):
|x|\le m,\quad
\frac1m\le t\le m
\right\},
\]
and set
\[
\mathcal S_m:=\mathcal S\cap K_m.
\]
Since
\[
\overline{\mathbb H^3}\times(0,\infty)
=
\bigcup_{m=2}^{\infty}K_m,
\]
we have
\[
\mathcal S
=
\bigcup_{m=2}^{\infty}\mathcal S_m.
\]
Thus it suffices to prove
\[
\mathcal P^1(\mathcal S_m)=0
\]
for every fixed $m\ge2$.

Fix $m\ge2$. Choose a bounded open set
\[
U_m\Subset
\mathbb R^3\times(0,\infty)
\]
such that
\[
K_m\subset U_m.
\]
For instance, one may take
\[
U_m
=
\left\{
(x,t):
|x|<m+1,\quad
\frac1{2m}<t<m+1
\right\}.
\]
By the local integrability of the dissipation density,
\[
e\in
L^1\bigl(
U_m\cap(\mathbb H^3\times(0,\infty))
\bigr).
\]

Extend $e$ by zero outside the half-space and set
\[
\widetilde e(x,t)
:=
e(x,t)
\mathbf 1_{\mathbb H^3\times(0,\infty)}(x,t).
\]
Then
\[
\widetilde e\in L^1(U_m).
\]

We first show that $\mathcal S_m$ has zero four-dimensional
Lebesgue measure. By the Lebesgue differentiation theorem for
parabolic balls, for almost every $z\in U_m$,
\[
\fint_{P_r(z)}
\widetilde e\,dx\,dt
\longrightarrow
\widetilde e(z)
\qquad\text{as }r\downarrow0.
\]
Let such a Lebesgue point satisfy
\[
z=(x,t)\in\mathbb H^3\times(0,\infty).
\]
For all sufficiently small admissible radii $r$,
\[
\mathcal Q_r(z)\subset P_r(z),
\]
and hence
\[
\frac1r
\int_{\mathcal Q_r(z)}e\,dx\,dt
\le
\frac1r
\int_{P_r(z)}\widetilde e\,dx\,dt.
\]
Since
\[
|P_r(z)|=C r^5,
\]
we obtain
\[
\frac1r
\int_{\mathcal Q_r(z)}e\,dx\,dt
\le
C r^4
\fint_{P_r(z)}
\widetilde e\,dx\,dt
\longrightarrow0.
\]
Therefore Theorem~\ref{main4} implies that every such interior
Lebesgue point is regular. Hence the interior part of $\mathcal S_m$
has zero four-dimensional Lebesgue measure. The boundary part is
contained in
\[
\Gamma\times(0,\infty),
\]
which itself has zero four-dimensional Lebesgue measure. Consequently,
\[
|\mathcal S_m|=0.
\]

Let $\bar\eta>0$.
Since $|\mathcal S_m|=0$, there exists an open set
containing $\mathcal S_m$ with arbitrarily small
Lebesgue measure; this is the outer regularity of
Lebesgue measure.
Intersecting this set with a fixed open neighborhood
of $K_m$ compactly contained in $U_m$, we obtain
an open set $J$ satisfying
\[
\mathcal S_m\subset J\Subset U_m.
\]
Since $\widetilde e\in L^1(U_m)$, the absolute
continuity of the Lebesgue integral ensures that,
by choosing $|J|$ sufficiently small,
\begin{align}\label{6.9}
\int_J\widetilde e\,dx\,dt<\bar\eta.
\end{align}

Let $z\in\mathcal S_m$. Since $z$ is singular, the contrapositive of
Theorem~\ref{main4} gives
\[
\limsup_{r\downarrow0}
\frac1r
\int_{\mathcal Q_r(z)}
e\,dx\,dt
>
\varepsilon_3,
\]
where the limit is taken along admissible radii. Hence there exist
arbitrarily small admissible radii $r>0$ such that
\begin{align}\label{6.11}
\int_{\mathcal Q_r(z)}
e\,dx\,dt
>
\varepsilon_3 r.
\end{align}

Fix $\delta_2>0$. Since $J$ is open and $z\in J$,
\[
d_P(z,J^c)>0.
\]
Because the admissible radii satisfying \eqref{6.11} can be chosen
arbitrarily small, for every $z=(x_z,t_z)\in\mathcal S_m$ we may
choose one such radius $r_z>0$ satisfying
\[
r_z
<
\min\left\{
\frac{\delta_2}{10},
\frac{d_P(z,J^c)}5,
\frac{\sqrt{t_z}}5
\right\}.
\tag{6.12}
\]
Consider the family of parabolic balls
\[
\mathcal F
:=
\{P_{r_z}(z):z\in\mathcal S_m\}.
\]
By the $5r$-covering lemma, there exists a countable pairwise disjoint
subfamily
\[
\{P_{r_j}(z_j)\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
\subset\mathcal F
\]
such that
\[
\mathcal S_m
\subset
\bigcup_{j=1}^{\infty}
P_{5r_j}(z_j)
\subset J.
\tag{6.13}
\]
Indeed, the last inclusion follows from
\[
5r_j<d_P(z_j,J^c).
\]

Since
\[
\mathcal Q_{r_j}(z_j)
\subset
P_{r_j}(z_j),
\]
and the balls $P_{r_j}(z_j)$ are pairwise disjoint, the family
\[
\{\mathcal Q_{r_j}(z_j)\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
\]
is also pairwise disjoint. Using \eqref{6.9} and \eqref{6.11}, we obtain
\[
\varepsilon_3
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}r_j
<
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}
\int_{\mathcal Q_{r_j}(z_j)}
e\,dx\,dt
\le
\int_{J\cap(\mathbb H^3\times(0,\infty))}
e\,dx\,dt
<
\bar{\eta}.
\tag{6.14}
\]
Consequently,
\[
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}r_j
<
\frac{\bar{\eta}}{\varepsilon_3}.
\]

Since
\[
\operatorname{diam}_P P_{5r_j}(z_j)
=
10r_j,
\]
and $r_j<\delta_2/10$, the family
\[
\{P_{5r_j}(z_j)\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
\]
is an admissible $\delta_2$-cover of $\mathcal S_m$. Hence
\[
\mathcal P^1_{\delta_2}(\mathcal S_m)
\le
10\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}r_j
<
\frac{10\bar{\eta}}{\varepsilon_3}.
\]
Since $\bar{\eta}>0$ is arbitrary,
\[
\mathcal P^1_{\delta_2}(\mathcal S_m)=0.
\]
Letting $\delta_2\downarrow0$, we obtain
\[
\mathcal P^1(\mathcal S_m)=0.
\]

Finally,
\[
\mathcal S
=
\bigcup_{m=2}^{\infty}\mathcal S_m.
\]
Therefore, by countable subadditivity,
\[
\mathcal P^1(\mathcal S)
\le
\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}
\mathcal P^1(\mathcal S_m)
=
0.
\]
Thus
\[
\boxed{\mathcal P^1(\mathcal S)=0.}
\]
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:ev-signal}
The approximate solutions in Section~\ref{sec:existence} satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ev-signal-decay}
\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}(t)\|_{L^\infty}
\leq \min\{\|c_0\|_{L^\infty},\,C L t^{-3/2}\},
\qquad t>0,
\end{equation}
with a numerical constant independent of both parameters.
The same estimate holds, almost everywhere in time, for each
limit selected below. In addition, for $2\leq q<\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ev-signal-energy}
\frac{d}{dt}\|c^{\varepsilon,\tau}\|_{L^q}^q
+\frac{4(q-1)}q\|\nabla(c^{\varepsilon,\tau})^{q/2}\|_{L^2}^2
\leq0.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Fix $\varepsilon,\tau>0$ and a finite time interval $[0,T]$.
For $0\leq s<t\leq T$, let $U(t,s)$ be the solution operator for
the linear Neumann problem with generator
\[
 \Delta-u^{\varepsilon,\tau}(t)\cdot\nabla
       -h_\tau(n^{\varepsilon,\tau}(t)).
\]
The coefficients are fixed throughout this argument. In particular,
$U(t,s)c^{\varepsilon,\tau}(s)=c^{\varepsilon,\tau}(t)$.
We first use smooth boundary-compatible initial data
$f\in L^1\cap L^2$, allowing either sign, and then pass by density.

The transport term vanishes in the scalar energy estimates because
$u^{\varepsilon,\tau}$ is divergence free and has zero boundary trace.
The absorption term is nonnegative. Testing with regularized signs
and with $U(t,s)f$, respectively, gives
\[
 \|U(t,s)f\|_{L^1}\leq\|f\|_{L^1},
 \qquad
 \frac{d}{dt}\|U(t,s)f\|_{L^2}^2
       +2\|\nabla U(t,s)f\|_{L^2}^2\leq0.
\]
These tests use the boundary-compatible cutoffs from
Section~\ref{sec:existence}. The half-space Nash inequality,
obtained by even extension, reads
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ev-nash}
 \|U(t,s)f\|_{L^2}^{10/3}
 \leq C\|U(t,s)f\|_{L^1}^{4/3}
          \|\nabla U(t,s)f\|_{L^2}^2.
\end{equation}
For $f\neq0$, it follows that
\[
 \frac{d}{dt}\|U(t,s)f\|_{L^2}^2
 +\frac{2}{C\|f\|_{L^1}^{4/3}}
       \bigl(\|U(t,s)f\|_{L^2}^2\bigr)^{5/3}\leq0.
\]
Integration gives
\[
 \|U(t,s)f\|_{L^2}\leq C(t-s)^{-3/4}\|f\|_{L^1},
 \qquad
 \|U(t,s)\|_{L^1\to L^2}\leq C(t-s)^{-3/4}.
\]
The case $f=0$ is immediate.

Integration by parts with the Neumann boundary condition gives
\[
\bigl(\Delta-u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla
-h_\tau(n^{\varepsilon,\tau})\bigr)^*
=\Delta+u^{\varepsilon,\tau}\cdot\nabla
